# SpartBoard Nightly Debugger Memory Doc

## ⚠️ MANDATORY Phase 0 step: check open `nightly/*` PRs before dispatching

**Read this before doing anything else, even if you skim the rest of this doc.** Before creating any worktree or dispatching any subagent, list open PRs with head refs matching `nightly/*` against base `dev-paul` (e.g. `list_pull_requests` filtered to open + base `dev-paul`, or equivalent). If any exist from a run you didn't personally just finish, a concurrent/prior nightly run is already in flight or unmerged. This has caused a **same-base-commit collision** — two runs independently fixing the identical bug from the same unmerged `dev-paul` commit — on 2026-08-15, 2026-08-16, and again 2026-08-18 (this collided with a same-day 2026-08-17 run whose PRs hadn't merged yet), despite this exact recommendation being recorded in Notes & Gotchas after the first two occurrences. The 2026-08-18 collision happened specifically because the doc's Notes & Gotchas section was read with a truncated `limit` that stopped short of the existing warning — **read Notes & Gotchas to the literal end of the file** (check for the `Run count` tracker at the bottom to confirm you reached it), not just a fixed offset/limit window. If open nightly PRs exist, either wait for them to merge, or branch from their head instead of `dev-paul` directly, or proceed anyway but budget real time in Phase 2/3 for cross-testing and reconciling likely duplicates (see the 2026-08-18 Notes & Gotchas entry for the empirical cross-testing method that worked).

## Project Commands

| Command                      | Script                                                                             |
| ---------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Install all deps             | `pnpm run install:all`                                                             |
| Dev server (port 3000)       | `pnpm run dev`                                                                     |
| Production build             | `pnpm run build`                                                                   |
| Full validate (gate)         | `pnpm run validate` → type-check:all + lint + format:check + test + functions test |
| Type-check only              | `pnpm run type-check`                                                              |
| Type-check all (+ functions) | `pnpm run type-check:all`                                                          |
| Lint (zero warnings)         | `pnpm run lint`                                                                    |
| Lint fix                     | `pnpm run lint:fix`                                                                |
| Format check                 | `pnpm run format:check`                                                            |
| Format write                 | `pnpm run format`                                                                  |
| Unit tests                   | `pnpm run test`                                                                    |
| Functions tests              | `pnpm -C functions run test`                                                       |
| E2E tests                    | `pnpm run test:e2e`                                                                |
| Build all                    | `pnpm run build:all`                                                               |

**Package manager**: pnpm (not npm). Node 24+ required (v22 prints warnings but works).

## Code Areas

| Area                   | Globs                                                                                      | Description                                                                             |
| ---------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Widgets**            | `components/widgets/**`                                                                    | All 60+ widget implementations, WidgetRegistry, WidgetRenderer, container-query scaling |
| **Dashboard & Layout** | `components/layout/**`, `components/common/**`, `App.tsx`, `index.tsx`, `index.css`        | Dashboard view, Sidebar, Dock, DraggableWindow, shared UI primitives                    |
| **State & Data**       | `context/**`, `hooks/**`, `utils/**`, `types/**`, `types.ts`                               | React contexts (Auth, Dashboard, Dialog, etc.), all custom hooks, utility functions     |
| **Admin & Config**     | `components/admin/**`, `components/auth/**`, `config/**`, `locales/**`, `i18n/**`          | Admin panel, auth UI, feature permissions, widget config, i18n, Firebase config         |
| **Build & Tooling**    | `functions/**`, `tests/**`, `scripts/**`, `*.config.*`, `firestore.rules`, `firebase.json` | Cloud functions, test suites, build pipeline, Firestore security rules                  |

Fixed with `vi.mock` factories for lucide-react, `useAdminBuildings`, and `MaterialsWidget/constants`. Run time: 8,481 ms → 787–1,119 ms.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | [#1778](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1778) |
| 2026-05-31 | Dashboard & Layout | `SettingsPanel` click-outside silently dropped after parent re-renders. `onClose` was listed in the `useEffect` dep array as `[onClose, widgetRef]`; `DraggableWindow` passes an inline arrow creating a new identity on every render (every drag tick, Firestore update, zoom change). Each identity change cancelled the 50 ms mount-guard timer and removed the `pointerdown` listener, then restarted the 50 ms window. Any outside click within that window was lost. Fixed by storing `onClose` in `onCloseRef` (render-body assignment), removing `onClose` from both effect dep arrays.                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | [#1780](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1780) |
| 2026-06-01 | Widgets            | `RandomWidget` (`components/widgets/random/RandomWidget.tsx`): `performUpdate` closed over `activeDashboard` at click time. For flash/slots styles, `performUpdate` fires inside a `setInterval` ~1.7–2.5 s after click. If Firestore delivered a dashboard update in that window, the stale snapshot caused `autoStartTimer` to target the wrong time-tool widget (or find none), and `sharedGroups` to read a stale value. Fixed with `activeDashboardRef` assigned synchronously on every render.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | [#1785](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1785) |
| 2026-06-01 | Dashboard & Layout | `DashboardView.tsx` global `Delete` key handler called `e.preventDefault()` unconditionally before checking active element. Added `isTypingField` early-return guard mirroring the existing Escape branch.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | [#1786](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1786) |
| 2026-06-01 | State & Data       | `publishAssignmentScores` (`hooks/useVideoActivityAssignments.ts`): unanswered-questions loop iterated raw `activityData.questions` array. Drive-sync / `arrayUnion` races can write the same question ID twice; each duplicate unanswered question inflated `pointsMax` and deflated the published score (2-question activity with one duplicate: correct student scored 33% instead of 50%). Changed loop to iterate `questionsById` (Map, already deduped). Same pattern as #1728, #1777.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | [#1787](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1787) |

| 2026-06-01 | Build & Tooling    | `checkUrlCompatibility` (Cloud Function) SSRF guard was IPv4-only. Node's `URL` class returns IPv6 in bracketed form (`[::1]`) which matched none of the existing regexes. An authenticated user could submit `https://[::1]/internal` and probe internal VPC services. Added targeted IPv6 blocklist patterns for loopback, unspecified, IPv4-mapped, ULA (fc/fd), and link-local (fe80::/10) ranges.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | [#1789](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1789) |
| 2026-06-02 | Widgets            | `GuidedLearningPlayer` (`components/widgets/GuidedLearning/components/GuidedLearningPlayer.tsx`): `startTimer` useCallback listed `answeredSteps: Set<string>` in its dep array. Each answer triggered `setAnsweredSteps`, producing a new Set identity, recreating `startTimer`, re-firing the timer effect, and resetting `progressRef.current = 0` from scratch. A student answering at second 8 of a 10-second step would see the countdown reset to 0 instead of advancing in 2 seconds. Fixed with `answeredStepsRef` (no-dep effect sync); `startTimer` reads `answeredStepsRef.current`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | [#1801](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1801) |
| 2026-06-02 | Dashboard & Layout | `DraggableWindow` (`components/common/DraggableWindow.tsx`): `shouldRenderSettings` latched `false→true` via `useEffect`, which fires after paint — causing the "Standard settings available." placeholder to flash for one frame on every first flip. Replaced with React's "adjust state while rendering" inline pattern (`if (widget.flipped && !shouldRenderSettings) setShouldRenderSettings(true)`). Side effect: `react-hooks/refs` v7 now flags pre-existing render-body ref assignments; added targeted `eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/refs` comments at 4 sites + narrow block disable for `dragState.current` reads.                                                                                                                                                                                 | [#1802](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1802) |
| 2026-06-02 | State & Data       | `publishAssignmentScores` (`hooks/useQuizAssignments.ts`): unanswered-questions denominator loop iterated `quizData.questions` (raw array) instead of `questionsById` (deduplicated Map). Duplicate question IDs inflate `pointsMax` — a 2-question quiz with q1 duplicated scores a student who skips q1 as 20% instead of 33%. Same pattern as #1728, #1777, #1787. Changed loop to iterate `questionsById.entries()`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | [#1803](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1803) |

| 2026-06-02 | Build & Tooling    | `adminAnalyticsCompute.ts` (`functions/src/adminAnalyticsCompute.ts`): `'blooms-ai'` missing from `GEMINI_SPECIFIC_FEATURES`. Doc IDs like `uid_blooms-ai_date` were misidentified as "overall" docs with UID `uid_blooms-ai` (no real match), silently dropping every Blooms AI call from `totalCalls`, `byFeature['blooms-ai']`, and `topUsers`. Added `'blooms-ai'` to the array.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | [#1805](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1805) |
| 2026-06-03 | Widgets            | `NumberLine/Widget.tsx`: fractions-mode tick label condition `(val * denom) % 1 === 0` used strict equality, failing for ticks computed as `min + i * step`. With `step=0.1`, JS FP accumulation gives `3 × 0.1 = 0.30000000000000004`, so ticks at 0.3, 0.6, and 0.7 silently fell back to decimal labels — producing a mixed axis despite teacher selecting fractions mode. Fixed with epsilon guard: compute `rawNumerator = val * denom`, accept fraction only when `\|rawNumerator − Math.round(rawNumerator)\| < 1e-9`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | [#1825](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1825) |
| 2026-06-03 | Dashboard & Layout | `AnnotationCanvas.tsx`: window-level `pointerup` fallback listener and React `onPointerUp` handler both fire for the same event when `setPointerCapture` succeeds (normal path). Both closures see `isDrawing=true` (setState is async) → `onPathsChange` called twice → duplicate annotation path in Firestore. Fixed with `committedRef` (reset on `pointerdown`, set synchronously by whichever handler fires first; second handler sees `true` and returns early).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | [#1826](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1826) |
| 2026-06-03 | State & Data       | `quizScoreboard.ts` `getEarnedPoints`: iterated `sortedAnswers` without deduplicating by `questionId`. Firestore `arrayUnion` races / Drive-sync double-writes can produce duplicate entries → question scored twice → score > 100%, streak reset by wrong duplicate. Fixed with `seenQuestionIds: Set<string>` guard (same pattern as #1728/#1777/#1787/#1803).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | [#1827](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1827) |
| 2026-06-03 | Admin & Config     | `widgets.stickers` namespace (12 keys) added to EN for Sticker Book widget never propagated to DE or FR. All `t('widgets.stickers.*')` calls in `StickerBookWidget.tsx` have no `defaultValue` fallback → raw key paths shown in buttons, tooltips, and toasts for DE/FR users. (ES had it; `admin.stickers` was fixed in #1788 but the separate widget-level path was missed.) Synced 12 keys to DE and FR.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | [#1828](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1828) |
| 2026-06-03 | Build & Tooling    | `normalizeInvite` (`functions/src/organizationInvites.ts`): `email.indexOf('.', atIdx)` searched from `@` position itself; for `user@.com` the `.` in `.com` is at `atIdx+1`, returning non-negative → address accepted as valid. Empty-first-label domains are rejected by every real mail server; accepting creates unclaim-able member docs and queues undeliverable invite emails. Changed to `email.indexOf('.', atIdx + 2)`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | [#1829](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1829) |
| 2026-06-04 | Widgets            | `useBreathing` (`components/widgets/Breathing/useBreathing.ts`): `toggleActive` always reset to `'inhale'` phase with full duration on every activation. A teacher pausing mid-exhale and pressing Resume was silently dropped back to the start of a new inhale cycle. Fixed by checking `stateRef.current.phase` on activation: if `'ready'` → fresh start; otherwise → reconstruct `startTime` as `now - (progress × phaseDuration)` so the RAF loop resumes from the exact pause position.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | [#1853](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1853) |

Child elements never carry the attribute → `targetId` always `null` → all keyboard widget actions silently dropped when focus was on any child element. Fixed by storing the `closest<HTMLElement>('.widget')` result and calling `getAttribute` on it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | [#1854](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/1854) |

A widget/feature placed in a folder stayed visible and addable there after access was revoked. Fixed with a `canAccessTool` prop filtering into `visibleItems` for the tile, popover grid, and its `SortableContext`; `folder.items` itself is untouched so a restored permission returns the item to its slot. Two further claude[bot] review rounds found real regressions in the fix itself: (1) `!items.some(...)` is always `true` for `items: []`, permanently hiding freshly-created or fully-drained folders with no edit-mode escape hatch — fixed by extracting `shouldShowFolder(isEditMode, items, canAccessTool)`; (2) `SortableContext` reordered in `visibleItems`-space but `handleDragEnd` resolved positions via `folder.items.indexOf`, silently shifting a hidden item's absolute slot on every visible-item drag — fixed by extracting `reorderPreservingHidden`, which splices a visible-space reorder back into the full array leaving hidden items' indices untouched. Both helpers moved to `folderPermissions.ts` (react-refresh/only-export-components requires component files export only components). 12 regression tests across 3 commits. | [#2136](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/2136) |

The two diverge whenever `given` has an item outside `correctItems` (e.g. a question edited — item removed — after a student already submitted): the LIS formula still reports a full-length subsequence over the remaining items (`pointsEarned = max`) while the stale `isCorrect` stays `false`. Same contradiction class already fixed for Matching partial credit in #1950, never mirrored to Ordering. | 2026-07-05 | Widgets | `TimeTool` (`components/widgets/TimeTool/useTimeTool.ts`): `adjustTime` (the +/- hold-to-ramp buttons, via `useHoldAccelerate`, ramps to a 5x tick multiplier while held) was the one duration-setting path with no ceiling — unlike the Keypad (bounded by its 3-digit minutes field) and the admin building-defaults validator, both of which already clamp to `TIME_TOOL_MAX_DURATION_SECONDS` (59999s). Holding "+" could push `elapsedTime`/`duration` past that ceiling, overflowing the timer readout. Clamped both `next` and `nextDuration` to the shared constant. Regression test fails before (60059 instead of 59999), passes after. Two subsequent claude[bot] review rounds found THREE further issues, fixed same-day: (1) the "+" `AdjustButton` had no `disabled` ceiling guard symmetric with "-"'s floor guard, so it stayed clickable at the cap; (2) a first no-op guard attempt (`next === config.elapsedTime`) fixed the intended write-storm-while-running-at-ceiling case, but (3) that SAME guard introduced a more severe, more common regression: while running (not necessarily at the ceiling), `current` is the live RAF-decremented display value, not the frozen `config.elapsedTime` — so once per second of runtime (whenever the integer-millisecond delta lands on an exact second), `current + delta` coincidentally equals the stale `config.elapsedTime`, causing the guard to silently swallow a legitimate "+"/"-" press. Fixed by comparing against `current` (what `next` was actually computed from) instead of `config.elapsedTime` — verified with a test that advances fake timers 60s on a running 300s timer (confirmed empirically: `vi.useFakeTimers()` does intercept `requestAnimationFrame`, so RAF genuinely ticks under it) then asserts a `+60` press is NOT dropped. Also gated the "+" button's `disabled` prop on `config.elapsedTime` (not just the decaying `displayTime`) since the live display drops below the ceiling within one RAF frame while running. Orchestrator also trimmed several multi-line comment blocks to one line each per CLAUDE.md during review. | [#2147](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/2147) |

| 2026-07-05 | Build & Tooling | `firestore.rules` `/shared_boards` read rule: the substitute-share `@orono.k12.mn.us` email branch had no `expiresAt` cutoff, unlike the sibling `/shared_collections` rule. `expireSubShares.ts` deliberately leaves an expired doc's `driveGrants` (email/fileId/permissionId) in place for up to a 7-day grace window for the client-side Drive-permission revoker — during that window any district staff account could read the frozen board + Drive-grant metadata indefinitely. `tests/rules/sharedBoards.test.ts` had zero substitute-mode coverage, which is how the asymmetry with the fully-tested sibling rule escaped notice. Added the same `expiresAt > request.time.toMillis()` cutoff (host/admin still read past expiry for cleanup). Verified via Firestore emulator: 1/29 new tests fail on unpatched rules, 74/74 (both sibling suites) pass after. All 4 assigned backlog leads (SSRF guard, `GEMINI_SPECIFIC_FEATURES` drift, LTI null-clobber, functions test-count guard) re-audited and confirmed clean/already covered. **Critical follow-up caught by claude[bot] review, missed by the rules-test verification above**: the doc-level `getDoc`/emulator tests all passed, but `hooks/useSubstituteShares.ts`'s live `onSnapshot` _query_ only filtered `intendedMode`/`buildingId` — Firestore rejects an entire list query if it can't statically prove the rule's new `expiresAt` inequality from the query's own `where()` clauses, so every non-host/non-admin Orono teacher's subs-portal query would have failed with `PERMISSION_DENIED` after deploy. Fixed by adding `where('expiresAt', '>', Date.now())` (mirroring `SubCollectionsList.tsx`'s existing pattern) plus the matching composite index `(intendedMode, buildingId, expiresAt, __name__)` in `firestore.indexes.json`; updated both files' now-stale comments. Also hoisted an admin-doc re-seed into a nested `beforeEach` per a secondary review note. A THIRD review round found the query fix itself was incomplete: `Date.now()` is only captured when the effect runs, so a share expiring WHILE the listener stays open remains inside the frozen filter — when `expireSubShares.ts` later writes to it, Firestore denies the WHOLE query with `permission-denied` (confirmed empirically against the real emulator: a live query listener gets a full stream error, not a silent per-doc removal, when a previously-matched doc loses rule eligibility via a write). Fixed with a capped retry-and-resubscribe (fresh `Date.now()` baseline) in the error handler. Separately, `SubBoardScreen.tsx`'s expiry UX broke: before this PR an expired share was still readable so the client-side 60s timer could show "This share has expired."; now the rule denies the read outright before that timer ever starts. Added a `permissionDeniedLikelyExpired` flag (permission-denied on a substitute share can only mean expiry, since the rule's other branches are host/admin) and wired it into the same expired-state UI path. A FOURTH review round on the retry fix itself (round 3's fix) found three more real issues: (a) `retryCountRef` was reset only on success, not on a `buildingId` change, so switching buildings after partial retries inherited a depleted budget — fixed by tracking the previous `buildingId` in a second ref and resetting only on an actual change (an initial "reset unconditionally at the top of the effect" attempt broke the retry cap entirely, since the effect also re-runs on the retry-driven `retryToken` bump); (b) the `permissionDeniedLikelyExpired` doc comment overclaimed the invariant — post-fix, a non-Orono/non-host/non-admin caller also gets denied regardless of expiry, so the comment was corrected without changing the (acceptable) redirect behavior; (c) the pre-existing `.*@orono[.]k12[.]mn[.]us$` email regex (3 occurrences) let the leading `.*` absorb an embedded `@`, so `x@evil.com@orono.k12.mn.us` passed — confirmed exploitable against the real emulator, fixed to `[^@]+@orono...` in all 3 spots. Five total review rounds on one PR, each surfacing a genuinely real, previously-undetected issue — see Notes & Gotchas on iterative review depth. | [#2150](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/2150) |
| 2026-07-07 | Build & Tooling | **SECURITY**: `AnnouncementOverlay.tsx` "PATH B" listener query (used whenever `orgId` hasn't resolved — a permanent state for internal-tier users never added as an org member) ran `where('isActive','==',true)` with no `orgId` filter. Confirmed against the real Firestore emulator: a security rule's per-document OR-branches (`isOrgMember(resource.data.orgId)`) are NOT enforced as a per-doc filter for a list query whose own `where()` clauses don't pin the same field — Firestore silently returned every `isActive` doc including other orgs', even though a direct `getDoc()` on the same doc is correctly denied. Fixed with `where('orgId','==',null)` (a filter Firestore can structurally prove safe) plus a `roleResolved` gate so the unscoped query can never open during the loading window either. New emulator test `tests/rules/announcementsQuery.test.ts` (documents the leak + proves the fix) plus updated component tests. Orchestrator independently re-ran `test:rules` (47/47 files, 1112/1112 tests) and reproduced fail-before (4/47) on the pre-fix component. | [#2158](https://github.com/ops-pivers/spartboard/pull/2158) |

Reordering (alphabetizing) or inserting a line above an existing student shifted every subsequent index lookup by one, silently reattaching another student's `id`/`pin`/`classLinkSourcedId` to the wrong name — a data-integrity bug since `student.id` anchors attendance, pins, and ClassLink sync. Rejected band-aid: pure name-based Map lookup, which would break the intentional "rename in place keeps the same ID" behavior. Fixed with two-phase matching: exact-name match first (FIFO per name-key, so reorder can't steal an ID), then positional pairing of only the genuine leftover renames. 2 regression tests; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`expected 'a1' to be 'b2'`) → pass-after (22/22) via file-swap against dev-paul, plus a full isolated `pnpm run validate`/build re-run post-dispatch. | [#2288](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2288) |
| 2026-07-26 | Dashboard & Layout | Three sibling FAB menus (`BoardNavFab.tsx` boards menu, `CollectionSwitcherMenu.tsx`, `BoardActionsFab.tsx` zoom popup) handled Escape via React `onKeyDown` with no `stopPropagation()`. All render outside any `.widget` ancestor, so the unstopped native event bubbled to `DashboardView`'s global Escape handler, which — finding no `.widget` ancestor for the focused menu item — fell back to minimizing the topmost z-index widget (e.g. a running timer) whenever a teacher dismissed one of these menus with Escape. Same bug class as `ToolDockItem`/`RemoteControlMenu`/`ClassRosterMenu` (#2266); these three FABs were added later and missed the fix. Added `stopPropagation()` to all three, matching the established pattern. 3 new test files, 33 tests total; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (window spy called) → pass-after (33/33) via file-swap, plus isolated validate/build re-run. | [#2289](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2289) |
| 2026-07-26 | State & Data | `buildContributionResponse()` (`utils/plcContributions.ts`) built `answerByQuestionId` via `new Map(response.answers.map(...))`, which keeps the LAST entry in array order when a response carries two answers for the same `questionId` (stale-write race or legacy/migrated doc). The canonical scoring source `getEarnedPoints` (`quizScoreboard.ts`) sorts by `answeredAt` ascending and credits the FIRST answer — divergence meant the PLC Shared Data view could show a different score than the teacher's own Scoreboard for the same response. Same dedup-fence bug family as #1728/#1777/#1787/#1803/#1827/#1935 (9th+ instance), applied here for the first time. Fixed by sorting by `answeredAt` before building the map, keeping only the first-seen entry per question. 1 new regression test (0 pts before fix → 2 pts after); orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before/pass-after via file-swap plus isolated validate/build re-run. | [#2290](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2290) |

Retired below. | — |
| 2026-07-26 | Build & Tooling | `functions/src/finalizeIdleQuizAttempts.ts` (hourly cron that auto-submits abandoned quiz attempts) fetched stale responses via a single un-paginated `.limit(MAX_READ_PER_RUN).get()`, ordered oldest-`lastWriteAt`-first. `'paused'` sessions are skipped indefinitely and always sort oldest, so once the accumulated paused/orphan backlog exceeded one page, a genuinely-idle ACTIVE response sorting after it was pushed out of the query window on every future run forever — silently reintroducing the exact data-loss bug this sweep exists to prevent. Same pagination-cliff class as `gcPlcOrphans.ts`/`expireActivityWallShares.ts`, never retrofitted here; file had zero prior test coverage. Fixed with `startAfter`-cursor pagination up to a much higher ceiling (20000 vs. 1600), and extracted a testable `runFinalizeIdleQuizAttempts(db, now)` core. **Orchestrator catch during review**: the subagent's own fail-before check reverted to a single fetch still capped at the _new_ 20000-ceiling, which didn't actually reproduce the bug at the test's fixture scale (505 fillers) — re-verified with a stricter revert (single fetch capped at `RESPONSE_PAGE_SIZE`, matching what an un-paginated fetch actually looks like at this scale), confirming a real fail (`expected 'in-progress' to be 'completed'`) → pass (6/6) after restoring real pagination. | [#2291](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2291) |

Re-ran the full `validate` cleanly once installs finished: GREEN (587 root test files / 7108 tests, 40 functions test files / 775 tests, test:counts guard). Disproven with evidence: `PlcRecoveryPanel.tsx` (the component rendering this namespace) also renders `plcDashboard.resources.loadingPlcs`/`loadPlcsError` on the _same screen_, and those already correctly say "PLGs werden geladen…"/"Cargando Comunidades…" — the same screen was mixing translated and untranslated terminology for the identical concept. | 2026-07-31 | Build & Tooling | `functions/src/index.test.ts`'s `generateVideoActivity — accessLevel enforcement` describe block never mocked `@google/genai`. An admin caller short-circuits every permission/usage gate in the handler and reaches the real `ai.models.generateContent(...)` call — `GEMINI_API_KEY.value()` resolves to a truthy mock string, so the SDK makes a genuine HTTPS round-trip to Google's live API. Normally rejected in well under 100ms, but under CPU/network contention (concurrent nightly worktrees — the exact condition that surfaced this in tonight's baseline) that round-trip can exceed Vitest's 5000ms timeout: a real defect (a unit test depending on live network reachability), not a one-off fluke. Fixed with a `vi.mock('@google/genai', ...)` that preserves real exports (the `Type` enum) but replaces `GoogleGenAI` with a deterministic spy; rejected band-aid: bumping the timeout, which leaves the network dependency intact and just makes failures rarer. New assertions on all 4 tests in the block (permission-denied tests assert the mock was never called; success tests assert it was called once). Orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`expected "vi.fn()" to be called 1 times, but got 0 times`, via removing only the new `vi.mock` block) → pass-after (4/4), confirming via the error trace that the real network call no longer happens at all. | [#2315](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2315) |

Org A's data stayed rendered under org B's heading until B's first snapshot landed; for `useOrgMembers` specifically, stale org-A building IDs could reach the org-B invite form (`usersLoading`/`rolesLoading` gate doesn't cover `buildingsLoading`) — a real data-integrity risk, not just a visual flash. | 2026-08-04 | Admin & Config | `components/admin/BetaUsersPanel.tsx` (widget-level beta-tester editor, used by `FeaturePermissionsManager.tsx`) stored beta-user emails with whatever casing the admin typed — the one outlier vs. its two siblings (`GlobalPermissionsManager.tsx`, `BackgroundManager/index.tsx`), which both lowercase on write. Several real consumers of `betaUsers[]` compare case-sensitively (Firestore `array-contains` queries in `CustomWidgetsContext`/`useBackgrounds`, `functions/src/aiGeneration.ts`'s check), so a beta-enabled widget could silently not show up as a _default_ dock tool (`DashboardContext.getDefaultDockTools`'s plain `.includes()`) for a user whose email an admin typed with any uppercase letter — the one safe path, `AuthContext.isBetaUser`, lowercases both sides, which is why this went unnoticed. Rejected band-aid: fixing the case-sensitive read site instead of the write-time normalization (leaves other current/future case-sensitive consumers exposed). | 2026-08-05 | Dashboard & Layout | `components/common/Modal.tsx` — the shared primitive behind 50+ call sites — had an opt-in `captureEscape` prop (default `false`, bubble-phase) that **no production caller ever set to `true`**. Since Modal is portalled straight onto `document.body`, every Escape-to-dismiss also bubbled to `DashboardView`'s global fallback handler and silently minimized the topmost widget behind it. **Original fix (reverted same night)**: making Modal register in the capture phase with `stopPropagation()`. An automated review flagged this as the run's "widest-blast-radius change" and recommended human verification that no in-modal control depends on bubble-phase ordering; the orchestrator investigated directly instead of dismissing it and confirmed 2 concrete, live regressions — (1) `useDialog()` confirm/prompt/alert dialogs opened from inside a Modal (e.g. `PlcEditModal`'s "Remove member?" confirm) would also close the parent Modal, a same-phase/same-target registration-order race where plain `stopPropagation()` doesn't block sibling capture listeners the way `stopImmediatePropagation()` does; (2) `RestrictionsPicker.tsx`'s in-modal popover (inside `RosterEditorModal`) would never receive the Escape keydown at all, since capture-phase `stopPropagation()` on `window` fires before the event reaches down to the popover's own bubble-phase handler. **Final fix**: reverted `Modal.tsx`/`domHelpers.ts`/`Modal.test.tsx` entirely to `dev-paul`, and instead fixed the actual source — `DashboardView`'s global Escape handler now bails via `useHasOpenModal()` (Modal's own pre-existing open-count tracker in `components/common/modalStore.ts`) before touching any widget. Zero blast radius on nested modal content. 2 new regression tests; fail-before (27/28, temporarily reverting only `DashboardView.tsx`) → pass-after (28/28) verified, plus a full validate/build re-run. | [#2392](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2392) |

Rejected band-aid: forcing `setIsLoading(false)` after `await inflight` with no catch — would silently swallow a shared-fetch failure for followers. Fixed with a follower-side `try/catch/finally` and a shared `toLibraryError()` helper so both paths settle `isLoading` and `error` correctly. 1 new regression test; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`expected true to be false`) → pass-after (16/16) via file-swap, plus a full validate/build re-run. | 2026-08-11 | Build & Tooling    | **SECURITY (SSRF).** `functions/src/lti/ags.ts` and `functions/src/lti/nrps.ts` (new LTI 1.3/Schoology integration, added 2026-08-04 — after the last SSRF audit on 2026-07-19) make outbound `fetch()` calls to platform-asserted URLs (AGS token/lineitem endpoints, NRPS membership endpoint, and NRPS `Link: rel="next"` pagination URLs taken from response headers) carrying an `Authorization: Bearer` header, none setting `redirect: 'manual'`. Native `fetch()` follows redirects by default — unlike the axios-based `embedProxy.ts` calls already guarded with `maxRedirects: 0`, this fetch-based client never inherited an equivalent guard, so a 3xx response (or a malicious `Link` header) could retarget the bearer-token-bearing request at an internal/cloud-metadata host. Empirically verified against a real HTTP server 302-ing to `169.254.169.254` — default `fetch()` followed it, `redirect: 'manual'` refused. Fixed by adding `redirect: 'manual'` to all 3 call sites; the resulting `!res.ok` response already falls through existing error handling. 3 new regression assertions across `ags.test.ts`/`nrps.test.ts`; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (3 fail: `expected undefined to be 'manual'`) → pass-after (21/21) via file-swap, plus a full validate/build re-run. First fix (`.widget, [data-widget-portal]` selector, no fallback) shipped with a real regression caught by automated review: the broadened selector also matches 9 other `[data-widget-portal]` elements repo-wide that carry no `data-widget-id` (Drawing tool/export popovers, GuidedLearningEditor menus, TextWidget formatting toolbars, dialog roots, etc.) — for those, `getAttribute('data-widget-id')` returns `null`, silently disabling the topmost-widget fallback that existed before this PR (Delete/Alt+P became a no-op instead of acting on `topWidget`). Fixed by extracting a `resolveTargetWidgetId(topWidgetId)` helper (also closing the reviewer's 3x-duplicated-logic nit) that falls back to `topWidgetId` via `?? ` whenever the matched ancestor has no `data-widget-id`, preserving pre-PR behavior for all 9 other portals while fixing the SettingsPanel case. 1 new regression test added to the 3 already shipped, covering the id-less-portal fallback specifically; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`expected [] to have a length of 1 but got 0`, against the already-shipped pre-fallback code) → pass-after (35/35), plus a full validate/build re-run. | [#2460](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2460) |

| 2026-08-16 | State & Data       | `hooks/useGlobalStyleEditor.ts`'s debounced window/dock transparency commits (200ms debounce) weren't scoped to the board the drag started on — `setGlobalStyle` always writes to whichever board is active when the timer fires, not whichever was active at drag time. Switching boards mid-debounce (e.g. via the global `Alt+ArrowLeft/Right` shortcut) silently wrote the old board's dragged value onto the new board's `GlobalStyle`, corrupting it; `pendingWindow`/`pendingDock` also weren't reset on board switch, so the slider itself could keep showing the stale value. Rejected band-aid: only resetting the pending display values (fixes the visual glitch but not the underlying data corruption — the already-armed timer still fires against whatever board is current). Fixed by capturing the origin board id at drag time and skipping the commit if the active board no longer matches when the debounce fires. 1 new regression test; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`expected 0.5 to be 0.9`) → pass-after via file-swap, plus a full validate/build re-run. Orchestrator trimmed two multi-line comments to one line each during review. Automated review: "Ready" — noted the fix's correctness depends on `useDebouncedCallback` storing its callback in a ref reassigned every render (so `activeDashboard?.id` reads current, not stale); flagged as a good follow-up, not blocking, that only `windowTransparency` got a dedicated test (`dockTransparency` gets identical treatment with no test of its own). | [#2469](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2469) |

| 2026-08-17 | Admin & Config     | `components/admin/Organization/components/primitives.tsx`'s `RowMenu`/`CellPopover`/`LocalModal` all registered their own bubble-phase `document` Escape listener, but `AdminSettings.tsx` also listens on `document` — same node, so registration order (not `stopPropagation()`) decided which fired first, and `AdminSettings` always won, closing the whole Admin Settings panel whenever a nested popover/modal was dismissed with Escape. This is a variant of the "portalled popover doesn't stop Escape propagation" bug class already fixed 10+ times elsewhere, but those fixes' pattern (bubble-phase `document.stopPropagation()`) doesn't work here since both listeners share the same node. Fixed with a new `useCaptureEscape` hook: `window` capture-phase listener + `stopImmediatePropagation()`, matching the existing `captureEscape` pattern in `components/common/Modal.tsx`. A concurrent 2026-08-18 run independently diagnosed a narrower version of this same bug (leaking only to `DashboardView`'s global handler, missing the more severe `AdminSettings`-own-listener case this fix addresses) and, after empirically cross-testing both fixes, closed its own PR (#2489) in favor of this one — see the 2026-08-18 Admin & Config row. | [#2481](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2481) |
| 2026-08-17 | Build & Tooling    | `functions/src/gcPlcOrphans.ts`'s `sweepVersionOverflow()` issued a single un-paginated `.get()` over a group's `versions` subcollection — the same "pagination cliff" bug class already fixed for every other scan in this file, just missed one level deeper. Backlog item open since 2026-08-16. Fixed by extracting a shared `fetchPaginated()` helper (also now used by `fetchCategoryPaginated`) and adding bounded pagination (`MAX_VERSIONS_SCAN_PER_GROUP` / `VERSIONS_PAGE_SIZE`), paginated on `documentId()` (matching this file's other sweeps). **Superseded same-day**: a concurrent 2026-08-18 run's fix (#2490) orders by the real numeric `version` field instead, avoiding a lexicographic-vs-numeric doc-id skew this PR's approach has (see this file's own backlog item below) — closed this PR in favor of #2490. | — (superseded by [#2490](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2490)) |

That assessment was disproven: `nexus.ts`/`stationsActions.ts` already treat assignment keys as opaque strings, so the bug was entirely contained in `Widget.tsx`'s own name-keyed construction. Fixed id-keyed (roster `id` for class mode) with a legacy-name-keyed read fallback (for pre-fix dashboards and `nexus.ts`'s still-name-keyed "Send Groups → Stations" writes) plus a `coalesceLegacyKeys` migration step in every persist, since `rotateAssignments`/`resetStation` carry forward existing entries generically by key. 2 new regression tests; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (2/2 fail) → pass-after (29/29, full `Stations/` suite) via patch-apply against dev-paul, plus a full validate/build re-run (both green, independently on 2nd attempt after an OOM kill from 5-worktree parallel contention — see Notes & Gotchas). A concurrent 2026-08-17 run independently found and fixed the identical bug (#2478, dispatched from the same unmerged base — see Notes & Gotchas); cross-tested both, ported #2478's 2 extra regression scenarios onto this fix (both pass), and closed #2478 as the weaker of the two (its per-call-site cleanup doesn't cover `handleRotate`/`resetStation`, unlike this fix's universal `persistAssignments`-level coalescing). Automated review flagged the rotate/reset-station coalescing path and the duplicate-name migration outcome as untested; both gaps closed with 3 additional regression tests. | [#2486](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2486) |
| 2026-08-18 | Dashboard & Layout | `components/common/SettingsPanel.tsx` anchors to `widgetRef.getBoundingClientRect()` but only recomputed position on `[updatePosition, viewport, zoom]` — panning the board canvas moves widgets via a CSS `transform` on an ancestor container without touching `widget.x`/`widget.y`, so the panel never re-fired and was left visually stranded at its pre-pan screen position while the widget slid out from under it. `DraggableWindow`'s tool menu and snap menu already subscribe to the `board-pan` window event `DashboardView` dispatches on every pan frame for exactly this purpose; `SettingsPanel` never wired up the same listener. Fixed by adding the same `board-pan` subscription, mirroring the established sibling pattern — no band-aid needed. 1 new regression test; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`expected 212 to be 512`) → pass-after (9/9) via patch-apply against dev-paul, plus a full validate/build re-run (second attempt clean after an OOM kill from parallel-worktree contention). Orchestrator trimmed a 9-line test comment to one line during review. Automated review: "Ready", no concerns. | [#2487](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2487) |
| 2026-08-18 | State & Data       | `context/DashboardContext.tsx`'s `getDefaultDockTools` (seeds a new user's dock, backs "Reset to defaults") re-derived beta-access eligibility independently of `AuthContext.canAccessWidget`: the authoritative gate (`AuthContext.isBetaUser`) compares emails case-insensitively and also grants access via `userRoles.betaTeachers`/`userRoles.superAdmins`/`roleId === 'super_admin'`, while `DashboardContext`'s copy did a case-sensitive `.includes()` against only the permission's own `betaUsers` array. #2375 (2026-08-04) partially addressed this by lowercasing `betaUsers` on write but explicitly left this read site's gap open and never covered the role-list sources. A legitimately-beta user could add a widget manually via the Dock but never get it seeded/restored by default. Fixed by extracting the check into a shared `utils/betaAccess.ts` (single source of truth) used by both `AuthContext` and `DashboardContext`; rejected band-aid: patching just the case-sensitivity, which would still miss the role-list sources and could re-drift later. 3 new regression tests; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (2/3 fail) → pass-after (3/3) via patch-apply against dev-paul, plus a standalone `tsc --noEmit` and a full validate/build re-run. Orchestrator trimmed 3 multi-line comments to one line each during review. Automated review ("Ready"): asked for the removed LO2 harmonization rationale comment to be preserved and for direct unit coverage of the `superAdmins`/`super_admin` grant sources in `utils/betaAccess.ts`; both addressed. | [#2488](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2488) |
| 2026-08-18 | Admin & Config     | `components/admin/Organization/components/primitives.tsx`'s `RowMenu`/`CellPopover`/`LocalModal` closed on Escape without `stopPropagation()`, flagged as an open, unverified lead on 2026-08-14. This run's own fix (`stopPropagation()` + `isEscapeFromWidgetInput()`, the idiom matching 13+ prior fixes) was independently re-verified fail-before/pass-after, but turned out to be an INCOMPLETE diagnosis: `AdminSettings.tsx` registers its own `document`-level Escape listener (closing the whole panel) that mounts before any nested popover, and same-target bubble-phase listeners all fire in registration order regardless of a later listener's `stopPropagation()` — only `stopImmediatePropagation()`/capture-phase stops an already-scheduled sibling. A concurrent 2026-08-17 run (dispatched from the same unmerged base — see Notes & Gotchas) independently found this deeper root cause and shipped a capture-phase `useCaptureEscape` fix in #2481, which this run's orchestrator empirically confirmed (via a standalone cross-test) fixes BOTH the `AdminSettings`-closes bug and the `DashboardView`-leak bug this run originally diagnosed — this run's own PR (#2489) only fixed the latter. Closed #2489 in favor of #2481; no PR credited to this run for this area. | — (superseded by [#2481](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2481)) |
| 2026-08-18 | Build & Tooling    | `functions/src/gcPlcOrphans.ts`'s `sweepVersionOverflow()` (flagged as a backlog lead 2026-08-16) read an entire synced group's `versions` subcollection into memory with an unbounded `.get()` and sorted client-side — the only sweep in this file with no per-run ceiling. More severe than a plain pagination cliff: this sweep runs BEFORE the per-PLC loop with no `try/catch` anywhere in the file, so one pathological group's unbounded read/sort could abort the entire nightly GC run before any PLC's activity/presence/tombstone sweep starts. Fixed via a Firestore-side `orderBy('version','desc').offset(VERSION_HISTORY_LIMIT).limit(MAX_VERSIONS_SCAN_PER_GROUP)` query, bounded by a new `MAX_VERSIONS_SCAN_PER_GROUP = 5000` ceiling mirroring the file's established `MAX_*_PER_RUN` pattern. Rejected band-aid: mirroring the file's other sweeps' doc-id `startAfter` cursor — doc ids here are stringified version numbers, so string-order pagination doesn't match numeric recency and could delete the wrong (non-oldest) snapshots; ordering by the real numeric `version` field server-side avoids that trap. 3 new regression tests (cap enforcement, ceiling-hit warning, unchanged-behavior-within-cap) plus stub-Firestore `orderBy(field, direction)`/`.offset()` support; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (2/2 fail) → pass-after (38/38) via patch-apply against dev-paul, plus a standalone `tsc --noEmit` and a full validate/build:all re-run. This file (and its `.test.ts`) has an established multi-paragraph-docstring convention throughout — matches the `firestore.rules` exception, so no comment trims were needed here (unlike the other 4 areas' PRs tonight). A concurrent 2026-08-17 run independently found and fixed the identical bug (#2482, dispatched from the same unmerged base — see Notes & Gotchas); closed #2482 as the weaker of the two — its own doc comment and backlog item concede a real lexicographic-vs-numeric doc-id skew (deferred "pending a schema change") that this fix avoids entirely by ordering on the real `version` field server-side; no schema change was actually needed. Automated review flagged that `orderBy('version')` silently excludes any doc missing the `version` field (unlike the old code's deliberate "malformed docs sort oldest, pruned first" behavior); confirmed via the single client writer (`PlcVersionSnapshot.version: number` is non-optional, always written via a full `setDoc`) that this is unreachable through normal operation, documented the reasoning, and added a test pinning the accepted trade-off. | [#2490](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2490) |

| 2026-08-20 | Admin & Config     | `BuildingsView.tsx`'s "New building" modal defaulted "Grades served" to a hardcoded `'K-5'` regardless of the selected building `Type` — `buildingRecordToBuilding()` only falls back to a Type-derived grade band when the stored string fails to parse, and `'K-5'` always parsed successfully, so an admin creating a High/Middle/Other building who left the field untouched silently got elementary-band widget filtering. Fixed by exporting `gradeLabelFromType()` and deferring the field's default to save time (`grades.trim() || gradeLabelFromType(type)`), so a manual entry is always respected. Rejected band-aid: a `useEffect` syncing `grades` to `type` on every change — discouraged by CLAUDE.md's derived-state guidance and would clobber a manually-entered value on a later `Type` change. 1 new regression test; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`expected 'K-5' to be '9-12'`) → pass-after via file-swap against dev-paul, plus a full validate/build re-run. **Mid-review repair**: automated review caught that this fix's own change introduced a NEW gap for `type === 'other'` — `gradeLabelFromType('other')` returned `''`, so an Other-type building left blank now saved `grades: ''` (worse than the old hardcoded-K-5 behavior, which was wrong but at least non-empty) — `buildingRecordToBuilding()` turns that into an empty `gradeLevels` array, which hides every grade-gated widget in `FeaturePermissionsManager.tsx`'s `.some()` filter. Orchestrator fixed by giving `'other'` an explicit non-empty default (all 4 grade bands / `'K-12'`), matching this same file's own precedent (the seeded "Orono Community Education" K-12 building), with 2 new regression tests covering both the UI save path and `buildingRecordToBuilding()` directly — independently fail-before/pass-after verified against the pre-repair commit. Ruled out (investigated, not a bug): `DomainsView.tsx:299`'s `addedAt` UTC-derived value — the computation is discarded, since `useOrgDomains.ts::addDomain` hardcodes its own server-managed `addedAt` and never reads the caller's. | [#2511](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2511) |
| 2026-08-20 | Build & Tooling    | `firestore.rules`' `announcements/{id}/pollVotes/{optionIndex}` allowed `write: if request.auth != null` with no shape/value validation — any authenticated user (including an anonymous PIN-joined student) could overwrite a poll's tally doc with an arbitrary shape/value or delete it, unlike the tightly-validated `poll_sessions/*/votes/*` sibling. Confirmed live-reachable via the standing security-rules tracking doc (open since 2026-06-10) and this repo's own backlog (open since 2026-08-14). Fixed with `hasOnly(['count']) && count is int && count >= 0` on create/update, `isAdmin()`-only delete — verified against the one live client writer (`PollWidget.vote()`'s `increment()`-merge) and the full `test:rules` suite. Rejected band-aid: routing votes through a new Cloud Function (an alternative floated in the tracking doc) — over-scope for a single-bug fix; the shape-validated-but-client-writable idiom already matches this repo's established pattern elsewhere (e.g. `quiz_attempt_ledger`). Scope note: still no per-voter dedup (a same-shaped overwrite by a different user remains possible) — would need a `poll_sessions/votes`-style per-voter-doc redesign, left open. 10 new regression tests against the real Firestore emulator; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (4/10 fail) → pass-after (10/10) directly against the real emulator (not just re-running the subagent's own tests), plus a full `test:rules` re-run (48/48 files, 1155/1155 tests, no regressions) and a full validate/build:all re-run. Bonus fix folded in: `tests/components/plcMeetingMode.test.tsx`'s longest test (5 chained UI steps) reproducibly flaked under real nightly-run CPU contention at Vitest's 5000ms default — bumped to 15000ms with a one-line comment. Automated review confirmed no blocking issues; the `nightly/build-tooling-2026-08-19` (`b3804bf9`) `lti_session_memberships` coverage commit was deliberately left uncherry-picked per instructions rather than padding the branch. **Post-push repair**: a full automated review round found that `hasOnly(['count'])` applies to the merged RESULT, not just the write payload — any pre-existing `pollVotes` doc carrying an extra field or non-int `count` (impossible via the current `PollWidget.vote()` call site, but not impossible via a pre-fix write or manual edit) would have every future increment-merge vote silently denied, and `vote()`'s fire-and-forget `setDoc` had no `.catch`, so the rejection became an unhandled promise rejection while the UI already showed the vote as cast. Orchestrator could not query the live production collection to rule this out, so instead of guessing: added `.catch(err => logError(...))` to surface any future rejection (did not revert the optimistic UI state — a larger UX change out of scope), plus a regression test seeding a legacy-shaped doc to pin the documented deny-on-update behavior. `test:rules` re-confirmed 48/48 files, 1156/1156 tests; full validate/build:all re-run. | [#2512](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2512) |

| 2026-08-21 | State & Data       | `context/DialogContext.tsx`'s `openNext()` batches `setCurrentDialog(null)` then `setCurrentDialog(next)` in one React render, so `DialogContainer` never renders an intermediate unmount frame between two queued dialogs — and `PromptDialog`'s un-keyed `useState(defaultValue)` meant React reused the same instance across a same-kind swap, leaking the first dialog's edited input into the second (the specific leak vector flagged 2026-08-11, 2 nights after the batching risk was first logged 2026-07-13). Rather than continuing the 7-night search for a reachable trigger path, closed the underlying risk directly: added a monotonic `id` to every enqueued dialog and keyed `DialogContainer`'s render on it, guaranteeing a fresh mount per occurrence regardless of batching timing. Rejected band-aid: forcing an extra render via `flushSync`/`setTimeout(0)` — fragile, depends on batching internals, and adds a visible flicker; keying is the sustainable fix. 1 new regression test (`DialogContainer.queuedPrompt.test.tsx`, using `vi.unmock` to exercise the real `DialogProvider`+`DialogContainer` integration); orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`expected 'edited by user' to be 'bar'`) → pass-after via file-swap against `dev-paul`, plus a full validate/build:all re-run; orchestrator trimmed 3 new comments (in `DialogContainer.tsx`, `DialogContext.tsx`, `DialogContextValue.ts`) down to one line each. | [#2519](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2519) |
| 2026-08-21 | Admin & Config     | `components/admin/WidgetBuilder/ConnectionsTab.tsx`'s `handleAdd()` gated `actionValue` on `form.actionValue !== 0`, treating `0` as "unset" — an admin explicitly configuring a step/index of `0` (e.g. "increment by 0") had it silently dropped from the saved `BlockConnection`. Downstream, `blockReducer.ts`'s `actionValue ?? 1` fallback then fired for `increment`/`decrement`/`add-score`, silently changing the value by 1 instead of the admin's chosen 0, with no indication the saved rule didn't match what was configured. Fixed by removing the `!== 0` sentinel check entirely, mirroring how the sibling `condition.value` field already stores `0` unconditionally. Rejected band-aid: changing `blockReducer.ts`'s fallback to `?? 0` — legitimately needed for genuinely-unconfigured legacy connections, and wouldn't fix the actual defect at the write site. 1 new regression test; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`expected undefined to be +0`) → pass-after via file-swap against this branch's pre-fix source, plus a full validate/build:all re-run; orchestrator trimmed the new test's 8-line comment down to one line. Also investigated and left the `gradeLabelFromType('other')` multi-building product-decision ambiguity (flagged last night, #2511) untouched, per instructions — its pinning test from last night stands as-is. | [#2520](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2520) |
| 2026-08-21 | Build & Tooling    | `scripts/recount-org-members.js`'s `emailDomain()` computed the domain without `.trim()`, while the live `organizationMemberCounters` Firestore trigger's own copy does trim — despite a doc-comment in the trigger explicitly claiming the two "must mirror" each other. A member email with a stray internal space after `@` (plausible from a bulk-invite paste; the invite boundary's outer `.trim()` never catches a mid-string space) buckets differently in the two, so running this "authoritative" reconcile script would silently overwrite a correct live per-domain count with a wrong one — and since both computations are deterministic, a re-run would never self-heal the drift. Secondary gap: the script had no `isMain` execution guard, so this logic could never be unit-tested at all, plausibly why the drift went unnoticed. Fixed with `.trim()` plus an `isMain` guard (mirroring `scripts/checkTestCounts.mjs`) exporting the pure helpers for testing. Rejected band-aid: adding just the `.trim()` without the guard/exports — would leave the script permanently untestable, the same condition that let this drift happen unnoticed in the first place. 2 new test files (a new `recount-org-members.test.ts` plus a mirrored parity case in `organizationMemberCounters.test.ts`); orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`expected 1 to be 2`, reverting just the `.trim()` call) → pass-after (9/9 and 25/25) via targeted revert against this branch's pre-fix source, plus a full validate/build:all re-run; orchestrator trimmed 4 new comment blocks down to one line each. | [#2521](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2521) |

Whenever the cheap 2nd call tipped over the daily quota cap right after the heavier 1st call, `searchYouTube()` silently resolved with every result's duration blanked to `0` instead of rejecting — while `components/widgets/VideoActivityWidget/components/Creator.tsx` has an explicit `instanceof YouTubeQuotaError` branch expecting the signal to propagate from either call, confirming the broken contract. Fixed by rethrowing `YouTubeQuotaError` before the blanket catch, mirroring the differentiated catch the first call already uses. Rejected band-aid: deleting the now-dead quota-detection branch to "simplify" toward the buggy behavior — the parallel structure with the first call's catch and `Creator.tsx`'s explicit branch make clear the signal was meant to propagate. 1 new regression test; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`promise resolved [...] instead of rejecting`) → pass-after (17/17, including the pre-existing non-quota-failure test confirming no regression) via file-swap against `dev-paul`, plus a full validate re-run. Restriction lists are meant to be symmetric (`normalizeRestrictions` enforces that when a teacher saves the roster editor), but the Randomizer reads `activeRoster.students` straight from Firestore, never normalized at read time — a historical, imported, or partially-edited roster can still be asymmetric, and whichever student's list was empty got silently treated as conflict-free, defeating the keep-apart safety feature depending purely on shuffle order. | 2026-08-23 | Build & Tooling    | `organizations/{orgId}/domains/{domainId}`'s `create`/`update` Firestore rules pinned the document schema but never validated the `domain` string's format. `resolveOrgIdForDomain` (`functions/src/classlinkShared.ts`) resolves a signed-in user's org via an EXACT-match query built from `'@' + lowercased email/hd domain` — it never normalizes the STORED side, so a domain doc written with mismatched case or stray whitespace would look "added" in the admin UI but silently break sign-in org-resolution for that domain forever, with no error anywhere. Fixed by adding a `^@[a-z0-9.-]+[.][a-z]{2,}$` format check to both `create` and `update`. Rejected band-aid: normalizing in `resolveOrgIdForDomain` itself — treats the symptom at every read site instead of preventing bad data at the one write path. 4 new regression tests against the real emulator; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (exactly the 4 new tests failed, 1171 others still passed, reverting `firestore.rules` and re-running the full `test:rules` suite) → pass-after (49 files/1175 tests), plus a full validate/build/`test:rules` re-run (this area was already based on `dev-paul` directly, no rebase needed). Also folded in the previously-written, previously-held-back `lti_session_memberships` coverage test (open backlog item since 2026-08-17/19, most recently re-preserved on the unopened `nightly/build-tooling-2026-08-22` branch) alongside this genuine bug fix, per the standing "coverage needs an accompanying bug fix to ship" guardrail — closes out that recurring backlog row. **Process note**: `pnpm run test:rules -- <path>` does not work for scoping to one file — the script wraps everything in `firebase emulators:exec`, which rejects extra positional args ("Too many arguments"); had to re-run the full suite instead. See Notes & Gotchas. **Post-push repair**: automated review found the `update` rule's new `domain.matches(...)` check was nested inside the `isDomainAdmin(orgId)` branch only — since `isSuperAdmin()` is a standalone top-level `||` term (unlike `create`, where `(isSuperAdmin() || isDomainAdmin(orgId))` is hoisted into one term ANDed with the check), a super admin could bypass the new validation entirely and reintroduce the exact bug this PR fixes. Fixed by restructuring to `(isSuperAdmin() || isDomainAdmin(orgId)) && domain.matches(...) && (isSuperAdmin() || <domain-admin-only identity/hasOnly checks>)`, mirroring `create`'s structure while still preserving super admin's broader write privileges for every other field. 2 new regression tests (super admin blocked on unnormalized, still allowed on normalized); orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (reverting just this rule change made the new "super admin cannot..." test fail — request succeeded) → pass-after, plus a full validate/build/`test:rules` re-run (49 files/1177 tests). | [#2539](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2539) |

Opening a confirm/prompt dialog over a modal (a common admin/settings pattern) and closing it dropped `DialogContainer`'s counter to 0 → `overflow='unset'`, unlocking page scroll while the modal underneath was still open (symmetric for modal-closing-first). Fixed by extracting one shared reference-counted lock (`components/common/bodyScrollLock.ts`, `acquire/release/getBodyScrollLockCount`); both consumers call it, so overflow releases only when the LAST overlay closes. `Modal` still increments/decrements `openModalCount` for the unrelated `useHasOpenModal` signal. | 2026-08-25 | Widgets            | **SECURITY.** `components/widgets/MiniApp/Widget.tsx`'s runtime iframe rendered untrusted, AI-generated/imported mini-app `srcDoc` HTML with `sandbox="allow-scripts allow-forms allow-popups allow-modals allow-same-origin"`. On a `srcDoc` iframe (no URL of its own), `allow-same-origin` doesn't grant an isolated app-local origin — it makes the iframe inherit the PARENT document's own origin; combined with `allow-scripts`, any mini-app's JS could reach `window.parent`'s DOM and same-origin `localStorage`/cookies, i.e. read/manipulate the live teacher session. Confirmed unnecessary: the mini-app protocol (`aiGeneration.ts`'s system prompt) is entirely `postMessage`-based and every sibling implementation (`CustomWidget/Widget.tsx`, the student runner, even the admin preview iframe's own doc comment) already omits the flag — this was drift from the team's own stated policy. Rejected band-aid: sanitizing/stripping scripts from `activeApp.html` — would break the legitimate, documented use of `allow-scripts`. Fixed by removing the one flag + correcting the now-inaccurate `MiniAppManager.tsx` doc comment. 1 new test; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`expected [...] to not include 'allow-same-origin'`) → pass-after (1/1), plus full validate (640/7514, 41/823) + build. | [#2553](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2553) |

| 2026-08-26 | Build & Tooling | `functions/src/studentIdentity.ts`'s `pinLoginV1` callable (PIN→SSO bridge for student quiz join) fetched only the first 5 `quiz_sessions` docs matching a reused join code (`.limit(5)`) and filtered for joinable status client-side — since `quiz_sessions` docs are never deleted, a code reused across a school year could accumulate 5+ old non-joinable sessions ahead of the live one in Firestore's default result order, causing `pinLoginV1` to wrongly report `no-joinable-session` and block a legitimate student login. Confirmed as a real inconsistency, not a hypothetical: sibling lookups (`useQuizSession.ts`'s `lookupSession`, `functions/src/lti/nrpsStore.ts`'s `persistLtiLaunchContext`) both correctly fetch all matches and filter for joinable status. Fixed by moving the status filter into the query itself (`.where('status','in',[...]).limit(1)`), backed by an already-existing composite index — no index changes needed. `.limit(1)` is safe because `allocateJoinCode()` (client-side) already guarantees at most one joinable session per code at creation time (verified directly by the orchestrator, not just the subagent's claim). Rejected band-aid: raising `.limit(5)` to a bigger number — reduces odds without eliminating the bug, and still wastes a full fetch of dead history on every login. 1 new regression test (verified the mock Firestore harness genuinely implements the `'in'` operator, not an inert stub); orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`no-joinable-session`, 1 failed/48 passed) → pass-after (49/49) via file-swap against `dev-paul`, plus a full validate (651/7563 root, 41/825 functions) + `build:all`. No comment trim needed (single-line comment, within convention). | [#2566](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2566) |

| 2026-08-28 | Widgets | `ClockWidget` (`components/widgets/ClockWidget/Widget.tsx`): unconditional `setInterval(..., 1000)` ticked every second even with `showSeconds` off, when nothing visible changes between minute boundaries — 59 of every 60 re-renders wasted on an always-on classroom display. Fixed by branching the effect on `showSeconds`: 1s ticks unchanged when true; when false, ticks realign to the minute boundary instead (mechanism evolved across the repairs below — see final state). Rejected band-aid: a `React.memo`/render-guard would still fire the timer/setState every second, only skipping the DOM diff, not the root cause. 2 new perf-regression tests (one-second-at-a-time `act()` calls, not a single `advanceTimersByTime(60_000)` — React 18 batching would collapse 60 ticks into one commit and hide the bug); orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (2 failed/19 passed) → pass-after (19/19) via file-swap against `dev-paul`, trimmed a 3-line comment to one line (file had no pre-existing comment convention to justify it), plus full validate + build, re-run after rebasing onto latest `dev-paul`. **Post-push repair 1**: a separate automated PR-babysitting session concurrently pushed a harmless, functionally no-op cleanup (`2d1114c`, dropping the now-genuinely-unused `React` default import from a review nit the orchestrator had independently confirmed was a false positive and already replied to — both were correct that it was harmless), adopted with no conflict. **Post-push repair 2**: a real bug — the seconds-hidden branch never resynced `time` when the effect (re)started, so toggling "show seconds" off shortly after a minute rollover left the previous minute on screen for up to 60s before jumping straight to the next one, reachable via the live Settings toggle. Reproduced directly (3/20 failed) before fixing with the adjust-state-while-rendering pattern (a `prevShowSecondsRef` + `react-hooks/refs` disable comments, since a literal synchronous `setTime()` in the effect trips `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`), committed `6aebffd`. **Two further concurrent commits from a separate session, discovered when a later review on the log PR itself (#2626) caught this row describing stale code**: `6c44fd4` replaced the free-running `setInterval(60_000)` with a self-rescheduling `setTimeout` chain that re-derives its delay from `Date.now()` on every tick (a bare interval drifts under background-tab throttling on an always-on display; the review that shipped #2621 had flagged this as a non-blocking polish note, and a separate session acted on it), with a new test asserting `setInterval` is never called and each tick re-schedules from the wall clock. `d06a398` then refactored the `prevShowSecondsRef` render-sync from a ref to a `useState` pair, matching CLAUDE.md's adjust-state-while-rendering guidance more literally (mutating a ref during render is impure under StrictMode's double-invoke; a state setter is naturally idempotent) and dropping both `react-hooks/refs` suppressions. Final shipped mechanism: self-rescheduling wall-clock-relative `setTimeout` for the tick, `useState`-based previous-value comparison for the toggle resync. | [#2621](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2621) |

| 2026-08-28 | State & Data | `hooks/useLiveSession.ts::startSession` generated its join `code` via unseeded `Math.random()` with no uniqueness check against active sessions, unlike the quiz path's `allocateJoinCode()`. Two teachers starting a session around the same time had a nonzero chance of colliding codes; `joinSession`'s lookup also took `querySnapshot.docs[0]` with no `isActive` filter, so a collision (or a stale/ended session sharing an old code) could silently route a student into the wrong teacher's session — a real cross-account data leak. Fixed by porting `allocateJoinCode()`'s exact pattern as `allocateSessionCode()` (probe + retry + last-resort fallback) and adding an `isActive` filter to `joinSession`'s match selection. Rejected band-aid: deduping `.docs[0]` selection alone would reduce but not eliminate the leak and could nondeterministically flip which teacher a student lands on. Swept `hooks/`/`utils/` for sibling unseeded code generators — found only the already-correct quiz path and a dead `generateSecureSessionCode()` helper. 4 new tests; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (4 failed/11 passed) → pass-after (15/15) via file-swap against `dev-paul`, plus full validate + build, re-run after rebasing onto latest `dev-paul`. | [#2623](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2623) |

| 2026-08-28 | Build & Tooling | `firestore.rules`' `sessions/{userId}/students/{studentId}` — a LOW finding tracked in `docs/scheduled-tasks/firestore-rules.md` since 2026-08-24 but never actioned by a prior nightly run. `create` had no field whitelist/type checks/size caps, and `update` was completely unconstrained for teacher/admin/owning-student alike; since the parent `sessions` collection allows any authenticated user to `read`/`list` (a separate, deliberately-unfixed MEDIUM), any caller who learns a live `sessionId` could spray unbounded arbitrary-shape docs into a teacher's roster or rewrite `pin`/timestamps post-create. Traced every real client write path in `hooks/useLiveSession.ts` and gated `create` to the exact `{pin, status, joinedAt, lastActive}` shape (pin ≤`MAX_PIN_LENGTH`, status enum, ints) and `update` to `status`-only (matching all 3 real update call sites). Reproduced the regression directly (fail-before against the shipped rules: exactly 1 new test failed, 1238/1239 others unaffected) before fixing. Widened the owning-student branch of `update` to also accept a full overwrite matching `create`'s own shape constraints (factored into a shared `isValidLiveStudentPayload()` helper) rather than changing the client, since the client can legitimately submit a different `pin` on rejoin. 3 new tests plus one pre-existing test updated (a student rewriting their own pin via update is now intentional — `studentId == auth.uid` already scopes it to their own record, so it isn't a new privilege). This project's Auth accepts email/password sign-in in addition to Google, and Identity Toolkit's public `accounts:signUp` endpoint lets anyone self-register any email with `email_verified: false` — so a caller could fabricate an address at a real org's domain (e.g. `attacker@orono.k12.mn.us`) and get classified "internal," defeating the external cap's cost-control purpose. `studentLoginV1` already gates its equivalent domain resolution on `!emailVerified` — this was a real inconsistency against an established pattern, not a stylistic gap. Fixed with an `email_verified === true` gate in `isExternalCaller` itself; an unverified email now fails toward `true` (external) — inverting the function's normal "fail toward org" default specifically for this case, since an unverified claim is exactly what an attacker controls. 2 new regression tests plus ~15 existing token fixtures updated to include `email_verified: true` (matching real Google-issued tokens); orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (both new tests failed, `expected false to be true`) → pass-after (29/29) via file-swap against the run-53 sibling tip, plus full validate + `build:all`, re-run after rebasing onto latest `dev-paul`.
