| 2026-08-30 | Widgets | `StationsWidget.handleResetAll` (`components/widgets/Stations/Widget.tsx`) called `persistAssignments(resetAllAssignments(activeRoster.map((s) => s.id)))`, replacing the ENTIRE `config.assignments` map with one built only from `activeRoster` — which excludes any student currently marked absent. Clicking "Reset all" silently deleted every absent student's station assignment, breaking the "snap back once un-marked absent" contract that `resetStation`/`rotateAssignments`/Shuffle (#2640) already honor — the identical bug class as #2640, just a sibling call site that fix missed. Rejected band-aid: special-casing "carry forward absent entries" inside `resetAllAssignments` itself — would fix only this call site and leave the general replace-vs-merge hazard open for any future caller. Fixed by merging onto the existing `assignments` map, mirroring the already-shipped Shuffle pattern. 1 new regression test; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`expected undefined to be 'st-b'`, 1 failed/8 passed) → pass-after (9/9) via file-swap against `dev-paul`, trimmed a 3-line and a 6-line comment to one line each (CLAUDE.md), plus full validate (681/7874, 43/867) + build. | [#2650](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2650) |
| 2026-08-30 | Dashboard & Layout | `components/common/AnnotationCanvas.tsx`'s render effect unconditionally ran `canvas.width = canvasWidth; canvas.height = canvasHeight;` on every run, including runs triggered purely by `currentPath` changing on each `pointermove` mid-stroke. Per the HTML canvas spec, assigning `.width`/`.height` clears and reallocates the entire backing bitmap even at an unchanged value — since `canvasWidth`/`canvasHeight` are stable for the whole stroke, every point of every annotation stroke on any widget's ink overlay forced a full bitmap reset immediately before `draw()`'s own `ctx.clearRect` redrew everything anyway, a real perf cost on a 60-120Hz pointer-move path. Rejected band-aid: throttling/debouncing the redraw effect during a stroke — would visibly lag or drop points, a real fidelity regression instead of fixing the redundant reassignment. Fixed by comparing against `canvas.width`/`canvas.height` directly before reassigning, reassigning only on an actual change. 1 new regression test (spies on the canvas element's `width`/`height` setters across a pointerdown+3×pointermove sequence); orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (width setter called 4×, 1 failed/7 passed) → pass-after (8/8) via file-swap against `dev-paul`, trimmed a 12-line module comment plus two smaller ones to one line each, plus full validate (681/7874, 43/867) + build. **Post-push repair**: automated review pointed out `DrawingWidget/useDrawingCanvas.ts:373-375` already solves the identical problem by comparing against the live `canvas.width`/`canvas.height` DOM properties instead of tracking a parallel `appliedDimsRef` (the fix's original shape) — simpler, self-correcting if the backing size ever drifts from what a ref believes was last applied, and now leaves one idiom in the codebase instead of two. Adopted the direct-comparison version and deleted the ref entirely (`f71d547`); a second review round then flagged that only the negative case (no reassignment on unchanged dims) was tested — added a companion case asserting a genuine `canvasWidth`/`canvasHeight` change still triggers the setter (`13d760a`), confirmed meaningful by temporarily inverting the comparison and watching it fail. Final shipped mechanism has no ref at all; the earlier `appliedDimsRef` never reached `dev-paul`. | [#2654](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2654) |
| 2026-08-30 | State & Data | `hooks/useFolders.ts::collectDescendantFolderIds` and `hooks/useCollections.ts::collectDescendantCollectionIds` (structurally identical) recursively walked a folder/collection tree with no cycle protection, unlike their sibling ancestor-check `isDescendantOrSelf` (already depth-capped "in case of corrupted data"). `moveFolder`/`moveCollection`'s cycle guard is a client-side check against a locally-cached snapshot, not a transaction — two concurrent moves from different tabs/devices (A under B, B under A) can each pass their own stale-state check and still land a genuine 2-node cycle once both writes commit. A later "delete folder" (delete-all mode) on either node then recurses forever, `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded`, permanently blocking deletion of the whole cyclic branch. Rejected band-aid: a depth cap only inside the two hook-local closures — stops the crash but silently truncates legitimate deep non-cyclic trees, and duplicates the gap across two files. Fixed by extracting a single cycle-safe `collectDescendantIds` (visited-set bounded) into a new `utils/folderTree.ts`, used by both hooks. 4 new tests (normal tree, leaf, 2-node cycle, 3-node cycle back to root); orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (unguarded version reconstructed: 2/4 failed, `RangeError`) → pass-after (4/4) plus existing `useFolders.test.ts`/`useCollections.test.ts` unaffected (12/12), trimmed a 16-line module JSDoc plus several smaller comments to one line each, full validate (682/7877, 43/867) + test:counts. Also confirmed the flagged `utils/quizStimuli.ts::projectSessionStimuli` dedupe backlog lead (open since 2026-08-27) was already fixed by #2642 (merged 2026-08-29, before tonight's base) — stale row, see Backlog. | [#2651](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2651) |
| 2026-08-30 | Admin & Config | `components/admin/SpecialistScheduleConfigurationModal.tsx` seeded a new building's default specialist-class options by comparing `selectedBuildingId` against legacy long-form building IDs (`'schumann-elementary'`, `'orono-intermediate-school'`) that were canonical before the org-buildings migration (`config/buildings.ts`'s `BUILDING_ID_ALIASES`) — `useAdminBuildings()`/`useBuildingSelection()` only ever return canonical short IDs (`'schumann'`, `'intermediate'`) post-migration, so the comparison was always false and every building silently got an empty `specialistOptions: []` ("No options added.") instead of its curated defaults. Same bug class as a dozen prior "canonicalize building IDs" fixes elsewhere; this file was simply missed. Rejected band-aid: hardcoding the new short IDs directly — would silently regress again for any org whose building doc IDs are still legacy long-form (a real path per `buildingRecordToBuilding`), and ignores the established `canonicalBuildingId()` helper. Fixed by comparing `canonicalBuildingId(selectedBuildingId)`. 1 new regression test; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`getByText('🎵 Music')` throws) → pass-after (1/1) via file-swap against `dev-paul`, trimmed a 5-line and a 4-line comment to one line each, plus full validate (682/7874, 43/867) + build. **Post-push repair**: automated review noted the original test only exercised canonical short IDs — the exact case a rejected hardcoded-comparison band-aid would also have passed — so it didn't actually pin the reason `canonicalBuildingId()` was chosen. Added legacy-long-form-ID cases for both buildings plus an Intermediate-branch case and a negative case (5 tests total); confirmed the legacy-ID cases are load-bearing by temporarily replacing `canonicalBuildingId(selectedBuildingId)` with the raw id and watching exactly those 2 fail (`9a31e98`). Also applied the review's minor nit: `canonicalBuildingId()` computed once into a local instead of twice in the same expression. **Second post-push repair**: the same review round flagged that canonicalization was only applied to the `specialistOptions` seeding choice — the `buildingDefaults` lookup and the `updateBuilding` write still indexed by the raw, non-canonical `selectedBuildingId`, the identical bug class one level up in the same file. Fixed by applying `canonicalizeBuildingKeyedRecord()` to the read and write paths, the same helper already used for this exact shape in `components/widgets/ActivityWall/buildingDefaults.ts`. 2 new tests exercising the real Firestore load/save paths (`isAuthBypass` flipped off, `getDoc`/`setDoc` mocked, since every prior test in the file short-circuited that path) — orchestrator independently re-verified each side of the fix fails in isolation when reverted (`139f399`), plus full lint/tsc/format clean and the broader `components/admin/` suite (37/142) unaffected. | [#2652](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2652) |
| 2026-08-30 | Build & Tooling | `firestore.rules`' `/artifacts/{appId}/users/{userId}/starterPacks/{packId}` (owner read/write) was missing the `notDeactivated()` gate that ~18 sibling owner-scoped collections carry as part of the M1 deactivation-lockout invariant — a LOW finding tracked in `docs/scheduled-tasks/firestore-rules.md` since 2026-08-24, never actioned. The lockout is enforced by an async `onSnapshot` listener in `context/AuthContext.tsx` that signs a deactivated member out on the *next* snapshot delivery, not synchronously — a deactivated member holding a still-valid ID token (in-flight write, stale tab, cached auth, direct SDK/REST call) could keep writing/reading personal starter packs after deactivation. Rejected band-aid: patching only the client (pre-emptively disabling Save, or making sign-out synchronous) — doesn't close the actual security boundary; rules are the enforcement layer here, same as every sibling collection. Fixed by adding `&& notDeactivated()`, matching the sibling pattern; left `/users/{userId}` and `/users/{userId}/userProfile/{profileId}` alone per the tracking doc's own bootstrap-path-exemption analysis, adding one-line documenting comments so future audits stop re-flagging them. 4 new tests extending `tests/rules/inactiveMemberLockout.test.ts`; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (2/19 failed) → pass-after (19/19) against the live emulator via file-swap against `dev-paul`, plus full validate (681/7873 unchanged) + `build:all`. Full `test:rules` (52/1247) showed 2 unrelated failures (`firestore-rules-external-user.test.ts`, `firestore-rules-organizations.test.ts`) that reproduced identically — different specific tests each run — against **unmodified `dev-paul` rules in isolation**, confirming pre-existing emulator flakiness unrelated to this change (see Notes & Gotchas — this is a stronger/different confirmation than the previously-documented "5+ concurrent worktrees" contention theory). Moved the corresponding item fully into Completed in `docs/scheduled-tasks/firestore-rules.md` (starterPacks branch resolved; two `/users/` branches intentionally documented-exempt). **Post-push repair**: automated review flagged that every test used `getDoc` (single-doc), but the real client (`hooks/useStarterPacks.ts`) subscribes with `onSnapshot(query(collection(...)))` — a list, which Firestore evaluates separately from a get, and the exact shape of last week's #2625 regression (tests exercised a different SDK call than the client). Added active-succeeds/inactive-denied `getDocs` cases against the real collection path; confirmed genuinely uncovered by reverting to `dev-paul`'s rule and reproducing 3 failures (the 2 pre-existing doc-level denials plus the new list-level one). Also pinned the documented `/users/{userId}`/`userProfile` exemption with 2 read assertions per the same review's smaller gap. 23/23 tests pass with the fix restored (`62f3878`). | [#2653](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2653) |

| 2026-08-31 | Widgets | `components/widgets/RecessGear/Widget.tsx` computed its effective "use feels-like temperature" flag as `adminConfig ? adminConfig.useFeelsLike : config.useFeelsLike` in two places — testing whether an `adminConfig` object exists at all, not whether the admin actually set `useFeelsLike` on it. Once an admin saves ANY RecessGear config (e.g. custom `temperatureRanges`, unrelated to this toggle), `adminConfig` becomes truthy for every teacher's widget; `adminConfig.useFeelsLike` (usually `undefined`) then wins the ternary and falls through `?? true`, permanently overriding a teacher's explicit `false` choice with no indication the toggle is dead. Confirmed live-reachable: `RecessGearSettings.tsx` renders the toggle unconditionally, and the admin config path is a normal `FeaturePermissionsManager` save flow. Rejected band-aid: hiding/disabling the teacher's toggle whenever `adminConfig` exists — doesn't fix the underlying semantics and makes the control disappear based on unrelated admin actions. Fixed with one shared `resolvedUseFeelsLike = adminConfig?.useFeelsLike ?? config.useFeelsLike ?? true`, so the admin value only wins when explicitly a boolean. 1 new regression test; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`1 failed \| 7 passed`, asserted "Long Sleeves" but DOM showed "Heavy Coat") → pass-after (8/8) via file-swap against `dev-paul`, plus full validate (686/7901, 43/867) + build. Plain `git commit` succeeded, no signing workaround needed. **Post-push follow-up**: automated review requested a test pinning the admin-wins direction (admin-set `true` overrides a teacher's `false`) — added in `c2a01c8`; passes on both old and new code (coverage-completeness, not a regression test, since the old ternary already gave admin priority when explicitly set). | 2026-08-31 | Dashboard & Layout | `components/common/GroupBoundingBox.tsx`'s group-selection corner-handle resize computed a uniform scale from raw pointer delta with only a minimum-size floor — no upper bound — unlike every other move/resize path in this area (`DraggableWindow`'s single-widget drag/resize, and this same file's own group-**drag** path), which all clamp against `getWorldBounds()`/`clampWidgetToWorld` from `utils/zoomPanMath.ts`. An aggressive corner drag could scale a grouped selection's far edge past the world bounds the camera can ever pan back to. Confirmed live-reachable: `GroupBoundingBox` is rendered by `BoardCanvas.tsx`'s `GroupBoundingBoxLayer` whenever a grouped widget is selected — a normal user feature, not dead code. Rejected band-aid: clamping each widget's `x/y/w/h` independently post-scale (like single-widget resize does) — would break the group's uniform-scale invariant, distorting relative sizes/positions within the group. Fixed with `computeMaxGroupScale()`, mirroring `clampWidgetToWorld`'s per-widget clamp but for a uniform scale anchored at one bbox corner, applied to both the live-drag frame and the committed value (same formula, so committed size matches the last drag frame exactly). 1 new regression test; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`expected 100200 to be less than or equal to 1536.01`) → pass-after (4/4, including the 3 pre-existing geometric-mean/proportional-drag/NaN-guard tests) via file-swap against `dev-paul`, plus full validate (686/7902, 43/867) + build. New comments matched this file's own pre-existing multi-line-comment convention — no trim needed. **Post-push repair**: automated review caught a real, orchestrator-missed correctness gap — the clamp order `Math.max(minScale, Math.min(maxScale, scale))` let the pre-existing `minScale` floor silently override the new `maxScale` world-bounds cap whenever `minScale > maxScale`, reintroducing the exact bug this PR fixes (concretely reachable for any group containing a `url` widget, whose real 80×80 min size via `WIDGET_MIN_SIZE_OVERRIDES` is smaller than this file's hardcoded 150×100 floor, forcing `minScale` above 1 even for a tiny drag). Reproduced with a standalone repro before fixing (a 50×50 widget 55px from the world edge: minScale=3 vs. maxScale=1.1, old order committed a far corner 95px past the boundary); fixed by capping `minScale`/`minFinalScale` at `maxScale`/`maxFinalScale` first, mirroring `DraggableWindow.tsx`'s `availableW`/`availableH` clamp for the same single-widget interaction, exactly as the reviewer suggested. 1 new regression test targeting the conflict case; orchestrator re-verified fail-before (1 failed/4 passed, `100200`-style boundary violation) → pass-after (5/5) via a second file-swap, plus a full validate + build re-run before pushing, then replied to and resolved the review thread. | [#2660](https://github.com/OPS-PIvers/SpartBoard/pull/2660) |

| 2026-08-31 | Build & Tooling | **SECURITY**: `firestore.rules`' `activity_wall_sessions/{sessionId}/submissions/{submissionId}` create/update rules validated each known field individually but never called `hasOnly()` on the document's key set — any authenticated caller (incl. anonymous students, the normal writer of this path) could smuggle arbitrary extra fields onto a submission doc, e.g. a large junk string toward the 1MB doc cap, past every per-field check. Fixed with `hasOnly([...])` on both rules; `create`'s whitelist matches the real `ActivityWallStudentApp.tsx` `setDoc()` shape exactly, `update`'s whitelist is the full superset including Admin-SDK-only archive fields (`driveFileId`/`archiveError`/`archivedAt`/`archiveStartedAt` from `functions/src/driveArchive.ts`), since `request.resource.data` on an `update` is the doc AFTER merge — a teacher's later unrelated client update must still pass fields it didn't touch. 4 new emulator tests; orchestrator independently re-verified fail-before (`2 failed \| 1278 passed`) → pass-after (53 files/1280 tests) against the real Firestore emulator via file-swap against `dev-paul`, plus full validate (686/7901, 43/867) + `build:all`. Added in `842a831`; verified load-bearing by temporarily narrowing the whitelist and confirming only the new test fails. | (process) | **Container restarts can kill dispatched subagents mid-run without losing their file-level work**: on 2026-07-15 the sandbox container restarted partway through Phase 1, killing all 5 dispatched subagents and their in-flight `pnpm run validate`/build background processes. Every worktree's uncommitted changes (the actual code fix + regression test) survived on disk untouched — only the processes died, not the git worktrees. Orchestrator recovered by treating each worktree's `git diff`/untracked files as the subagent's finished-but-unreported work: reviewed each diff directly, fixed a handful of lint/format issues the interrupted agents hadn't reached, then ran validate/build itself for all 5 areas. None needed redispatch. Lesson: after any interruption mid-run, check every worktree's `git status`/`git diff` before assuming an area needs to be redone — a dead subagent process does not mean lost work. | 2026-07-15 | open (standing operational note) |

| Widgets | **New sub-variant of the recurring Escape bug class: the `document`-listener fix does not work for popovers rendered INSIDE a `DraggableWindow`.** `DraggableWindow.handleKeyDown` (`onKeyDown` on `GlassCard`, line ~2143) calls synthetic `e.stopPropagation()` on Escape for a focused non-input target (line ~931), which halts the native event at the React root — so a descendant's `document.addEventListener('keydown')` never fires at all. Empirically confirmed 2026-08-24 via RTL (ancestor minimize fired, document handler 0 calls, popover stayed open) and fixed for `HotspotImage/Widget.tsx` in #2544 by switching to a React `onKeyDown` on the widget's own content div, which wins the synthetic bubble. **Second, independent corollary found the same night: switching to a React `onKeyDown` on a descendant div is not sufficient by itself either.** `DraggableWindow.handlePointerDown` (line ~782) calls `(e.currentTarget as HTMLElement).focus()` on the GlassCard root on every pointerdown inside the widget (unless the target is contenteditable) — so a mouse click on an interactive element (e.g. a hotspot pin) moves DOM focus to the ancestor *before* the click's `onClick` runs. Since keydown only bubbles from the focused/target element up to its ancestors (never down into a descendant's handler), a widget-internal `onKeyDown` on a div below the pin is unreachable for the mouse-click path — only a pure-keyboard Tab+Enter open (no intervening pointerdown) avoids the focus steal. Fixed for `HotspotImage/Widget.tsx` in the same PR (#2544) by having the click handler reclaim focus onto the clicked element (`(e.currentTarget as HTMLElement).focus()`) so keydown's target lands back inside the widget's subtree. Empirically confirmed via RTL: a test simulating `pointerDown` then `click` (not just `click`) against an ancestor that mimics `handlePointerDown`'s focus-steal reproduced the bug (popover stayed open) before the fix, 4/4 passed after. `components/widgets/DrawingWidget/PageStrip.tsx` is structurally analogous (rendered inside a real `DraggableWindow`) and still uses the `document`-listener idiom — likely affected by BOTH gaps, not empirically verified. `CatalystSetPickerPopover.tsx` is NOT affected by either (rendered from `Dock`, no `DraggableWindow` ancestor). Re-audit every widget-internal Escape handler against both: (1) prefer React `onKeyDown` over a `document` listener inside a widget, and (2) if the Escape-relevant element is opened via a mouse click on a descendant, reclaim focus onto that descendant in the click handler, or fire `pointerDown`+`click` (not just `click`) in tests to catch the steal. **`PageStrip.tsx` confirmed affected and fixed #2563** (2026-08-26) — empirically confirmed the same focus-steal-on-pointerdown gap via an RTL test mimicking `DraggableWindow`'s real handlers, fixed with the same focus-into-popover + React `onKeyDown` pattern as `HotspotImage`. | 2026-08-24 | **fixed #2544, #2563** |

| Admin & Config | **Systemic gap, ~35 files**: `components/admin/*ConfigurationPanel.tsx`/`*ConfigurationModal.tsx` widely index `buildingDefaults[buildingId]`/`buildings[buildingId]` by the raw id from `useAdminBuildings()` without canonicalizing via `canonicalBuildingId()`/`canonicalizeBuildingKeyedRecord()` first — the same bug class fixed piecemeal so far in `SpecialistScheduleConfigurationModal.tsx` (#2652) and `GraphicOrganizerConfigurationModal.tsx` (#2662). Every affected file needs the same 3-part fix (canonicalize on read, canonicalize on write, leave the raw id alone for UI tab-selection/display-name lookups) plus its own regression test. Recommend a shared `useCanonicalBuildingDefaults`-style helper to fix all remaining call sites at once rather than continuing file-by-file — full file list surfaced via `grep -n "buildingDefaults\[" components/admin` (e.g. `TalkingToolConfigurationPanel.tsx`, `EmbedConfigurationPanel.tsx`, `QRConfigurationPanel.tsx`, `NoteConfigurationPanel.tsx`, `DrawingConfigurationPanel.tsx`, `ClockConfigurationPanel.tsx`, `CalendarConfigurationModal.tsx`, `BloomsTaxonomyConfigurationModal.tsx`, and more). Spotted 2026-08-31 (run 56). **Related, widget side**: automated review on #2662 flagged `components/widgets/GraphicOrganizer/Widget.tsx`/`Settings.tsx` also index `buildingsConfig[buildingId]` without canonicalizing — `buildingId` there is already canonical (`useWidgetBuildingId`), so this only bites legacy-keyed Firestore data saved before #2662 (won't resolve until re-saved through the admin modal or the widget-side lookup is fixed the same way). Non-blocking per the review, worth folding into the same shared-helper follow-up. | 2026-08-31 | open (systemic, needs a shared-helper fix, ~35 files) |
| Dashboard & Layout | `App.tsx`'s top-level `App` function computes `isXXXRoute` booleans from a plain `window.location.pathname` read (not via the `usePathname()` `useSyncExternalStore` hook), so it never re-renders on `SPA_NAVIGATE_EVENT`/`popstate`. Currently harmless — every `spaNavigate`/`spaReplace` call site (PLC routes) stays within the same top-level branch, and `AppContent`'s own `usePathname()` handles that reactively — but it's a latent trap if a future top-level route ever needs client-side (non-full-reload) navigation across branches. Spotted 2026-08-31 (run 56). | 2026-08-31 | open (latent, not reachable today) |
| State & Data | Three near-identical dedupe-by-id loops now exist: `dedupeQuestionsById` in `utils/quizMaxPoints.ts`, a second `dedupeQuestionsById` in `utils/videoActivityGrading.ts` (#2661), and `dedupeStepsById` in `hooks/useGuidedLearningSession.ts` (#2661) — the last is also the only one living in a hook file rather than `utils/`, which is why its test sits in `tests/hooks/` while its siblings' sit in `tests/utils/`. Flagged by automated review on #2661: a single generic `dedupeById<T extends { id: string }>(items: T[]): T[]` in `utils/` would collapse all three with no loss of type safety and give the next occurrence of this recurring bug class one place to land. Not done in #2661 (out of scope for a single-bug PR). | 2026-08-31 | open (refactor, spans 3 call sites) |
| Dashboard & Layout | `GroupBoundingBox.tsx`'s world-bounds clamp (#2660) has an edge case flagged by automated review: `getWorldBounds()` derives from `window.innerWidth`/`innerHeight`, so a group legitimately sized on a large screen can find itself partly outside bounds after a viewport shrink — the *first frame* of any subsequent resize drag then forces an immediate shrink (since `minScale` is now capped at `maxScale`) rather than doing nothing until dragged, and the degenerate end (anchor exactly on a bound) collapses the group to `scale === 0`. Neither is reachable through normal in-bounds interaction and `Math.max(0, …)` keeps it from going negative, so this is a noted trade-off, not a bug — no test added. | 2026-08-31 | open (noted trade-off, not a bug, no action needed unless reachability changes) |

## Notes & Gotchas

- `@/` path alias maps to **repo root**, not `src/` — no `src/` directory exists.
- Widget front-face content must use `cqmin` container-query units (never Tailwind `text-sm` etc.).
- `useEffect` should only sync with external systems; computed/derived values go inline.
- Audio context uses a global singleton pattern (see `TimeToolWidget.tsx`).
- `pnpm run validate` runs type-check:all → lint → format:check → test → functions test. All must pass before push.
- Node v22 works but prints "unsupported engine" warnings; CI uses Node 24.
- **Git worktrees & signing server**: The signing binary (`/tmp/code-sign`, configured as `gpg.ssh.program`) identifies the repo by the git process's working directory. Any commit from a worktree path (including `/home/user/spartboard-nightly-*`) returns HTTP 400 "missing source". **Workaround** (confirmed 2026-05-27): create `/tmp/sign-from-root.sh` containing `#!/bin/bash\ncd /home/user/SpartBoard\nexec /tmp/code-sign "$@"`, then commit with `git -c gpg.ssh.program=/tmp/sign-from-root.sh commit --no-verify`. The `--no-verify` is acceptable only after lint-staged has already passed on the same staged files.
- **ESLint from `/tmp/` paths**: Running `pnpm run lint` from a worktree under `/tmp/` produces spurious `Unsafe member access` errors (TypeScript path resolution artifact). Worktrees under `/home/user/` don't have this issue for ESLint itself, but `pnpm run lint` (full repo scan) consistently times out after >3 min. **Workaround**: lint per-directory — `pnpm exec eslint <dir> --max-warnings 0`.
- **Worktree working-directory contamination**: Subagents working in one worktree may leave unstaged changes from other areas (other agents' fixes) in a different worktree's working directory. Before committing from any worktree, always run `git status` and `git restore <foreign-files>` to discard contamination.
- **Escape handling inside a widget must use React `onKeyDown`, not a `document` listener** (learned 2026-08-24, #2544). `DraggableWindow` attaches `onKeyDown={handleKeyDown}` to `GlassCard`; on Escape with a focused non-input target it calls synthetic `e.stopPropagation()`, which also stops the underlying native event at React's root container — so any `document.addEventListener('keydown')` registered by a descendant never fires for events targeting that widget. The long-established one-line `document`-listener idiom is only valid for popovers with NO `DraggableWindow` ancestor (Dock-level, e.g. `CatalystSetPickerPopover`). Inside a widget, handle Escape with a React `onKeyDown` on the widget's own content element so it wins the synthetic bubble first. Corollary for tests: firing `keyDown` on `document` proves nothing — fire it on the focused element and wrap the component in an ancestor `onKeyDown` spy, or the regression is invisible. **Second corollary** (same PR, second review round): a React `onKeyDown` on a descendant div is still not enough on its own — `DraggableWindow.handlePointerDown` explicitly calls `.focus()` on the GlassCard root on every pointerdown (unless the target is contenteditable), so a mouse click on an interactive descendant (e.g. a popover-opening button) moves focus to the ancestor *before* `onClick` runs, and a later Escape's keydown target is then the ancestor — unreachable from a descendant's `onKeyDown` since bubbling never flows into descendants. Fix: the click handler that opens the popover must reclaim focus onto the clicked element (`(e.currentTarget as HTMLElement).focus()`). Corollary for tests: `fireEvent.click(el)` alone does not reproduce this — you must `fireEvent.pointerDown(el)` then `fireEvent.click(el)` against an ancestor that mimics the real focus-steal, or the gap stays invisible.
- **Verification pattern for orchestrator**: When copying files from a worktree to dev-paul for baseline testing, always backup the ORIGINAL dev-paul file BEFORE the copy (not from the worktree). Pattern: `cp /home/user/SpartBoard/<file> /tmp/<file>.orig` → apply worktree fix → test → `cp /tmp/<file>.orig /home/user/SpartBoard/<file>`. Using `git restore` is even safer after the test. Never use the worktree file as the backup source.
- **Stale-closure risk pattern**: Any widget that creates a `setInterval`/`setTimeout` inside a non-effect callback (e.g. a click handler) and reads props/context values inside that callback is susceptible to stale closures if props can change during the interval. The fix is to assign refs synchronously in the render body (`ref.current = value`) and read from refs inside the callback.
- **`react-hooks/refs` v7 false positives**: `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` v7.0.1 (installed; active via `recommended.rules`) includes `react-hooks/refs: "error"`. Introducing any `setState` call during render (the "adjust state while rendering" pattern) causes this rule to flag all render-body ref assignments in the same component. Silence each affected line with `// eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/refs -- intentional render-body ref sync (CLAUDE.md pattern)`. Use targeted line disables or a narrow block disable with a matching `/* eslint-enable react-hooks/refs */` immediately after — never a file-tail disable with no closing enable.
- **`GEMINI_SPECIFIC_FEATURES` maintenance**: `adminAnalyticsCompute.ts` uses this array to parse `ai_usage` doc IDs. Any new `genType` added to `index.ts` that writes per-feature docs must also be added here, or analytics will silently misparse the doc ID and drop the feature's data. The 2026-06-03 memory doc note claiming `video-activity-recommend` was already in the array was incorrect — audit notes in memory doc are not a substitute for reading the actual source.
- **Double-handler guard pattern**: When a component registers both a React synthetic event handler and a native window/document listener for the same event (e.g. `onPointerUp` + `window.addEventListener('pointerup')`), event bubbling means both can fire for the same event. Guard with a `committedRef = useRef(false)` reset on the initiating event — first handler to fire sets it `true` synchronously; second handler checks and returns early.
- **Email domain validation**: `str.indexOf('.', atIdx)` searches starting at `atIdx` itself. If `atIdx` is the `@` position and you need a dot _after_ at least one domain character, use `str.indexOf('.', atIdx + 2)`.
- **`data-widget-id` attribute lookup**: This attribute lives on the `.widget` root element (GlassCard). Any keyboard/pointer handler that needs the focused widget's ID must call `element.closest<HTMLElement>('.widget')?.getAttribute('data-widget-id')` — calling `getAttribute` on `document.activeElement` directly always returns null when a child element is focused.
- **`isInput`/`isTypingField` guard must include `'SELECT'`**: Any keyboard handler that needs to pass through native browser interaction (dropdown dismiss, etc.) must include `'SELECT'` in the tag-name check alongside `'INPUT'` and `'TEXTAREA'`. Omitting it causes global shortcuts (Escape → minimize, Delete → confirm-delete) to fire while a native `<select>` dropdown is open. Pattern: `['INPUT', 'TEXTAREA', 'SELECT'].includes(el?.tagName || '') || el?.isContentEditable`.
- **`vi.mock` path resolution in test files**: `vi.mock('./foo')` resolves relative to the test file's directory, not the source file being tested. If the module being tested imports `@/hooks/foo`, the mock must use `vi.mock('@/hooks/foo', ...)` — not `vi.mock('./foo', ...)`. Using the wrong path silently makes the mock inactive (no error thrown); tests may still pass if the real implementation is safe to run in the test environment, but the mock intentions are unfulfilled.
- **Spurious `useEffect` deps pattern**: If a function is listed in a `useEffect` deps array but never called _inside_ the effect body (only in JSX click handlers or other event handlers), it is a spurious dep. If that function's identity changes frequently (e.g. wrapped in `useCallback` that depends on auth state), it will tear down and re-register the effect on every change — classic for paste/keydown listeners. Audit: grep the effect body text for the dep name; if absent, remove it from the array and add a comment explaining why it's intentionally omitted.
- **`CalculatorTool.pressDecimal` pattern**: When a calculator function builds both a display string and a parallel expression string, the expression must be seeded from `newDisplay` when expression is empty (not by appending directly). Appending `'.'` to `""` gives `"."` not `"0."`. The general fix pattern (also used by `pressDigit`): when `prev.expression === ''`, seed with `newDisplay`; otherwise replace the display-length tail of the expression.
- **Testability of unexported hook-internal functions**: When a pure function lives inside a hook (unexported) and needs a real regression test, extract it to the nearest `utils/` file and export it. Inline `normalizeBuggy`/`normalizeFixed` stubs in tests are self-referential — they pass before AND after the production fix and provide no regression protection. Confirmed pattern 2026-06-08: `normalizeSession` → extracted to `utils/videoActivityNormalize.ts` as `normalizeVideoActivitySession` (#1902).
- **LTI null-clobber pattern**: `args.field ?? null` in a Firestore write overwrites a stored value when the field is absent from the launch payload (e.g. privacy-configured relaunches). Prefer `args.field ?? storedDoc.field ?? null` — the seen-section inventory in `nrpsStore.ts` already applied this correctly; the context membership doc path missed it (#1903).
- **`Alt+Arrow` keyboard shortcut guard**: `Alt+ArrowLeft`/`Right` is the standard word-navigation shortcut in text inputs on macOS and Linux. Any global keyboard handler binding these shortcuts must apply the `isTypingField` guard before `e.preventDefault()`. Adding the guard without also moving `preventDefault` inside the guard is a band-aid — the cursor still stalls even if the action doesn't fire.
- **Shallow-copy queue mutation pattern**: `[...queue]` only copies the array shell — element objects are shared references. Mutating `updated[i].status = 'x'` also mutates `queue[i]`. React's state closure captures the pre-render queue reference; a rapid double-click sees the already-mutated state and advances by two instead of one. Fix: `queue.map(item => ({ ...item, newField: value }))` produces a new array with new item objects. Confirmed 2026-06-09: `NextUpWidget.handleNextStudent` (#1914).
- **Normalizer destructuring + `...restData` pattern**: When a hook's `normalizeSession` uses a hand-enumerated literal return, it silently drops every optional field not listed. The correct fix is to destructure ONLY the fields that need explicit normalization logic (guards, defaults, type checks), assign `...restData` for everything else, and spread `restData` first in the return object so explicit fields take precedence. This preserves all future optional fields automatically. Do NOT use a flat `...data` spread — that bypasses the normalization guards entirely. Confirmed 2026-06-09: `normalizeMiniAppSession` (#1912); same pattern as `normalizeVideoActivitySession` (#1902).
- **`window.paste` guard must include `'SELECT'` + `?.` on `e.target`**: Window-level paste event handlers must guard on `SELECT` (in addition to `INPUT`/`TEXTAREA`/`isContentEditable`) to prevent accidental widget insertion when a native dropdown is focused. Also apply `?.` optional chaining when accessing `e.target` properties — synthetic events and detached elements can produce a null target. Pattern: `const target = e.target as HTMLElement | null; if (target?.tagName === 'INPUT' || ... || target?.tagName === 'SELECT' || target?.isContentEditable) return;`. Confirmed 2026-06-09: `Dock.handlePaste` (#1913).
- **`progress === 0` vs phase sentinel**: In animation loops where `progress` resets to 0 at every phase boundary, using `progress === 0` as a quiescent-state guard fires at every transition, not just the initial state. Use a dedicated sentinel value (`phase === 'ready'`, `status === 'idle'`, etc.) for guards that should only fire in the true initial state. Confirmed 2026-06-11: `BreathingWidget` Reset disabled logic (#1934).
- **SSRF redirect guard**: Any `axios` call that precedes a blocklist check must include `maxRedirects: 0`. Without it, axios follows up to 5 redirects by default — a public host can 302 to a private/internal IP and bypass the blocklist entirely. Pattern already applied to `fetchExternalProxy`; extended to `checkUrlCompatibility` in #1937. Any future Cloud Function that fetches a user-supplied URL must include this flag.
- **Verbatim-EN placeholder in i18n locales**: A translation key can be "present" in a locale file but still serve as an untranslated placeholder if its value was copied from the EN source string. `i18next.defaultValue` triggers only when the key is absent — a wrong stored value renders verbatim. Test assertion pattern: `expect(locale.ns.key).not.toBe(en.ns.key)` (not just `toHaveProperty`). Confirmed 2026-06-11: `boardBreadcrumb.openManager` and `collectionSwitcher.title` in DE/ES/FR (#1936).
- **FP noise in Firestore persistence**: When a computed value passed to Firestore is the result of floating-point arithmetic (e.g. `min + i * step`), round before storing with `parseFloat(value.toFixed(N))` where N preserves the needed decimal precision. Rounding at display-time only masks the bug — the dirty value is still stored and rendered verbatim in settings panels. Confirmed 2026-06-12: `NumberLine/Widget.tsx` `addMarker()` (#1947).
- **Partial-credit `isCorrect` invariant**: In partial-credit grading (`isCorrect`, `pointsEarned`, `pointsMax` triple), `isCorrect` must be derived as `pointsEarned >= pointsMax` — never from a stricter formula (e.g. `givenPairs.length === total`). Mixing a strict correctness formula with a lenient scoring formula produces `isCorrect=false` when `pointsEarned=pointsMax`, breaking scoreboard and streak logic. Confirmed 2026-06-12: `useQuizSession.ts` `gradeAnswer()` matching partial-credit path (#1950).
- **`e.key` case-sensitivity for keyboard shortcuts**: `e.key` is case-sensitive. With CapsLock active, letter keys produce uppercase (`'P'` not `'p'`). Any global keyboard handler that compares `e.key === 'x'` must use `e.key.toLowerCase() === 'x'` instead. The widget-level `DraggableWindow` handler already used toLowerCase; the global `DashboardView` path missed it for Alt+P (fixed #1956). Confirm all shortcut comparisons normalise to lowercase.
- **`.toISOString()` timezone pitfall for local-date comparisons**: `new Date(localMidnightTimestamp).toISOString().split('T')[0]` shifts to UTC before formatting. For users in UTC+ timezones, local midnight converts to the previous day in UTC (e.g. 2026-06-13 00:00:00 UTC+12 becomes 2026-06-12T12:00:00Z). Use local time methods: `${d.getFullYear()}-${String(d.getMonth()+1).padStart(2,'0')}-${String(d.getDate()).padStart(2,'0')}`. Confirmed 2026-06-13: `CalendarWidget.isBlocked` (#1955).
- **`normalizeActivityWallLibraryEntry` pattern**: Same hand-enumerated literal return as #1902/#1912. Extracts to `utils/activityWallNormalize.ts` with `...restData` spread (fixed #1960). Both remaining audit targets (`useQuizSession.ts` and `useGuidedLearningSession.ts`) confirmed clean 2026-06-14 — neither has a hand-enumerated normalizer.
- **Stale `onBlur` on unmounting controlled input**: When a state setter unmounts a focused input (e.g. `setIsEditingTitle(false)`), React batches the update and the input fires a synchronous `blur` event during unmount — before the new state is committed. The `onBlur` callback at that moment is the stale one from the previous render, which closes over old state values. Guard with an `isCancellingRef = useRef(false)` set synchronously before the state setter; check at the top of the save handler and short-circuit if true. Confirmed 2026-06-14: `DraggableWindow` custom title Escape path (#1965).
- **Verbatim-EN collection UI placeholders**: When the Collections / Boards Modal feature was shipped, DE/ES/FR locale files received copy-pasted English source strings as placeholders. These are "present" keys that never trigger the `defaultValue` fallback — non-EN teachers see English silently. Pattern: search for blocks of locale keys where `de.value === en.value` across an entire namespace, not just individual keys. Fixed 59 such keys in #1964. A second pass fixed 10 more `shareCollection` keys in #1977 — namespaces can accumulate new verbatim-EN values across feature branches even after an initial fix.
- **Stale-closure regression test pattern for Escape-cancel**: Subagent regression tests for the stale `onBlur`/Escape bug must use `act(() => { fireEvent.keyDown(input, { key: 'Escape' }); fireEvent.blur(input); })` — wrapping BOTH events inside the same `act()` call. jsdom does NOT fire `blur` automatically when a DOM node is removed; without the manual `blur`, the test passes on BOTH original and fixed code (the race is never reproduced). The single `act()` defers React's state flush until after both events have fired, replicating the browser's synchronous blur-during-unmount sequence. Confirmed required in 2026-06-15 orchestrator review of #1974 and #1975 subagent tests — both agents wrote bare `fireEvent.keyDown(Escape)` without the blur, producing invalid tests that passed before the fix.
- **`proportionsValid` must include value-range checks, not just `isFinite`**: Any guard that validates whether layout proportion fields (`xProp`, `yProp`, `wProp`, `hProp`) are already in proportional form must check BOTH `Number.isFinite(v)` AND `Math.abs(v) <= 1.5` (or appropriate bounds). Pixel values like `300` pass `isFinite` — a partial-migration widget with pixel `xProp`/`yProp` but valid proportional `wProp`/`hProp` would satisfy an `isFinite`-only guard and skip migration, leaving position fields as raw pixels. Confirmed 2026-06-15: `migrateWidgetToProportional` (#1976).

- **`vi.mock()` stubs for modules with module-level side effects**: If a source file executes code at import time (e.g. `onDocumentWritten(...)`, `setGlobalOptions(...)`, `initializeApp()`), any test file that imports it (directly or transitively) must provide `vi.mock()` stubs for all side-effecting modules BEFORE the import. Without stubs, Vitest crashes with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` or throws during module initialisation — silently dropping all tests in the file (no failure, just omission). The fix mirrors the sibling test files that already use the correct hoisting pattern. Confirmed 2026-06-22: `mirrorPlcIndex.test.ts` (#2047).
- **`toISOString()` launch-key scope**: Any key computed as `new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)` for scheduling/launch deduplication will use the UTC date, diverging from local midnight for UTC+ users. Use `getTodayStr()` (which wraps `getFullYear/getMonth/getDate`) for any "today" marker that governs teacher-facing scheduling logic. Confirmed 2026-06-22: `ScheduleWidget.checkAutoLaunch` (#2046).
- **Enter double-commit on title rename**: Same class as the Escape-cancel stale-onBlur pattern, but triggered by the commit path. Pressing Enter calls `saveTitle()` directly, which schedules `setIsEditingTitle(false)`. Before React commits the unmount, the browser fires a synchronous `blur` — calling `saveTitle()` a second time via `onBlur`. `isConnected` does NOT work here (node is still connected at blur time). Fix: `hasCommittedTitleRef = useRef(false)` guard, set to `true` on first commit, reset in the render body while editing is active (`if (isEditingTitle) hasCommittedTitleRef.current = false`). React 18 automatic batching ensures `updateWidget`'s state update and `setIsEditingTitle(false)` commit in a single render cycle — no intermediate render resets the guard before blur fires. Confirmed 2026-06-22: `DraggableWindow` (#2045).
- **CSS source-order wins cascade over `@tailwind base` (equal-specificity rules)**: A manual `@media` block placed AFTER `@tailwind base` in `index.css` wins the cascade over Tailwind-generated rules of equal specificity — CSS source order is the tie-breaker when specificity is the same. The `tailwind.config.js` `reducedMotionPlugin` injected into `@tailwind base` does NOT override a later `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block in the same file, because both produce identically-specific `@media { .class { animation: none !important } }` declarations. When hand-editing `index.css` reduced-motion blocks, verify the intended set of suppressed classes matches what `tailwind.config.js` defines as non-essential. Confirmed 2026-06-24: `.animate-spin` frozen for reduced-motion users (#2068).
- **WCAG 2.3.3 — essential animations must be preserved under reduced-motion**: Loading spinners (`animate-spin`, `Loader2`, etc.) are essential animations: freezing them removes the only in-progress affordance during async operations, which reads as "stalled" to the user. Under WCAG SC 2.3.3, animations that communicate necessary state information (loading, progress) must NOT be suppressed by `prefers-reduced-motion` rules. Only decorative/looping animations (slow spins, jitter, pulse, bounce) should be suppressed. `tailwind.config.js`'s `reducedMotionPlugin` intentionally excludes `animate-spin` from the suppression list; any `index.css` manual block must mirror this exclusion. Confirmed 2026-06-24: (#2068).
- **`quizDriveService` stats-block dedup pattern**: `exportResultsToSheet` and `buildResultsSheetDataShared` are two separate code paths that each do their own dedup. Drive-sync/arrayUnion races can write duplicate question IDs; both paths must apply a `Set<string>` fence before iterating `questions`. The response-level stats loops (`answeredSet`/`correctSet`) only dedup per-student responses — they do not protect against duplicate question rows in the output. Dedup must happen at the `questions` level before any loop that emits one row per question. Confirmed 2026-06-24: `exportResultsToSheet` stats block (#2065).

- **jsdom programmatic blur is always a no-op in tests**: `e.currentTarget.blur()` called inside a `keyDown` handler does NOT dispatch a real `blur` event in jsdom — it is silently ignored regardless of whether the element was focused via `fireEvent.focus()`. Only `fireEvent.blur(input)` triggers the `onBlur` handler. This means: (a) Escape-cancel tests must include `fireEvent.blur` inside `act()` alongside `fireEvent.keyDown`; (b) Enter-commit tests where the handler calls `e.currentTarget.blur()` as the commit mechanism ALSO require `fireEvent.blur` — without it, `onSave` is never called and `toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1)` fails with 0 calls. The claim that `fireEvent.focus()` "makes programmatic blur fire in jsdom" is FALSE — CI failures prove it. Confirmed 2026-06-24: `OptionInput.test.tsx` Enter test (#2064).
- **`import.meta.url` is not a `file:` URL in CI — use `__dirname` + `resolve` in Vitest tests**: `fileURLToPath(new URL('../file', import.meta.url))` throws `TypeError: The URL must be of scheme file` in CI because the Vitest runner presents `import.meta.url` with a non-file scheme. The Vitest `__dirname` shim (available even with `"type": "module"` in package.json) works reliably in both local and CI environments. Always use `resolve(__dirname, '../path')` for file path construction in Vitest test files. Confirmed 2026-06-24: `tests/indexCss.reducedMotion.test.ts` (#2068).
- **tsc vs ESLint type-inference disagreement on `screen.getByLabelText()` casts**: TypeScript strict mode (`tsc --noEmit`) types `screen.getByLabelText()` as `HTMLElement` — accessing `.value` on it is a type error. ESLint's TypeScript language service infers `HTMLInputElement` from the DOM context and flags `as HTMLInputElement` as `@typescript-eslint/no-unnecessary-type-assertion`. Resolution: keep the `as HTMLInputElement` cast (required for tsc) AND add `// eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-unnecessary-type-assertion` on the preceding line. Both lines are required; removing either breaks one checker. Confirmed 2026-06-27: `NumberLine/Settings.test.tsx` (#2092).
- **`react-refresh/only-export-components` and re-exports**: A component file that re-exports a non-component value (even via `export { X } from './other'`) still violates the rule — the file now has mixed exports. The correct pattern is: extract the constant to its own module, import it in the component file for internal use (no re-export), and import it directly from its own module in test files. A re-export intermediate is not a valid workaround. Confirmed 2026-06-27: `AnalyticsManager.tsx` / `aiFeatureLabels.ts` (#2094).
- **`e.nativeEvent.stopImmediatePropagation()` must be called BEFORE `target.blur()`**: React's `e.stopPropagation()` only stops propagation through React's synthetic event system; native `window.addEventListener` listeners still fire. When an Escape handler must both stop native event propagation AND call `element.blur()`, call `e.nativeEvent.stopImmediatePropagation()` FIRST — `blur()` is synchronous and moves focus immediately. After `blur()`, any native listener that checks `isTypingFieldActive()` (which tests `document.activeElement`) will see the post-blur state (nothing focused) and misfire. Order matters: stop propagation, then blur. Confirmed 2026-06-28: `DraggableWindow.handleKeyDown` Escape-in-input branch (#2101).
- **`useEffect(() => { ref.current = value }, [value])` anti-pattern — stale-ref window**: `useEffect` is a passive effect — it runs asynchronously after the browser paints (via `setImmediate`/`MessageChannel`). Any `setTimeout`/`setInterval` callback that fires in the window between a synchronous re-render (e.g. `flushSync`) and the passive effect flush will read the stale `ref.current`. The fix is a synchronous render-body assignment `ref.current = value` (with `// eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/refs -- intentional render-body ref sync (CLAUDE.md pattern)`). This is guaranteed to be up-to-date by the time any timer callback fires in the same task. Confirmed 2026-06-28: `GraphicOrganizer/EditableNode.onUpdateRef` (#2102).
- **Sibling permission-gate consistency**: any code path that renders a list of user-facing items alongside a top-level gate (e.g. `Dock.tsx`'s `if (!tool || !canAccessTool(tool.type)) return null;`) needs the SAME gate applied wherever else that data appears — a nested container (Dock folders) that re-lists the same items via a different render path (`TOOLS.find` with no permission check) is easy to miss and silently bypasses the gate. Confirmed 2026-07-04: `FolderItem.tsx` (#2136).
- **`Array.prototype.some()` on an empty array is always `false`**: a visibility guard shaped `!isEditMode && !items.some(canAccessTool)` will permanently hide anything with `items: []` — including a freshly-created container (e.g. `addFolder` seeds `items: []`) or one drained to empty one entry at a time. Any such guard needs an explicit escape hatch (e.g. `isEditMode ||`) for the legitimate empty/mid-edit state, not just the "all inaccessible" state. Confirmed 2026-07-04: Dock folder visibility guard, `shouldShowFolder` (#2136).
- **Reordering a filtered subset must translate indices back to the full array**: when a drag-and-drop `SortableContext` is scoped to a filtered view (`visibleItems`) but the reorder handler resolves positions via `fullArray.indexOf(...)`, the two index spaces disagree — reordering visible items silently shifts hidden items to different absolute slots. Fix: compute the reorder entirely in filtered-space, then splice the result back into the full array by filtered-membership, leaving every non-visible element's original index untouched. Confirmed 2026-07-04: `reorderPreservingHidden` in Dock folder drag-reorder (#2136).
- **A locale key can be "present" in EVERY locale including `en.json` and still never resolve**: the earlier "verbatim-EN placeholder" pattern (key present, value copied from EN) has a worse variant — a `t()` call site can ship with `{ defaultValue: '<EN text>' }` while NO locale, not even `en.json`, ever defines the real key. i18next's fallback chain (current language → `fallbackLng` → `defaultValue`) never finds a real value at any locale, so every user in every language silently sees the hardcoded `defaultValue` string forever. This reads identically to "working in English, broken elsewhere" but is actually "never had a working translation path at all." Test pattern: assert the namespace itself exists in `en.json`, not just that DE/ES/FR match/mismatch it. Confirmed 2026-07-04: `plcDashboard.resources` namespace absent from all 4 locales (#2138).
- **Partial-credit `isCorrect` invariant applies per question type, not just per feature**: the `isCorrect ⟺ pointsEarned >= pointsMax` invariant (first fixed for Matching in #1950) is derived independently for each question type's grading branch — fixing it for one type does not fix it for a sibling type with its own strict-equality-vs-formula split. Confirmed 2026-07-04: the identical contradiction existed in `Ordering`'s partial-credit branch, unfixed since #1950, discovered fresh this run (#2137).
- **CI guard for a class of bug, not a single instance**: when a historical incident (`mirrorPlcIndex.test.ts` crash-at-import, #2047) prompts a backlog item, verify the CLASS of failure is still reproducible in the CURRENT toolchain before designing a fix — Vitest v4.1.8 already fails loudly on a crash-at-import today, so that part of the backlog item was stale. The actual live gap (a test file becoming invisible to the collector — renamed extension, over-broad `exclude`, disabled-flag-hidden `describe` block — with exit 0 and no failure) required a different mechanism: an aggregate file/test-count floor checked against a committed baseline, since there's no per-file signal for "this file no longer exists to the collector." Confirmed 2026-07-04: `scripts/checkTestCounts.mjs` / `test:counts` (#2139).
- **Multiple concurrent worktrees running full test suites causes real flakiness — always verify in isolation before accepting a failure**: with 5+ nightly worktrees each running `pnpm run test`/`pnpm run validate` at once, host load average can spike to ~50, producing `Test timed out in 5000ms` failures in tests completely unrelated to any change (timing-sensitive `act()`/`waitFor` calls). Never treat a CPU-contention timeout as a real regression — re-run the specific failing file(s) in isolation once load drops; if it passes clean, the earlier failure was noise, not a real change side-effect. Confirmed repeatedly 2026-07-04 across state-data, dashboard-layout, admin-config, build-tooling agents and orchestrator verification passes.
- **Two Claude sessions can work the same PR concurrently and race on a push**: a separate automated pr-review process was independently applying (and pushing) fixes for the same Gemini review comments on PR #2139 while this run's orchestrator was doing the same thing by hand — `git push` was rejected ("fetch first") because the remote had already advanced. Resolution: `git fetch` + inspect the remote commit — if it's a functionally-equivalent fix (same review comment, same suggested change), `git reset --hard origin/<branch>` to adopt it rather than force-pushing a duplicate. Confirmed 2026-07-04: `nightly/build-tooling-2026-07-04` (#2139, commit `21f08db`).
- **`??` chained across a value that can be meaningfully `null` silently prefers a stale fallback**: `a?.field ?? fallback` cannot distinguish "field is absent" from "field is present and legitimately `null`" — if `field: null` is a real, meaningful state (e.g. "this board lives at root," not "board data is missing"), the fallback fires anyway and can surface stale data. Fix by branching explicitly on the outer optional (`a ? (a.field ?? null) : fallback`) rather than chaining `??` through it. Confirmed 2026-07-05: `SidebarBoardsActive.tsx` (#2148); the correct pattern already existed next door in `BoardNavFab.tsx`.
- **i18n bug class: "translated but wrong word choice," distinct from verbatim-EN**: the established verbatim-EN-placeholder pattern (key present, value literally copied from `en.json`) has a sibling bug where a key holds a GENUINE translation that nonetheless uses the wrong word for a concept the rest of the locale translates consistently (e.g. DE using the English loanword "Board" in 12 spots while ~80 other keys correctly use "Tafel"). Neither i18next's `defaultValue` fallback nor a verbatim-EN test (`value !== en.value`) catches this — the value differs from EN and isn't absent, it's just inconsistent with the locale's own established terminology. Test pattern: assert the canonical term appears (not just that the EN term is absent), and/or sweep the whole locale file for disallowed loanwords. Confirmed 2026-07-05: DE `Board`→`Tafel` drift (#2149).
- **Firestore rules: new share-like collections must mirror the sibling's `expiresAt` cutoff, not just its read-gating structure**: when a rule set has two structurally similar collections (e.g. `/shared_boards` and `/shared_collections`, both supporting a "substitute" mode gated to host/admin/a specific email domain), copying the read-gating condition without also copying an `expiresAt` cutoff leaves the copy permanently readable past expiry — a real access-control gap, not just an inconsistency. Any future third sibling (or a new mode on an existing collection) must be diffed field-by-field against the most complete existing sibling, not just structurally skimmed. Confirmed 2026-07-05: `/shared_boards` substitute reads had no cutoff; `/shared_collections` did (#2150). Also: a rules file with zero tests for a given mode (`sharedBoards.test.ts` had no substitute-mode coverage at all) is a strong signal that sibling asymmetry has never been checked.
- **Orchestrator note — subagents dispatched via the Agent tool cannot receive background-task completion notifications**: only the top-level session receives `<task-notification>` events for `run_in_background` Bash/Agent calls. A subagent that backgrounds its own `pnpm run validate`/`lint` and then waits for "the notification" will stall indefinitely — it has to poll synchronously (foreground re-run, or check the redirected output file directly) instead. Confirmed 2026-07-05: 4 of 5 dispatched subagents stalled this way on their first attempt at the final validate/build step; each needed an explicit `SendMessage` correction telling them to stop using `run_in_background`. **Future dispatch prompts should state this constraint up front** ("do not use run_in_background for your own validate/build/lint calls — run them as plain synchronous foreground commands with a generous timeout") to avoid the wasted round-trip.
- **Multi-line comment blocks are a near-universal subagent habit that needs orchestrator cleanup**: despite CLAUDE.md's "one short line max" comment rule being in the dispatch prompt's referenced context, 5 of 5 subagents this run wrote 2-9-line comment/docstring blocks explaining root cause or verification rationale (the kind of narrative that belongs in the PR description, not the diff). The orchestrator trimmed all of them to one line (or removed the per-`describe`-block ones entirely where the test names already carried the meaning) during Phase 2 review before pushing. Treat this as a standing review checklist item, not a one-off. One exception: `firestore.rules` itself already has an established convention of multi-line explanatory comments throughout (unlike its own `.test.ts` files) — match the surrounding file's convention, don't blindly enforce the one-line rule where a file already does otherwise consistently.
- **Area-coverage gap discovered mid-review, not by design**: a sub-agent's flagged out-of-scope find (`components/classroomAddon/`) surfaced that roughly two dozen `components/*` subdirectories (all the student-facing session apps, PLC UI, sharing modals, Spotify/ClassLink integration, etc.) have never been covered by any of the 5 code areas' globs since bootstrap — they're invisible to every nightly run. See the Backlog row for the list and a re-segmentation recommendation for a future run.
- **A passing Firestore rules test suite does not prove a live client query still works**: `getDoc`/single-document rules tests (and even `assertSucceeds`/`assertFails` on ad-hoc queries built by hand in the test) only prove the rule is _logically_ correct — they don't catch that a real app query lacks a `where()` clause matching a new rule constraint. Firestore rejects an entire list query if it cannot statically prove every possible matching document passes the rule from the query's own filters; adding an inequality to a rule (e.g. `expiresAt > request.time`) silently breaks any existing `onSnapshot`/`getDocs` query on that collection that doesn't already filter on the same field. When changing a read rule for a collection with a real-time list subscription, grep the client hooks/components for every query against that collection and check each one's `where()` clauses BEFORE considering the rules-test suite sufficient proof of safety. Confirmed 2026-07-05: `useSubstituteShares.ts` (#2150), caught only by claude[bot] review, not by the emulator rules-test suite (which only exercised single-doc reads).
- **Concurrent-process reconciliation happened twice more tonight**: same pattern as 2026-07-04's #2139 incident — a separate automated pr-review process pushed its own fixes for the same claude[bot] review comments the orchestrator was independently addressing, on both `nightly/admin-config-2026-07-05` (redundant type cast + case-insensitive regex, identical to the orchestrator's uncommitted local fix — resolved by discarding the local duplicate and adopting the remote commit) and `nightly/dashboard-layout-2026-07-05` (a `beforeEach`/`mockReset` fix pushed as a separate commit while the orchestrator had unrelated uncommitted changes to the same file — resolved via `git stash` → `reset --hard origin/<branch>` → `stash pop`, which auto-merged cleanly since the changes touched different regions of the file). Rule of thumb: before any `git commit --amend`/force-push on a nightly branch mid-review, `git fetch` and diff against `origin/<branch>` first — if a foreign commit already contains an identical or overlapping fix, stash-reset-pop (or just adopt the remote directly) rather than clobbering it with a force-push.
- **Mock interfaces for context hooks should match the real type's nullability and function signatures exactly, or real branches go untested**: a hand-rolled mock interface (`MockUseDashboardReturn`, etc.) that narrows a nullable field to non-null (`activeDashboard: Dashboard` instead of `Dashboard | null`) or widens/narrows a callback's parameters (`loadDashboard: () => void` instead of `(id: string) => void`) doesn't just risk a type-check gap — it makes TypeScript actively reject a test that would exercise the real fallback/null branch, so that branch silently goes uncovered even though the mock "compiles fine." When hand-rolling a mock interface (rather than using `ReturnType<typeof useX>` directly), copy the nullability and signatures from the real context-value type file, not from what the component under test happens to destructure. Confirmed 2026-07-05: `SidebarBoardsActive.test.tsx`'s `MockUseDashboardReturn`/`MockUseAuthReturn` (#2148), caught by claude[bot] review across two rounds.
- **A no-op guard must compare against the value it was computed FROM, not a separately-tracked persisted baseline that can drift**: when a live/derived value (`current`, from a ticking ref/state) and a persisted value (`config.elapsedTime`, frozen at last write) start equal but diverge over time, a guard shaped `if (next === <persisted>) return` is checking the wrong thing — `next` was computed from `current`, so the only correct no-op condition is `next === current` (clamping had no effect on what was actually passed in). Comparing against the persisted value instead produces false positives whenever the two values coincidentally realign, which can be a `once per second of runtime` occurrence for integer-millisecond timing arithmetic — not just at extreme edge cases. Confirmed 2026-07-05: `TimeTool.adjustTime`'s ceiling no-op guard (#2147), caught by claude[bot] review; the bug was introduced by a same-day fix for a narrower issue and was more severe than the issue it was fixing.
- **`vi.useFakeTimers()` intercepts `requestAnimationFrame` too**: confirmed empirically 2026-07-05 — advancing fake timers (`vi.advanceTimersByTime(ms)`) drives RAF-based loops (e.g. `TimeTool`'s countdown tick) exactly as real elapsed time would, without needing a separate RAF polyfill or manual frame stepping. This makes RAF-driven state fully testable deterministically: render, `act(() => vi.advanceTimersByTime(ms))`, then assert on the resulting state — no need to fall back to `renderHook` + manual ref manipulation for scenarios that depend on real elapsed time.
- **A live Firestore QUERY listener dies entirely — not a silent per-doc removal — when a previously-matched document is written to after it loses rule eligibility**: confirmed empirically against the real Firestore emulator 2026-07-05 (not just reasoned about): open an `onSnapshot` query listener, let a matched document's data drift outside the security rule's condition purely via elapsed wall-clock time (e.g. an `expiresAt > request.time` check), then WRITE to that document (any write, even unrelated fields) — the listener's error callback fires with `permission-denied` for the WHOLE query, not a `removed` DocumentChange for just that doc. This is a DIFFERENT failure mode from the "query establishment requires provable `where()` constraints" issue (also real, also fixed this run) — that one fires at subscribe time; this one fires later, mid-listener-lifetime, triggered by ANY write to a now-rule-ineligible document that's still inside the client's frozen query filter (which only reflects the world at subscribe time, not "now"). Any `onSnapshot` query gated by a rule with a time-based or otherwise-driftable condition needs either a periodic re-subscribe or a `permission-denied` retry-with-fresh-baseline handler — a green rules-test suite (built on `getDoc`/one-shot assertions) does not catch this; it requires a live listener + a deliberate mid-stream write to reproduce. Confirmed 2026-07-05: `useSubstituteShares.ts` (#2150), caught by claude[bot] review, verified and fixed with a capped retry-and-resubscribe.
- **A security-rule change that starts denying a previously-always-allowed read can silently break a client's error-handling assumptions, not just its query shape**: `SubBoardScreen.tsx`'s expiry countdown/redirect logic depended on the read always SUCCEEDING (even for an expired share) so its own client-side timer could detect expiry and drive the UX. Tightening the rule to deny expired reads outright meant the component never got the data it needed to run that logic at all — the fix has to teach the error path to recognize "this specific denial reason means the same thing my old success-path expiry check meant" (here: a `permission-denied` on this document type can only mean expiry, since the rule's only non-time-gated branches are host/admin). When a rule fix changes a read from "always succeeds, client filters" to "server denies," audit every client consumer of that read for logic that assumed success and only branched on the DATA (not the error). Confirmed 2026-07-05: `SubBoardScreen.tsx` (#2150).
- **Retry/reconnect counters belong in a `useRef`, not `useState`, when the SAME effect must both increment (to trigger the retry) and reset (on success) them**: putting a retry counter in `useState` because "we need it in the effect's deps to trigger a resubscribe" also means RESETTING it on success is a state change, which re-runs the same effect and tears down/rebuilds a listener that just recovered — one wasted round-trip per successful recovery. Split the concerns: a `useRef` holds the count for read/cap-check/reset (mutating it triggers no render), and a separate, monotonically-increasing `useState` "token" is bumped ONLY on the path that should actually trigger a resubscribe. Confirmed 2026-07-05: `useSubstituteShares.ts` retry logic (#2150), caught by claude[bot] review of the retry fix itself.
- **Don't log an error for a condition your own code already treats as recoverable and expected**: the retry fix's `logError` call originally ran before the retry-vs-give-up branch, so it fired on every transient `permission-denied` reconnect attempt — 3 log entries for one routine share-expiry event, noise that can trip real alerting/rate-limit thresholds in production. If a handler treats an error code as "retry silently," only log when the retry budget is actually exhausted (the path that surfaces a user-facing error), not on every attempt along the way. Confirmed 2026-07-05: `useSubstituteShares.ts` (#2150).
- **A reset-on-condition-X ref/counter must fire on the condition that actually changed, not on every re-run of the effect it lives in**: fixing "the retry budget doesn't reset when it logically should" by resetting unconditionally at the top of the effect is a trap the moment that effect ALSO re-runs for a reason unrelated to the reset (here: the effect re-runs both on `buildingId` changing AND on the retry mechanism's own `retryToken` bump — resetting on every run zeroed the just-incremented counter before its cap could ever be reached, silently converting a capped retry into an infinite one). When a value should reset on condition A but must survive re-runs triggered by condition B, track "did A actually change" explicitly (a second ref holding the previous value of A, compared at the top of the effect) rather than resetting unconditionally. Caught by re-running the EXISTING test suite after the "obvious" one-line fix — the naive version passed the reviewer's own suggested diff but broke a different, already-passing test. Confirmed 2026-07-05: `useSubstituteShares.ts` `retryCountRef`/`prevBuildingIdRef` (#2150).
- **Five consecutive claude[bot] review rounds on one PR each found a genuinely real, previously-undetected bug — iterative review depth compounds, it doesn't just repeat**: PR #2150 went security-rule fix → query-fix-that-breaks-production → retry-fix-with-a-worse-regression → retry-fix-refinement (log noise + rebuild waste) → retry-fix-edge-cases (cross-building leak, misleading comment, unrelated regex CVE). Each round's fix was itself imperfect in a way only surfaced by the NEXT round of scrutiny, and at least two of the five rounds (the query-vs-rule mismatch, the live-listener permission-denied behavior) were only confirmed by direct empirical testing against the real Firestore emulator — reasoning about Firestore's rule-evaluation semantics from the docs alone was insufficient and in one case actively misleading (the orchestrator's own first-principles reasoning about query listener behavior was wrong until empirically tested). Do not treat "the reviewer approved it" or "one round of review passed" as a depth ceiling — for security/rules/concurrency-adjacent code, budget for multiple review-and-fix cycles, and prefer live empirical verification over static reasoning whenever the claim concerns real-time system behavior (listeners, rule re-evaluation, timing) rather than pure logic. Confirmed 2026-07-05: PR #2150, all 5 rounds.

- **Firestore list-query enforcement gap, second confirmed instance**: same class of bug as `useSubstituteShares.ts` (#2150) but on a plain equality-vs-OR-branch mismatch rather than a time-based inequality — `AnnouncementOverlay.tsx`'s PATH B ran `where('isActive','==',true)` with no `orgId` filter, relying on the rule's `isOrgMember(resource.data.orgId)` OR-branch for isolation. Confirmed against the real emulator that Firestore does NOT enforce `resource.data`-dependent rule branches as a per-doc filter for a list query unless the query's own `where()` clauses pin the same field — even a _correct_ rule leaks data through an unscoped list query. The fix is always in the query shape (add a `where()` that structurally proves the same field the rule branches on), never in the rule alone. Any collection with an OR-branch rule (`fieldA == X || fieldB == Y || isAdmin()`) needs its list queries audited for this pattern, not just collections with time-based (`expiresAt`) conditions. Confirmed 2026-07-07: `AnnouncementOverlay.tsx` (#2158).
- **`AudioContext.resume()` must be awaited before checking `ctx.state`, and the caller shouldn't await the whole helper if it also drives a visual cue**: `resume()` is async and does not flip `ctx.state` synchronously — a fire-and-forget `void ctx.resume()` immediately followed by a `ctx.state === 'suspended'` guard silently no-ops on first use (browser autoplay policy starts every context suspended). Centralize "await resume, then play" in one shared helper rather than duplicating it per caller (confirmed duplicated identically in two widgets, `CatalystWidget`/`StarterPackWidget`, while a third, `DiceWidget`, already had it right). Separately: if the same click handler also triggers an unrelated visual cue (e.g. `confetti()`), don't `await` the audio helper before firing it — `void` the audio call so the animation isn't delayed by resume latency. Confirmed 2026-07-07: `playCleanUpUnlocked()` (#2159); the `void` correction was a same-day claude[bot]/Gemini review catch, not part of the original fix.
- **"Always mounted, only `isOpen` toggles" components need a session/generation counter for every async handler, not just the "obvious" one**: when a component's callers never unmount it (only flip a boolean prop) and it resets its own state on that prop's transition, EVERY async handler that can outlive a close→reopen cycle needs the same stale-result guard — not just the one a bug report happened to name. `ImportWizard.tsx` had 5 handlers (`runParse`, `handlePickSheet`, `handleFilePicked`, `handleAiGenerate`, `handleConfirmSave`) sharing the identical race; a backlog item that named only `runParse` still required auditing (and fixing) all 5. Pattern: a `sessionRef` counter bumped on every `isOpen` transition, captured at each handler's entry, checked after every `await` before any `setState` (including inside `finally` blocks). Confirmed 2026-07-07: `ImportWizard.tsx` (#2160).
- **A PII-scrub allowlist drifts every time a widget config grows a new raw-name field — audit at the config-shape level, not the feature level**: `PII_WIDGET_FIELDS` (`utils/dashboardPII.ts`) is a flat list of field names; it does not know about a widget's _shape_, so it's silently incomplete whenever a widget gains a new name-carrying config field. Confirmed 2026-07-07 for Jigsaw's `lockedNames`/`unassignedNames`/`doneNames` and Stations' `customRoster` — all fixed. When auditing this allowlist, don't just check "does this widget have a known PII field" — read every optional field on the widget's full config type and ask whether it can ever hold a student name, roster array, or name-keyed object.
- **"Mechanically safe to add to a PII allowlist" is not the same as "safe to add"**: a same-day Gemini review pass on the #2161 fix above found 3 more `RandomConfig` fields carrying raw roster names (`lastResult`, `jigsawHomeGroups`, `jigsawExpertGroups`, all populated via `commitGroups()`) and confirmed each field name is unique to `RandomConfig` (no collateral-stripping risk). That mechanical safety check is necessary but not sufficient: `lastResult` is also load-bearing cross-widget display state that Scoreboard and Stations read back from Firestore, so scrubbing it (moving it Drive-only) would silently break reload persistence for any teacher not using Google Drive sync — a real product trade-off, not a pure privacy win. A parallel review session correctly deferred all 3 fields to the maintainer instead of auto-pushing them once it traced `lastResult`'s cross-widget read sites. Before adding a field to `PII_WIDGET_FIELDS`, grep every read site of that field, not just its write site — a field can be simultaneously "contains PII" and "required for a shipped feature to survive reload."
- **A field-name-based PII scrubber can't safely strip a field that's reused across widget types with different key semantics**: `assignments` appears in `StationsConfig`, `LunchCountConfig`, and `SeatingChartConfig` — the last already migrated from name-keyed to ID-keyed via its own widget-level effect, but Stations still keys `assignments` by literal student display name in both class-roster and custom modes. Blanket-adding `assignments` to `PII_WIDGET_FIELDS` would delete legitimate non-PII data (SeatingChart's ID-keyed assignments) on every save. When a field name is shared across multiple widget configs with different semantics, the allowlist-based scrubber is the wrong tool — the fix has to happen at the widget level (re-key by student ID, mirroring `SeatingChart`'s precedent) before the field can be safely added here. Confirmed 2026-07-07, deferred: `components/widgets/Stations/Widget.tsx` (see Backlog).
- **Scope a locale-terminology recursive-scan test to the specific namespace you fixed, not the whole locale file**: when fixing a terminology-consistency drift (e.g. "PLC"→"PLG") in one namespace (`plcDashboard`), a recursive "no remaining wrong-term usages" test must walk only that namespace's subtree. A whole-file scan would false-positive on legitimately-scoped uses of the same term elsewhere (e.g. `admin.plc.recovery`, `plc.errors.*`, `sidebar.nav.plcs` — namespaces that either haven't drifted yet or use the term correctly for a different feature). This is the same lesson as the DE `Board`/`Tafel` sweep test but the scoping matters more here because "PLC" legitimately co-exists with "PLG" in unrelated namespaces, whereas "Board" had no legitimate DE usage anywhere. Confirmed 2026-07-07: `deDashboardPlgTerminologyLocales.test.ts` scoped to `plcDashboard` only (#2162).
