- **Orchestrator note — subagents given a full-scratchpad path for their own validate/build logs can collide with a sibling agent's identically-named log file**: two subagents this run both independently chose to redirect their own `pnpm run validate` output to a generic filename (`validate2.log`) inside the orchestrator's _shared_ session scratchpad directory (visible/writable by all subagents in the same session), clobbering each other's output mid-run. Neither agent's own validate evidence could be trusted as a result — the orchestrator had to independently re-run validate for both anyway (which Phase 2 already requires, so no correctness impact, but wasted the agents' own verification effort). Future dispatch prompts should tell subagents to write their own logs inside their own worktree directory (e.g. `/home/user/nightly-worktrees/<area>/validate.log`), never to a shared scratchpad path, or to use a worktree-name-prefixed filename if a shared location is unavoidable.
- **Orchestrator note — the "don't background validate and wait for a notification" instruction needs to be reinforced, not just stated once**: despite the dispatch prompt explicitly warning against it (per the 2026-07-05 note on this exact failure mode), 3 of 5 subagents this run still ended a turn saying some variant of "waiting for the notification" after backgrounding their own `pnpm run validate`. Each required at least one, and in one case three, explicit follow-up messages before it actually re-ran the command in the foreground and read the result itself. The instruction in the initial prompt is necessary but not sufficient — budget for at least one correction round-trip per subagent on this specific failure mode until dispatch prompts are restructured to make foreground-only verification the path of least resistance (e.g. by pre-emptively telling agents the orchestrator will independently re-verify, removing their incentive to prove it themselves at all).
- **Concurrent-process reconciliation is now a routine occurrence, not an edge case — always `git fetch` and diff before pushing a review-comment fix**: happened on 3 of 5 PRs this run alone (#2158, #2159, #2162), each time a separate automated process pushed a fix for the same reviewer comment the orchestrator was independently addressing, sometimes with slightly different wording but functionally identical. In each case a straight `git fetch origin <branch>` + diff against the local uncommitted change confirmed equivalence, and `git checkout -- <file> && git reset --hard origin/<branch>` adopted the remote cleanly with no lost work. The container also restarted mid-run this session, killing in-flight background validate jobs — after any interruption (restart, resume, long gap), re-fetch and diff EVERY branch with pending local changes before assuming your last-known local state is still the frontier; don't just resume from memory of what you were about to push.
- **A hasty "mechanically safe" read of a reviewer suggestion is not a full read — check downstream consumers, not just producers, before extending someone else's fix**: on #2161, a Gemini suggestion to add 3 more fields to a PII allowlist was mechanically correct (unique field names, no collateral stripping) and the orchestrator applied it without first tracing where those fields were _read_. A parallel review session caught what the orchestrator missed: `lastResult` is read back from Firestore by Scoreboard and Stations to restore a settled pick/grouping on reload, so scrubbing it would silently break that feature for any teacher not using Google Drive sync. The orchestrator's own uncommitted change (adding all 3 fields) was reverted before it reached the remote, purely because the container restart happened to surface the parallel session's more careful comment first. Lesson: before extending a PII/security allowlist based on a "field X also contains sensitive data" finding, grep every read site of that field, not just confirm the write side is safe to delete — "safe to remove from Firestore" and "safe to delete" are different claims when the field round-trips through a reload.
- **`refs/stash` is shared across ALL worktrees of the same repo, not per-worktree**: two subagents this run each independently ran `git stash` in their own worktree and collided — one popped and briefly lost a _different_ agent's WIP from a _different_ worktree (`nightly/dashboard-layout-2026-07-11`'s `ImportWizard.tsx` change got dropped into `nightly/admin-config-2026-07-11`'s working tree via a stash pop). `git worktree` gives each worktree its own working directory and index, but the stash ref lives at the repo (`.git`) level and is shared by every worktree attached to it — this is different from the previously-documented "shared scratchpad file" and "shared refs/stash... during a push race" gotchas; this is a _local, uncommitted-work_ collision, not a remote one. Both agents independently self-corrected (verified the foreign file's origin, restored it via `git checkout`/`git restore`, reapplied their own fix directly) before the orchestrator's review caught it — confirmed via clean `git diff dev-paul --stat` on both final branches. **Standing rule: subagents working in concurrent worktrees of this repo must avoid `git stash` entirely** — use `git diff > /tmp/patch && git checkout -- <file>` (or just re-derive the edit) instead of stashing, since the stash ref is not worktree-scoped.
- **Subagents given `run_in_background`/Monitor tools during a nightly run reliably try to use them for their own `pnpm run validate`, despite standing dispatch-prompt warnings** — 2 of 5 subagents this run still stalled waiting for a background-task/Monitor notification that can never arrive for a subagent (this exact failure mode was already documented 2026-07-05; it recurred anyway). One needed two separate corrective nudges before it ran validate as a plain synchronous foreground command. Treat this as expected overhead per run, not a one-time fix — budget for at least one nudge per subagent, and consider whether a future dispatch-prompt revision could make foreground-only verification the literal path of least resistance (e.g. omitting background-capable tools from the subagent's toolset entirely for the validate/build step).
- **A mature, heavily-audited area can still yield a real bug from a _sibling-hook drift_ pattern, not just a fresh file read**: `useSyncedQuizGroups.ts`'s missing dedup fence (2026-07-11) was found not by reading an unfamiliar file cold, but by comparing it against its already-fixed sibling `useSyncedVideoActivityGroups.ts` — the sibling's fix (with its own explanatory comment) had simply never been ported over. When two hooks/utils implement the same pattern for different domains (quiz vs. video-activity vs. guided-learning, etc.), a fix landing in one is a strong signal to diff it against all siblings, not just trust that "this bug class is already fully audited" once one instance is fixed.
- **Worktree setup: `pnpm install` alone leaves `functions/node_modules` missing**: creating a nightly worktree and running plain `pnpm install` (not `pnpm run install:all`) installs only the root workspace — `functions/` needs its own `pnpm -C functions install`. 4 of 5 area agents this run hit `type-check:all`/`validate` failing at the functions step and had to self-heal with `pnpm -C functions install --frozen-lockfile` before proceeding. Future dispatch setup should run `pnpm run install:all`-equivalent (both root and `functions/` installs) per worktree before spawning the agent, not just root `pnpm install`.
- **A CI job that runs only a subset of the suite needs an explicit target filter, not "evaluate everything unconditionally"**: `scripts/checkTestCounts.mjs`'s `rules` target (#2194) shipped with `optional: true` so a _missing_ rules report wouldn't break `validate`/`test:all` — but that alone wasn't sufficient. `pnpm run test:rules` runs in the CI workflow's own isolated job with no `root`/`functions` reports ever present there, and the script unconditionally evaluated ALL targets including the two _required_ ones (root/functions), so the rules CI job failed on "report not found" for reports it never had any reason to produce — even though the rules suite itself was 1112/1112 green. Caught by Gemini review after the PR was already open, confirmed against the real failing CI log. Fixed by adding an explicit CLI target argument (`node scripts/checkTestCounts.mjs rules`) that scopes evaluation to just the requested target; no argument evaluates everything (unchanged default). Lesson: when a guard script has both "always run together" targets and "runs standalone in its own job" targets, `optional` on the missing-report case is not the same as "runs correctly when invoked alone" — test the actual invocation each CI job uses, not just the local `pnpm run validate` path.
- **Reviewer suggestions must be checked against the repo's actual lint rules before applying, even when they look obviously correct**: a Gemini suggestion to remove `return Promise.resolve();` from synchronous `act()` callbacks (as "redundant" since `await` handles non-promise returns) is correct in general JS but wrong for this repo — `@testing-library/react`'s `act()` has separate sync/async overloads keyed on the callback's return type, and removing the explicit promise return flips the call to the sync overload, which `@typescript-eslint/await-thenable` then correctly flags as an `await` of a non-thenable. Confirmed 2026-07-13: applying the suggestion produced 6 new lint errors; reverted and left a short PR comment explaining why, rather than silently ignoring the suggestion or applying it blind.
- **Subagent background-notification stalling is now a near-universal per-run tax, not an occasional one**: all 5 area agents this run stalled at least once waiting for a background-task/Monitor notification that can never reach a subagent (previously documented 2026-07-05, 2026-07-07, 2026-07-11 — recurred a 4th time). 2 of 5 required three or more explicit correction messages before self-recovering; 2 never fully recovered and the orchestrator finished their validate+commit directly instead. Treat "will need at least one correction per agent, and be prepared to finish 1-2 of them yourself" as the expected baseline for this failure mode, not an exception — the dispatch-prompt warning alone does not reliably prevent it.
- **Orchestrator's own `nohup cmd > log &` backgrounding needs the real child PID, not `$!` from a wrapped shell**: when the harness runs a Bash call as `bash -c 'eval "cmd &" ...'`, `$!`/the tool's own "background command" ID captures the outer wrapper, which can exit almost immediately (the `&` launch itself succeeds fast) while the actual `pnpm run validate` process keeps running detached with a different, unrelated PID. Confirmed repeatedly 2026-07-13: the reported "background command completed" notification fired in under a second while `pnpm run validate` was still minutes from finishing. Fix: after backgrounding, `pgrep -f "<distinctive part of the real command>"` to find the actual process PID, then track _that_ PID (`kill -0 <pid>` in a wait loop) — never trust the launcher's own exit as a proxy for the real work finishing.
- **A `Monitor` completion condition that greps log content for a phase-name string can false-positive on the npm script's own echoed command line**: a `grep -q "test:counts"` (or similar) intended to detect "validate reached its last step" matched instantly because `pnpm run validate`'s very first echoed line is `> pnpm run type-check:all && ... && pnpm run test:counts` — the full command text, not the step actually running. Confirmed 2026-07-13, multiple times. Prefer a PID-liveness condition (`while kill -0 <pid>; do sleep N; done`) over grepping for a string that might appear in earlier echoed output.
- **Container restarts mid-run preserve worktree file state, just not processes**: a sandbox container restart kills every dispatched subagent and any backgrounded `pnpm run validate`/build process it had running, but git worktrees on disk (including uncommitted changes and untracked new files) survive intact. Treat a worktree's `git diff`/`git status` as ground truth after any interruption — don't assume an area needs redispatch just because its subagent process is gone; review the diff directly and finish validation yourself. Confirmed 2026-07-15: all 5 area agents died mid-`validate`, all 5 worktrees' fixes were complete and salvageable.
- **Backlog rows go stale when a later, unrelated run fixes the same code without cross-referencing the row**: found 2 instances tonight — `migrateProportionalLayout`'s happy-path test (flagged 2026-05-26, actually shipped 2026-06-15 in #1976) and `GuidedLearningManager.tsx`'s folder-badge undercount (flagged 2026-07-13, actually shipped 2026-07-15 in #2211) — both backlog rows were still marked "open" a run or more after the fix landed, because the PR that fixed them didn't happen to touch the Backlog table. When a subagent investigates a backlog lead and finds it already fixed, don't just skip it silently — check the row off with the fixing PR number so the next run doesn't re-investigate the same dead lead. Confirmed 2026-07-18.
- **"Verifiable" (fail-before/pass-after in a unit test) is not the same as "reachable by a real user"**: a subagent's first attempt at the dashboard-layout bug tonight (`PeriodSelector.tsx`'s missing Escape guard) passed every mechanical bar — real logic bug, matches an established codebase pattern, clean fail-before/pass-after test — but the component turned out to have zero live render sites anywhere in the app (dead/staged code pending future wiring). The orchestrator caught this by grepping for consumers before approving, sent the agent back, and it found a genuinely-wired replacement bug (`FolderTree.tsx`, confirmed mounted in 4 live widget managers) instead. Before approving any fix, grep for real consumers of the component/function being fixed — a green unit test on an unmounted component proves the code is wrong, not that anyone is affected by it today.
- **The orchestrator is not exempt from its own "never `git stash` in this repo's worktrees" rule**: while re-verifying the widgets fix solo (all subagents already finished, so the immediate collision risk was low), the orchestrator used `git stash`/`git stash pop` out of habit to swap in the pre-fix source for a fail-before check — exactly the operation this doc has warned subagents against since 2026-07-11 (`refs/stash` is repo-level, not worktree-scoped). No collision occurred this time only because no other worktree was active. Use `cp file /tmp/backup && git show dev-paul:path > file` (restore via `cp` back, or `git checkout HEAD -- file`) instead, every time, regardless of who else is or isn't active — don't rely on being the only one currently touching the repo.
- **A `process.env.TZ = 'X'` mid-process reassignment in a Vitest test is not guaranteed to be honoured** — V8/ICU can cache the process timezone, especially after a `setupFile` (like this repo's `tests/setTz.ts`, which pins `TZ=UTC`) has already run; if the reassignment is silently ignored, a timezone-dependent regression test can pass on both fixed AND unfixed code (a false-positive guard). Caught by claude[bot] review on PR #2227 (Countdown bare-date fix) before merge — the orchestrator had verified fail-before/pass-after locally, which is necessary but doesn't rule out CI/future-Node fragility. Prefer testing the pure date-math helper directly via its own `Date` getters (inherently timezone-independent — `new Date(y, m, d, 12).getHours()` reads back `12` regardless of process TZ) as the primary regression guard, and use Vitest's own `vi.stubEnv`/`vi.unstubAllEnvs` (not raw `process.env.TZ =`) for any supplementary integration-level test that still wants real end-to-end coverage.
- **When one of several structurally-identical loops gets a safety/observability addition, audit every sibling loop for the same addition**: `gcPlcOrphans.ts`'s paginated PLC sweep warned via `console.warn` on hitting its safety ceiling; the two structurally-identical group-pagination loops (empty-group reap, version-overflow sweep) added in the same PR did not get the equivalent warning, creating a silent blind spot in Cloud Logging for exactly the failure mode the PLC warning exists to catch. Caught by claude[bot] review on PR #2230. When copying a pattern across N parallel loops in one change, checklist every instrumentation/logging addition against every copy, not just the first one written.
- **A `run_in_background`/`timeout` Bash call can silently truncate a long `pnpm run validate` mid-step**: the orchestrator's first baseline attempt used `timeout 590 pnpm run validate`, which killed the process (SIGTERM, exit 124) while `format:check` was still mid-scan — the log looked like a clean failure but was actually a truncation artifact, not a real formatting error. Fixed by backgrounding with `nohup cmd &` + a PID-liveness poll loop (`pgrep -f "vitest|tsc|eslint"` in a `sleep`-and-recheck loop across multiple Bash calls, since a single call caps at 10 min) instead of a single bounded `timeout` invocation. Confirmed 2026-07-19: full `validate` regularly runs 10–15 min wall-clock under this sandbox even at low contention: use the poll pattern by default for this specific command, not a one-shot timeout.
- **A rules-security fix should be checked for "does any live client query touch this collection" before shipping, not just after a review catches it**: unlike the #2150/#2158 incidents (where the list-query-breakage risk was discovered mid-fix or by review), the orchestrator proactively grepped every client call site against `shared_activity_walls` (`ActivityWallGalleryView.tsx`, `ShareModal.tsx`) BEFORE approving #2242's `expiresAt`/`revoked` read-gate, confirming all reads were single-doc `getDoc()` with no live `onSnapshot` list query in the blast radius. Promote this from "lesson learned after an incident" to a standing pre-ship checklist item for any `firestore.rules` change that adds a new denial condition to an existing collection: grep client consumers first, don't wait for a review round to find it.
- **The comment-convention exception isn't limited to `firestore.rules` — it extends to any file family with its own pre-existing multi-line-comment style**: confirmed 2026-07-19 for two more families — `tests/i18n/*TerminologyLocales.test.ts` (each file already opens with a multi-paragraph rationale comment, e.g. `esPlcErrorsComunidadTerminologyLocales.test.ts`) and `tests/rules/*.test.ts` (each file already opens with a multi-line "what this guards and why" header, e.g. the pre-existing header in `sharedActivityWalls.test.ts`). Before trimming a subagent's multi-line comment during review, check `git show dev-paul:<path>` for the file's own pre-existing convention — don't apply the one-line default blind to files whose established style is already otherwise. Widgets' and Dashboard & Layout's new comments this run did NOT match any such local convention and were correctly trimmed to one line.
- **A claude[bot] review's "not a blocker" nit is still worth taking when it's a true one-line, zero-risk change**: PR #2240 got a non-blocking ordering nit (clear the stale timer before `setState`, not after — functionally identical but more readable); PR #2242 got a non-blocking test-coverage suggestion (cover the `expiresAt: null` permanent-share path, already correct without it). Both were applied as same-day follow-up commits after re-verifying the specific affected test file (and, for the rules change, re-running the full test through the emulator) rather than the entire `validate` suite — proportionate verification for a proportionately small change, not full re-validation ceremony for a one-line reorder or a single new test case.
- **A Firestore-persisted config key-scheme migration needs the READ path, the WRITE path, AND every write BRANCH audited up front, not just the primary write site**: #2247 (LunchCount id-keying) took 4 rounds to close. Subagent fix switched reads/writes to `student.id`. Orchestrator review caught round 2: the read path alone needed a legacy-name fallback or every pre-existing dashboard's assignments would silently reset to "unassigned" on load — a real production data-loss risk, not just a theoretical one, since this widget has been live. claude[bot] review then caught round 3: the _unassign_ write branch deleted only the id key, so a legacy-keyed student could never actually be unassigned (read-path fallback kept resurfacing the stale entry) — a regression from the pre-fix state. A second claude[bot] pass caught round 4: the _positive-reassignment_ write branch had the same gap, silently orphaning stale legacy entries in Firestore on every direct reassignment. All 4 gaps were instances of the exact same class (enumerate every place the migrated key is read OR written, not just the first one you touch) — next time a fix changes which field a persisted config is keyed by, explicitly list every read site and every write branch before calling it done, rather than fixing them one review-round at a time.
- **Mutating the actual decision logic gives stronger fail-before verification than deleting/renaming the module under test**: the Build & Tooling subagent's own fail-before check for `expireActivityWallShares.ts` temporarily removed the source file and observed an import-failure crash — real, but only proves the module exists, not that the specific branch logic is exercised. The orchestrator's independent re-verification instead flipped one condition inside `isLapsedShare` (`=== true` → `=== false`) and got a concrete wrong-value diff (`sessionsRelocked: 1` expected, `0` received) from 3 of 13 tests — a much sharper signal that the tests actually pin down the logic, not just the module's existence. Prefer targeted logic mutation over module deletion when independently re-verifying a subagent's fail-before/pass-after claim.
- **Orchestrator-authored mid-review repair commits get reviewed too — keep watching PR activity after pushing your own fix, not just after the subagent's original commit**: on #2247, claude[bot] posted 3 separate review passes across the PR's lifetime (one on the subagent's original fix, one after the orchestrator's read-path backward-compat commit, one after the orchestrator's first write-path fix) — each catching a new, real, previously-unaddressed gap in the immediately-preceding commit. Treat your own review-driven repairs as still-under-review, not as the final word, until the PR goes quiet.
- **The background-wait failure mode is now happening to 3+ of 5 subagents per run, every run — treat the dispatch-prompt warning as necessary but never sufficient, budget for a first-turn nudge as standard, not exceptional**: on 2026-07-23, 3 of 5 area subagents (Build & Tooling, Widgets, Dashboard & Layout) ended their first turn saying some variant of "I'll wait for the notification" after backgrounding `pnpm run validate`/`tsc`/`type-check` — despite the dispatch prompt explicitly stating, in nearly identical wording to prior runs, that subagents cannot receive background-task notifications. Every one of the 3 self-corrected in exactly one round once told directly "there is no notification, poll `kill -0 <PID>` yourself" and went on to deliver a fully-verified fix. A 4th agent (State & Data) hit a milder variant: it backgrounded a validate run for a _clean_ report where no code had changed, meaning the run was pointless regardless of foreground/background — worth calling out explicitly in future dispatch prompts that a clean report with zero commits never needs a validate run at all, since the orchestrator's pre-dispatch baseline already covers it. Only 1 of 5 (Admin & Config) ran end-to-end without any stall. Stop treating this as a bug to eventually eliminate via better prompt wording — it is now the steady-state baseline for this harness. Plan every dispatch expecting 1 nudge round for ~60% of agents, and don't be surprised when it recurs after the prompt is reworded again.
- **Independent orchestrator re-verification via file-swap (not `git stash`) scales cleanly across a 4-fix review pass**: this run's Phase 2 review used `cp <file> /tmp/backup_<x> && git show dev-paul:<path> > <file>` to force pre-fix source into each worktree, ran the new test file directly (confirmed fail), then `cp` restored the fixed file back and confirmed pass — for all 4 shipped fixes (Widgets, Dashboard & Layout, Admin & Config, Build & Tooling), zero `git stash` use, zero cross-worktree collision, `git status --short` clean after every restore. This is the standing pattern from 2026-07-19's "orchestrator isn't exempt from its own stash rule" note, now confirmed to work smoothly at full run scale, not just for a single solo re-check.
- **Git worktree signing workaround may no longer be needed**: confirmed 2026-07-26 — a plain `git commit` (with hooks, no `--no-verify`, no `gpg.ssh.program` override) succeeded cleanly from a worktree at `/home/user/spartboard-nightly-widgets`. The 2026-05-27 note above (HTTP 400 "missing source" from `/tmp/code-sign`) may have been environment-specific and since resolved. **Do not assume either way** — try a plain `git commit` first each run; only fall back to the `/tmp/sign-from-root.sh` workaround if it actually fails with that specific error.
- **A pagination-cliff fail-before check must revert to page-scale behavior, not just "single fetch capped at the current ceiling"**: when verifying a fix that replaces `.limit(bigCeiling).get()` with paginated fetching, reverting to `.limit(bigCeiling).get()` alone does NOT reproduce the bug if the test fixture's data volume is smaller than `bigCeiling` — the single fetch still captures everything and the test passes on "pre-fix" code, giving a false sense of verification. The correct revert is a single fetch capped at the PAGE size (what an un-paginated fetch actually looked like at the scale the original bug occurred), not the final safety ceiling. Confirmed 2026-07-26: a subagent's own fail-before check for `finalizeIdleQuizAttempts.ts` used the new 20000-ceiling and passed even on "reverted" code (505 fixture rows < 20000); the orchestrator's re-verification with a `RESPONSE_PAGE_SIZE`-capped single fetch correctly failed. Apply this to any future pagination-cliff fix's fail-before check (`gcPlcOrphans.ts`, `expireActivityWallShares.ts` pattern family).
- **A Backlog row marked "superseded"/"stale" by a later run must be struck through immediately, not left for a future run to notice**: the `plcDashboard` "~140 keys remain" row (open since 2026-05-28) was correctly identified as stale by the 2026-07-18 State & Data audit, which added a NEW superseded-row entry — but the ORIGINAL open row was never struck through and sat active (and technically actionable-looking) for 8 more nights until the 2026-07-26 Admin & Config agent re-confirmed it clean from scratch and the orchestrator finally struck it through. When you determine an existing Backlog row is stale/resolved, edit that row in place (strikethrough + explanation) in the SAME pass — don't just add a new note elsewhere that supersedes it in spirit.
- **A unit test that reaches a real, unmocked external SDK call is a latent flakiness bug even when it "usually" passes fast**: `functions/src/index.test.ts`'s `generateVideoActivity` admin-bypass test made a genuine HTTPS round-trip to Google's live Generative Language API (no `vi.mock('@google/genai')` anywhere in the file) — normally rejected in under 100ms with an invalid-key error, but under CPU/network contention (concurrent nightly worktrees, this harness's normal operating condition) that round-trip exceeded Vitest's 5000ms default timeout and failed the orchestrator's baseline `pnpm run validate`. The tell: the test passed instantly in isolation but flaked only under load — that pattern (isolation-passes, contention-fails) usually means CPU-bound flakiness per prior notes, but here it meant a network-bound unit test. Any `functions/` test that constructs a real third-party SDK client (`GoogleGenAI`, etc.) needs that SDK mocked at the module level, full stop — "the fake key gets rejected fast enough in practice" is not a substitute for determinism. Confirmed 2026-07-31: fixed with `vi.mock('@google/genai', ...)` preserving real enum exports but replacing the class with a deterministic spy (#2315).
- **A precedented, established fix pattern (`stopPropagation()` on Escape for portalled menus) still needs a fresh grep per run, not a one-time sweep**: this bug class has now been fixed independently in `ToolDockItem`/`RemoteControlMenu`/`ClassRosterMenu` (#2266), `BoardNavFab`/`CollectionSwitcherMenu`/`BoardActionsFab` (#2289), and `OverflowMenu` (#2314, 2026-07-31) — three separate nights, three separate sets of components, because new portalled popovers keep getting added without the guard. Treat "fixed in components X/Y/Z" as "fixed in the components audited so far," not "fixed for the codebase," and re-grep `components/**` for portalled Escape handlers missing `stopPropagation()` on every Dashboard & Layout run until a lint rule or shared hook makes the omission structurally impossible.
- **husky/lint-staged's own pre-commit hook runs `git stash` internally** (`Backing up original state...` / `Backed up original state in git stash`) even though this doc tells subagents/the orchestrator never to use `git stash` manually in these worktrees. Confirmed 2026-07-31: this auto-stash-and-pop completed cleanly with no collision (verified `git stash list` empty immediately after), but it's a reminder that the "never `git stash`" rule is about deliberate manual use — the hook's own internal use is outside anyone's control and carries the same theoretical repo-wide-`refs/stash` collision risk documented since 2026-07-11 if two worktrees' commits land in the exact same instant. No action needed unless this is ever observed to actually collide.
- **A claude[bot] review nit is worth taking even when you've independently verified the current form matches file precedent**: on #2314, the reviewer flagged a 3-line comment as exceeding the one-line convention; the orchestrator had already checked sibling files (`RemoteControlMenu.tsx` uses a 5-line comment for the identical bug class) and concluded the length was justified. Applied the suggested one-liner anyway (per the existing "true one-line, zero-risk change" lesson from 2026-07-19) — the shorter version still carries the essential why, and taking a harmless nit costs nothing even when your own prior read of the codebase would have defended the status quo.
- **The harness's native `run_in_background` Bash parameter survives a full sandbox container reboot; manual `nohup cmd &`/`setsid nohup cmd &` backgrounding does not**: confirmed empirically 2026-08-04 — Phase 0's baseline `pnpm run validate` was killed twice by genuine container reboots (`uptime` reset to 0 min both times) while backgrounded via manual `nohup`/`setsid` + disown, even with stdin redirected from `/dev/null` and the process fully detached from the calling shell's process group. Switching to the Bash tool's own `run_in_background: true` parameter (which persists a background-task record the harness tracks independently, rather than a bare detached OS process) survived the same class of interruption and delivered a normal completion notification. For any command expected to run long enough to risk an interruption (i.e. essentially all `pnpm run validate`/build calls in this routine), prefer the harness's native background mechanism over manual shell backgrounding — this is now the standing default in Phase 0/1/3, not just a fallback.
- **Two genuine container reboots during Phase 0 (before any worktree existed) cost time but not correctness**: unlike the previously-documented mid-run restart pattern (worktrees survive, only processes die), a Phase-0 restart has nothing to preserve yet — the only cost is re-running the baseline validate from scratch. Distinguish this from a mid-Phase-1 restart (where the existing "check every worktree's git diff before assuming redispatch is needed" rule applies) by checking `uptime`/`who -b` before assuming a stalled background process needs a full restart-recovery procedure — a Phase 0 restart just needs a plain retry.
- **The background-wait stall recurred at the same ~60% rate again, 2026-08-05**: 3 of 5 subagents (Widgets, Dashboard & Layout, Build & Tooling) ended their first turn with some variant of "waiting for the monitor/notification" after backgrounding `pnpm run validate`, despite the dispatch prompt not even mentioning background execution as an option this run — this happens even when subagents aren't explicitly told to background anything, because they reach for `Monitor`/`run_in_background`-style tools on their own for a long-running command. All 3 self-corrected in exactly one round once told directly that background processes they start do not wake their own turn back up and to block synchronously instead. Continue budgeting one nudge round per dispatch as standard.
- **Give every independent verification a uniquely-named log file, never a shared/predictable scratchpad path**: this run, both the orchestrator (re-verifying State & Data) and the State & Data subagent itself independently backgrounded `pnpm run validate` to output paths that collided with a sibling agent's concurrent run on the shared host, producing an interleaved/unreadable log (the subagent caught this itself and re-ran to a fresh path before trusting its own "green" conclusion). Always suffix background-command output paths with the specific area/purpose (e.g. `verify-<area>-validate.log`, not a bare `validate.log`) so 5 concurrent worktrees' outputs never share a destination.
- **Two capture-phase listeners on the same node: `stopPropagation()` does NOT block a sibling; only `stopImmediatePropagation()` does — and registration order, not DOM tree position, decides who fires first among same-phase-same-target listeners**: confirmed empirically 2026-08-05 while investigating the `Modal.tsx` capture-phase Escape fix (#2392, later reverted). When two `{capture:true}` listeners are both registered directly on `window`, they fire in registration order regardless of which component "logically" nests inside the other in the React tree — a `Modal` opened first, with a `useDialog()` confirm dialog opened later from inside it, has Modal's listener firing FIRST (registered earlier) even though the confirm dialog is the "innermost" UI. If the first listener only calls `stopPropagation()` (not `Immediate`), the second (sibling, same node) listener still runs — but by then the first listener's side effect (e.g. closing the outer Modal) has already happened. Any future fix that adds a new `window`-level capture-phase Escape listener must audit every OTHER capture-phase `window` listener for this same-node race, not just bubble-phase ones.
- **When a component both fires an escape/close side effect on target/bubble AND is nested inside a portal, capture-phase interception anywhere on the ancestor chain (even one level up) silences it entirely, not just "runs after"**: `RestrictionsPicker.tsx`'s own bubble-phase (React-synthetic, delegated at the app root) Escape handler for its per-row popover NEVER fired once `Modal.tsx` intercepted Escape in the capture phase on `window` — `stopPropagation()` called during capture, before the event reaches the target, prevents the target/bubble phases from running at all for every descendant, regardless of that descendant's own listener's phase. This is stricter than "runs later" — it's "never runs." Any fix that adds capture-phase interception at an ancestor (window, a portal root, etc.) must audit for in-descendant Escape handling that isn't captured by an existing widget/portal exemption guard (`isEscapeFromWidgetInput` only recognizes `[data-widget-portal]`/`[data-draggable-window]` — it does not know about arbitrary in-modal dropdowns/popovers).
- **A non-blocking "verify before merging" note from an automated review is not the same as "no action needed" — investigate wide-blast-radius flags directly rather than replying with reassurance**: the `Modal.tsx` PR's automated review correctly identified the exact failure mode ("any control depending on bubble-phase-first ordering will break") as its single non-blocking note on an otherwise "ready" review. Treating it as answered by well-reasoned-sounding text would have shipped 2 confirmed regressions across a 50+-call-site shared primitive. The actual investigation (grep every Escape+stopPropagation handler, trace real JSX nesting into actual `<Modal>` call sites, empirically verify DOM event-phase semantics with a standalone repro) took real effort but found concrete, cited, file:line regressions — not hypothetical ones — in under 10 minutes of agent time. For any review flag on a change touching 5+ call sites, budget for this kind of trace-through before replying, especially when no human is watching to catch a wrong "safe as shipped" reply.
- **A dispatched investigation/Explore subagent given Bash access can mutate the orchestrator's own main checkout even without Edit/Write tools** if not isolated into its own worktree: this run, an Explore-type agent asked to "check out the PR branch or diff against dev-paul" ran a `git checkout <ref> -- <paths>` (or equivalent) directly in the shared `/home/user/SpartBoard` working directory, staging 3 files' fixed-branch content onto `dev-paul` without switching HEAD or creating any commit — caught only because the harness's own stop-hook flagged uncommitted changes. No work was lost (the content was a duplicate of what was already safely committed on the PR branch), and `git restore --staged --worktree <paths>` cleanly reverted it since `git log` confirmed HEAD never moved. Lesson: any subagent instructed to inspect a different branch's diff, even read-only in intent, should either be told to use read-only commands explicitly (`git diff branch1..branch2 -- path`, `git show branch:path`) or be isolated into its own worktree — Bash access alone is enough to mutate shared state regardless of Edit/Write tool restrictions.
- **Native `fetch()`'s SSRF guard is `redirect: 'manual'`, not `maxRedirects: 0` — an axios-focused SSRF sweep will miss fetch-based clients entirely**: this codebase's established SSRF-guard pattern (`embedProxy.ts`, audited repeatedly since 2026-06-11) is axios's `maxRedirects: 0`. The new LTI 1.3 integration (`ags.ts`/`nrps.ts`) uses the native `fetch()` API instead of axios, which has no `maxRedirects` option at all — the equivalent guard is `redirect: 'manual'` in the `fetch()` init. A prior SSRF audit (2026-07-19) that confirmed "the two blocklist-validated fetch call sites... both guarded" was specifically about axios call sites; it would not have caught this fetch-based gap even if re-run verbatim. Confirmed 2026-08-11 via a real HTTP-server repro (302 to `169.254.169.254`, cloud-metadata address) — default `fetch()` followed it, `redirect: 'manual'` refused (#2433). Any future SSRF sweep of `functions/src/**` must grep for BOTH `axios(...)`/`axios.get/head(...)` missing `maxRedirects: 0` AND bare `fetch(...)` missing `redirect: 'manual'` — they are two different guards for two different HTTP clients, not interchangeable, and a sweep scoped to only one client family will silently miss the other.
- **A Backlog row can go stale via a direct human commit to `dev-paul`, not just a later nightly run** — same lesson as the 2026-07-18 "row never cross-referenced" incident, new mechanism: the `hasOpenModalRef`/group-build Escape-ordering row (flagged 2026-08-05, "unconfirmed reachability") was silently resolved by `d353eca` (2026-08-07, a direct maintainer commit addressing PR #2412 review feedback) — nobody ever struck the row through because no nightly PR touched it. Caught only because the 2026-08-11 dashboard-layout agent happened to re-read the current source before investigating and found the guard already correctly ordered. Every run's backlog-lead investigation should diff against CURRENT `dev-paul` source, not assume last-known status still holds — a row can be resolved by any commit to the branch, nightly or otherwise.
- **Stating the background-task-notification constraint in the dispatch prompt is not sufficient to prevent it — the orchestrator must actively watch for and interrupt the stall**: despite the 2026-07-05 lesson being folded directly into every area's dispatch prompt verbatim ("do not use run_in_background for your own validate/build/lint calls... only the top-level orchestrator receives background-task notifications"), 2 of 5 subagents this run (dashboard-layout, admin-config) still backgrounded their own `pnpm run validate`/lint and then reported "I'll wait for the monitor notification" or "waiting for the lint job to finish via the monitor" — stalling with real, uncommitted, already-correct fixes sitting in their worktrees. A `SendMessage` correction telling them to re-run synchronously worked for zero of the two attempts made (both stalled a 2nd time, one on a different backgrounded command). What actually worked: the orchestrator directly inspecting the worktree's `git diff` (uncommitted but complete and correct in both cases), independently verifying fail-before/pass-after by hand, trimming style issues, running `pnpm run validate`/`build` itself, and committing — treating the subagent's stall as "finished-but-unreported work" (same recovery pattern as the 2026-07-15 container-restart incident) rather than attempting a 3rd redispatch round. Future dispatch prompts should additionally warn subagents that after 2 self-corrections they should just leave the diff uncommitted-but-complete and stop, rather than looping on the same mistake — and orchestrators should default to direct takeover on the 2nd stall instead of a 2nd correction attempt.
- **The orchestrator itself fell into the exact "manual `nohup cmd &` inside `run_in_background`" trap this doc already warns about (2026-07-13 note)** — Phase 0's baseline validate was launched as `nohup pnpm run validate > log 2>&1 &` wrapped inside a `run_in_background: true` Bash call. The harness's background-task notification fired almost instantly reporting "exit code 0," but the log had only captured ~15 lines (through the start of `tsc --noEmit`) — the outer wrapper (which forks the real process with `&` and returns immediately) is what the harness actually tracked, not the real `pnpm` process, which was still running 20+ minutes later (confirmed via `pgrep`/`ps aux`, PID still alive mid-`tsc`). Recovered by polling `while kill -0 <real-pid>; do sleep 15; done` in a *plain* (no manual `&`) `run_in_background: true` call, which correctly tracked the real work to completion. **Do not combine manual shell backgrounding (`&`, `nohup ... &`, `setsid`) with the harness's own `run_in_background` parameter — pick exactly one.** The harness parameter alone is sufficient and already survives container restarts (per the 2026-08-04 note); adding manual backgrounding on top of it breaks the harness's own completion tracking.
- **Zero subagent background-wait stalls this run — first time in many consecutive runs** (compare 2026-08-04 through 2026-08-12, where 2-4 of 5 agents stalled every single night despite the same warning): all 5 area agents ran their own `pnpm run validate`/build fully synchronously and returned real, complete results with no orchestrator nudge needed. The only prompt change from recent nights was making the constraint more concrete and specific — explicitly stating "there is no notification mechanism available to you," giving a specific generous timeout number (600000ms) to use instead of backgrounding, and naming `Monitor`/`run_in_background` explicitly as the tools to avoid rather than describing the failure mode abstractly. Not proof this is fully solved (sample size of one run), but promising enough to keep this exact phrasing in future dispatch prompts rather than reverting to the shorter version.
- **A single explicit `SendMessage` correction CAN recover a stalled subagent, contradicting the 2026-08-12 "zero of two attempts" data point**: 2026-08-15's dashboard-layout subagent hit the identical background-task-notification stall (reported "waiting for the background lint verification... before proceeding"), despite the same dispatch-prompt warning. One `SendMessage` telling it (1) it cannot receive background-task notifications as a subagent, (2) to read the backgrounded task's output file directly instead of waiting, and (3) to run all subsequent validate/build/lint calls as synchronous foreground commands — resumed it successfully to a complete, independently-verified delivery (34 tests, full validate/build green) with no further intervention. Sample size is still small (1-for-1 this run vs. 0-for-2 the 2026-08-12 run), so the orchestrator's default should stay "watch closely and take over directly on a 2nd stall" per the prior note — but a single well-specified correction is worth trying once before assuming takeover is required.
- **2026-08-18: `SendMessage` correction for the background-task-notification stall went 2-for-2** — the build-tooling subagent hit the identical stall ("I'll pause here and wait for the background `pnpm run validate` monitor notification"); one correction message (same 3-part structure as 2026-08-15's) resumed it to a complete, independently-verified delivery with no further intervention. Running tally: 3-for-4 successful single-correction recoveries across the two nights this has been tracked. A DIFFERENT failure mode also appeared this run for the first time: the widgets subagent was killed mid-run by a genuine transient API error (`Server error mid-response`), not a self-inflicted stall — distinguishable in the task-notification's `status` field (`failed` with an API-error summary, vs. `completed` with a stall-shaped final message). Recovery was the same shape regardless: a `SendMessage` telling the agent its worktree likely still has the in-progress uncommitted work, to check `git status`/`git diff` first, then continue synchronously — worked on the first attempt. Treat any interrupted-subagent notification (whether self-stalled or API-killed) as "probably recoverable via one targeted resume," not as a redispatch-from-scratch situation.
- **A backlog row's "too large / architectural, re-confirmed Nx" status can rest on a premise nobody re-derived** — the Stations widget name-collision bug (flagged 2026-07-20, re-confirmed "too large" 2026-07-31 and again as a standing backlog item) was assessed as requiring a cross-widget rework of `nexus.ts`/Randomizer's nameless `RandomGroup.names` every time it was looked at, but nobody had actually re-read `nexus.ts`/`stationsActions.ts` to check whether that premise still held. It didn't: both files already treat assignment-map keys as opaque strings, so the "large" fix was actually a same-file, same-night, contained change. Lesson: when re-confirming a "too large / architectural, skip" backlog item for the Nth time, don't just re-read the bug description — re-derive the specific dependency claim that makes it "too large" against CURRENT source. A claim can go stale exactly like a "still open" status can.
- **Running the orchestrator's own independent verify passes (`pnpm run validate` + `build`) across all N worktrees in parallel hits the same OOM contention as N subagents doing it simultaneously** — 3 of 5 orchestrator-launched verify runs this night were killed (exit 137) mid-`eslint` when launched together right after all 5 subagents had just finished (residual memory pressure + the verify runs themselves stacking). Sequential retries (one worktree at a time, `free -h` confirming memory was clear first) passed cleanly on the first attempt every time. Lesson: the "don't run 5 heavy `pnpm run validate`s at once" rule from 2026-07-04 applies to the orchestrator's OWN verification phase, not just subagent dispatch — default to sequential (or at most 2-at-a-time) for the Phase 2 re-verify step, not full-parallel.
- **Same-base-commit collision between unmerged nightly runs is a real, now-observed failure mode, not just a theoretical risk**: 2026-08-15's run branched every area's worktree from the same `efc375e` `dev-paul` commit as 2026-08-14's run, because none of 2026-08-14's 4 PRs (#2452–#2455) had merged yet. Consequence, caught only by automated PR review (not by the orchestrator's own Phase 2 verification, which has no visibility into other open PRs): the Widgets sweep independently re-found and re-fixed the exact bug 2026-08-14's sweep had already fixed (`RandomGroups.tsx` color-picker Escape), producing a strictly worse duplicate (#2459, missing the `isEscapeFromWidgetInput` guard #2452 had) that had to be closed; and both nights' log PRs (#2455, this run's #2464) independently bumped the same `Run count` line and struck the same backlog row, guaranteeing a merge conflict and a contradictory PR-attribution if merged out of order. Recovered by rebasing this run's log branch onto the prior unmerged log branch (`nightly/debugger-log-2026-08-14`) rather than `dev-paul` directly, renumbering this run 40 (not 39), and re-attributing the Widgets backlog row to #2452. **Standing recommendation, now with a concrete incident behind it**: either merge each night's PRs same-day before the next run dispatches, or have each run's Phase 0 check for open `nightly/*` PRs targeting `dev-paul` and branch from the most recent one's head instead of `dev-paul` directly when any are still open.
- **The same-base-commit collision recurred a THIRD consecutive night (2026-08-16) despite two prior nights documenting it — the standing recommendation still has not been implemented, and this run made the same mistake the 2026-08-15 note explicitly warned against**: this run's Phase 0 branched every area's worktree from `dev-paul` at `efc375e` — the *same* commit 2026-08-14 and 2026-08-15 both used — without first checking for open `nightly/*` PRs, reproducing the exact failure the prior note recommended checking for. Consequence: Widgets re-found/re-fixed `RandomGroups.tsx`'s color-picker Escape bug for the SECOND re-discovery in as many nights (closed in favor of #2452, same as 2026-08-15's #2459), and Admin & Config independently re-found/re-fixed `CalendarConfigurationModal.tsx`'s UTC-date bug already fixed by #2454 two nights earlier (closed in favor of #2454) — both caught only by an automated PR review's `git merge-tree` conflict check, not by this run's own process, exactly as before. The doc-update PR itself (this one) ALSO collided three ways with the still-open #2455 (run 39) and #2464 (run 40) log branches, requiring a full rebase-and-reconcile of this file rather than a simple append — the same class of problem the 2026-08-15 note predicted for the log PRs specifically. **This note is being recorded a third time because the fix genuinely requires a human decision outside any single nightly run's ability to self-correct**: a run branching from `dev-paul` cannot know about a sibling run's unmerged PRs without an explicit Phase 0 step to check for them (`gh pr list --base dev-paul --head "nightly/*"` or equivalent), and that step has been recommended, not implemented, twice already. Until it is, expect this to keep recurring roughly nightly whenever same-day merges don't happen.
- **Running 4-5 full-repo `pnpm run validate` calls in parallel during orchestrator re-verification triggered a genuine OOM kill (exit 137), a new failure mode distinct from the previously-documented CPU-contention timeout flakiness**: after Phase 2 review, re-running all 4 fixed areas' final `pnpm run validate` concurrently killed 2 of 4 (`admin-config`, `dashboard-layout`) with `Killed`/exit 137 mid-`eslint . --max-warnings 0`, while system memory bottomed out around 1.6GB free (each ESLint process alone was using 4-5GB RSS at peak). Recovery: re-ran the 2 killed ones sequentially once memory freed up — do not treat exit 137 as a code problem needing investigation, and do not assume a concurrency level that worked for Phase 1 subagent dispatch (staggered by each worktree's own `pnpm run install:all` completion time) is also safe for Phase 2/3's synchronized final-gate validates. Check `free -h` and cap true concurrency at 2 when re-verifying multiple areas' final `pnpm run validate`+`pnpm run build` back-to-back.
- **5 of 5 subagents stalled on the backgrounded-validate failure mode this run, the worst rate observed yet — treat this as a structural harness gap the dispatch prompt cannot fully close, not a prompt-wording problem**: contradicts 2026-08-15's "zero stalls" data point (which credited more concrete prompt phrasing) — this run used comparably explicit phrasing and still saw every single subagent end at least one turn waiting on a background-task/Monitor notification it could never receive. Two needed two rounds of correction each; the Build & Tooling agent burned ~300k tokens/159 tool calls in the stalled state with zero committed or uncommitted changes before a correction forced a decision (which turned out to be a legitimate "clean" report — sunk token cost is not a reason to manufacture a finding). Sample-size-of-one improvements (2026-08-15) and regressions (this run) both being observed suggests prompt wording alone does not reliably control this; budget for at least one, likely two, correction round-trips per subagent as the baseline expectation on every run until the harness itself changes.
- **Same-base-commit collision recurred a 4th time (2026-08-18), this time against a genuinely concurrent same-day run (2026-08-17) rather than a prior unmerged night — and when it recurs, "which fix is stronger" is NOT always the run's own PR**: this run's Phase 0 missed the standing open-PR-check recommendation (see the new top-of-file warning) because its Notes & Gotchas read stopped at a fixed line-count limit before reaching the two prior warnings about this exact failure mode — a process gap in the *reading*, not a missing instruction. The 2026-08-17 run below DID implement the check and found nothing blocking at the time — but a run dispatched hours later from the same base commit is invisible to any point-in-time PR check, so the two collided anyway. Consequence: 3 of 5 areas (Widgets, Admin & Config, Build & Tooling) duplicated bugs already fixed by the unmerged 2026-08-17 run (#2478/#2481/#2482 vs. this run's #2486/#2489/#2490). Resolution required empirically cross-testing each pair rather than assuming either side's PR automatically wins: for Widgets and Build & Tooling, this run's own fix was independently confirmed stronger (ported the other run's extra test scenarios onto this run's fix, all passed; the other run's fix had a real, self-documented gap in each case) — closed the other run's PRs. For Admin & Config, this run's OWN fix was the weaker one: the other run's capture-phase `useCaptureEscape` fix (#2481) solved a more severe, correctly-diagnosed root cause (`AdminSettings.tsx`'s own `document`-level Escape handler, which mounts before any nested popover and can't be stopped by a later same-target `stopPropagation()` call) that this run's fix — despite passing its own regression test — left completely unfixed, confirmed by writing a standalone test that reproduced `AdminSettings`'s handler registration order and applying each fix in turn. Closed this run's own #2489 in favor of #2481. **Lesson**: when reconciling a same-base-commit collision, do not default to "keep the run's own PR" — write a shared, mechanism-accurate test for the ACTUAL observed bug and apply each candidate fix to it; the orchestrator's own subagent can misdiagnose a bug's root cause just as easily as a concurrent run can, and only empirical cross-testing catches that. A point-in-time open-PR check (like 2026-08-17's, below) reduces but cannot eliminate this risk against a truly concurrent same-day run — only same-day merging before the next dispatch closes the gap completely.
- **This run's own log PR also collided with the 2026-08-17 run's log PR** — both edited the same `docs/routines/debugger.md` lines in opposite directions (struck vs. un-struck backlog rows for #2478/#2482, depending which run's PRs were known to have shipped at merge time). Resolved by merging the 2026-08-17 log branch into this one directly (not waiting for a human to hit the conflict at merge time) and reconciling by hand: kept both runs' unique Run Log/Backlog/Notes content, ordered 2026-08-17 rows before 2026-08-18's, and rewrote the handful of rows both runs touched to reflect the actual final state (which PR shipped, which was closed and why) rather than either run's incomplete point-in-time view. **Lesson**: when a same-base-commit collision is discovered, proactively merge the other run's log branch into your own and resolve by hand — don't just leave a note for the human reviewer to untangle a real conflict later.
- **First fully clean dispatch in 5 consecutive nights — zero same-base-commit collision, zero subagent background-wait stall requiring correction**: all 5 area subagents delivered complete, self-verified reports on their first background completion notification with no `SendMessage` nudge needed (dispatch prompt used the same explicit "there is no notification mechanism available to you... 600000ms timeout... do not use `run_in_background`/`Monitor`" phrasing credited for 2026-08-15's zero-stall run). Phase 0's open-`nightly/*`-PR check against `dev-paul` found nothing at dispatch time, and — unlike 2026-08-18's incident — no other debugger run collided later the same night either. Two of tonight's 4 shipped fixes (`#2496` State & Data, `#2498` Widgets) independently targeted the SAME underlying bug class (name-keyed `assignments`/PII in Stations-family widgets) from two different angles (scrub-side allowlist gap vs. producer-side cross-widget write path) with zero file overlap — confirmed via diff inspection before dispatch even finished, not after a collision; this is what independent-but-complementary discovery looks like when it works, contrasted with the actual duplicate-bug collisions documented on 2026-08-14 through 2026-08-18.
- **A genuinely separate, unrelated nightly routine (`docs/routines/unifier.md`, the accessibility/consistency-sweep sibling of this debugger routine) ran concurrently the same night and is NOT a same-base-commit collision to reconcile** — its PRs (#2494/#2495, `nightly/unify-settings-labels-2026-08-19`/`nightly/unifier-log-2026-08-19`) surfaced in this run's Phase-0 `nightly/*`-PR check alongside this run's own branches. Confirmed via `get_files` that #2494 touches only `Settings.tsx` files in 3 widgets (`Checklist`, `MathToolInstance`, `RevealGrid`) unrelated to any of tonight's debugger fixes, and #2495 touches only `docs/routines/unifier.md`, not `debugger.md` — zero overlap, zero action needed. **Lesson for future Phase-0 checks**: a `nightly/*`-prefixed branch from a sibling automated routine (this repo apparently runs at least two: `nightly/debugger-log-*` and `nightly/unifier-log-*`) is not automatically a same-base-commit collision risk — check the PR's actual files/title before assuming it's a competing instance of THIS routine; only `nightly/<area>-*`/`nightly/debugger-log-*` branches are this routine's own.
- **A backlog item marked "too architecturally large, spans N widgets" can be RIGHT about the remaining scope even after most of it turns out fixable** — the 2026-08-18 assessment that Stations' name-collision bug was "too large" was fully disproven that same night (#2486); tonight's PII-scrub fix (#2496) similarly disproved that the *scrubbing* side of the Stations/LunchCount/SeatingChart custom-mode `assignments` gap needed a coordinated multi-widget fix (one conditional check in one shared utility closed it for all three, since the check keys on `config.rosterMode`, not widget type). But the *producer* side investigated fresh tonight (a stable synthetic-id scheme so custom-roster mode assignments aren't PII-by-construction in the first place) was investigated in full and reconfirmed genuinely architectural — not just re-asserted from a stale prior read. **Takeaway**: re-deriving a "too large" claim against current source (per the 2026-08-18 lesson) can legitimately re-confirm it as still-too-large for part of a bug's scope while disproving it for another part of the same bug's scope in the same night — these aren't contradictory outcomes, they're two different sub-claims that happen to share a backlog row.
- **A subagent that finds no live bug across every dispatched lead can still deliver real, well-verified value — but that value shouldn't automatically become a PR**: Build & Tooling investigated 4 backlog leads, found all 4 already clean/covered, and instead of stopping at "clean," wrote and fully verified a 9-test emulator regression suite for a rule that was already correct (`lti_session_memberships` deny-all). This technically satisfies the mechanical "fail-before/pass-after" ship-gate criterion (achieved by deliberately weakening the already-correct rule to prove the test catches a regression) but does NOT satisfy "a real, verified bug" — the rule was never broken. Orchestrator judgment call: did not open a PR for it (per the explicit no-artificial-work guardrail), but did push the branch (no PR) so the verified commit isn't lost, and logged it in the Backlog for a future run to fold in or a maintainer to cherry-pick directly. **Standing guidance for future Build & Tooling dispatch prompts**: when offering a "cheap completeness" backlog lead like a rules-test-coverage gap as a fallback option, make explicit in the prompt that shipping it as this area's sole result should default to "report clean, note the completed test as a backlog item" rather than "open a PR" — the ambiguity this run resolved by orchestrator judgment call could be closed at the prompt level instead.
- **Automated PR review caught a real, orchestrator-missed correctness gap in tonight's own Widgets fix (#2498), same failure shape as the 2026-08-18/#2478 incident below**: `RandomSettings.tsx`'s `rosterNameToId` construction was gated only on the TARGET Stations widget's `rosterMode`, not the SOURCE Random widget's own `rosterMode` — if Random is in its own freeform custom mode while Stations is in class mode, a manually-typed name that happens to match a real active-roster student's full name would be silently misattributed to that unrelated student's roster id. Neither the dispatching subagent's own test nor the orchestrator's independent fail-before/pass-after re-verification exercised this cross-widget-mode-mismatch case (both only tested same-mode scenarios). Fixed same-run: extracted the gating decision into a small, independently-testable pure function (`shouldResolveRosterNames(sourceRosterMode, targetRosterMode)` in `nexus.ts`, mirroring the existing pattern of pulling testable logic out of `Settings.tsx` into `nexus.ts`) with 5 new unit tests covering all 4 mode combinations plus the `undefined`-defaults case. **Takeaway, reinforcing the 2026-08-18 lesson**: a subagent's fail-before/pass-after test reliably covers the specific bug it was dispatched to fix, but not every mode/branch combination touched by the fix — for any fix threading a NEW cross-component condition (here: two widgets' independent `rosterMode` fields), explicitly enumerate the full combination matrix as a checklist before considering the fix complete, not just the one path in the original bug report.
- **A second automated review round on #2498 caught a SECOND, distinct correctness gap in the same fix, after the first round's fix had already shipped**: the first review round caught the source/target `rosterMode` gating gap (fixed via `shouldResolveRosterNames`, see above); a follow-up review round on that SAME fix then caught that `targetRoster = rosters.find(...) ?? rosters[0]` disagreed with how this file resolves the active roster everywhere else (no fallback) — when `activeRosterId` is stale/unset with 2+ rosters, this silently keyed assignments against an arbitrary, unrelated class, reopening the exact misattribution risk the first fix closed, just via a different route. Fixed same-run by dropping the fallback (degrades to the safe name-key path instead of guessing). **Takeaway, extending both the 2026-08-18 and this run's earlier #2498 lesson**: iterative review depth compounds even within a SINGLE PR's post-push review cycle, not just across a multi-round PR lifetime (#2150's 5-round pattern) — a fix for one instance of a bug class can leave a second, structurally different instance of the SAME underlying risk (name/id misattribution) reachable through a different code path in the same function. Don't treat a review-fix round as closing the door on further findings in the identical area; watch subsequent review passes on your own repair commits just as closely as the original.
- **Comment-length review findings landed on 2 of 4 shipped PRs tonight (#2496, and this doc records #2499's separate reuse finding), both correctly caught despite the orchestrator's own Phase-2 review only checking Dashboard & Layout's comments**: the orchestrator trimmed Dashboard & Layout's 4-line comment during Phase 2 (per the established pattern) but did not re-check State & Data's `dashboardPII.ts` for the same issue before shipping #2496 — a 3-line `NOTE:` block and a 10-line JSDoc both slipped through, caught only by the async review pass. **Standing lesson, worth promoting from "trim what you notice" to "checklist item"**: Phase 2 review must explicitly scan EVERY shipped file's new/changed comments for length, not just the one file that happened to catch the orchestrator's eye — the CLAUDE.md one-line rule applies repo-wide, and a subagent violating it is now confirmed to happen more often than not (this run: 2 of 4 areas, on top of prior runs' "5 of 5 subagents" data point).
- **A review suggestion to "simplify a test to match the codebase convention" can silently defeat the regression it's protecting — verify empirically, don't just apply it**: a non-blocking nit on #2509 suggested replacing `exportFilename.test.ts`'s `Date`-getter-stubbing with a plain `new Date(2026, 5, 15)` (matching `utils/localDate.test.ts`'s simpler style). Applying it directly, then independently re-running the "fail-before" check against the buggy implementation, showed the simplified test PASSED on both the buggy and fixed code — because this repo pins `TZ=UTC` for all tests (`tests/setTz.ts`), so a plain local-constructed `Date` has no UTC offset to expose, and this specific test needs to simulate one. Reverted to the getter-stubbing approach with a one-line comment explaining why, and replied to the reviewer with the empirical evidence rather than a bare "I'll pass on this." Lesson, extending the 2026-07-13 "check reviewer suggestions against actual lint rules" note: the same applies to test-*simplification* suggestions — a suggestion that looks like it "exercises the same regression more simply" can actually exercise no regression at all under this repo's specific test-environment constraints (TZ pinning being the recurring one). Always re-run the fail-before check after applying a reviewer's test-style suggestion, not just the pass-after check.
- **A `Type`-derived default fix can introduce a NEW gap for a `Type` value the original bug report's test didn't cover — enumerate every branch, not just the reported one**: #2511's fix correctly closed "New building defaults to hardcoded K-5 regardless of Type" for `elementary`/`middle`/`high`, but `gradeLabelFromType('other')` already returned `''` (unchanged, pre-existing) — before the fix, ALL types defaulted to the hardcoded `'K-5'` fallback (wrong for `'other'`, but at least non-empty); after the fix, an `'other'`-type building left blank now saved `grades: ''`, which `buildingRecordToBuilding()` turns into an empty `gradeLevels` array, silently hiding every grade-gated widget for that building. Caught by automated review, not by the subagent's or orchestrator's own re-verification (both only tested the reported `high`-type scenario). Fixed by giving `'other'` an explicit non-empty default (all 4 grade bands / `'K-12'`), matching this same file's own established precedent for a building that doesn't fit one band (the seeded "Orono Community Education" building). **Takeaway, a fresh instance of the "enumerate every branch a fix touches" family (2026-08-19's `shouldResolveRosterNames` 4-combination lesson, 2026-08-18's Stations 4-write-branch lesson)**: any fix that changes a per-`Type`/per-enum-value default must be checked against EVERY value of that enum, not just the specific value named in the original bug report — a 4-case switch has 4 cases to verify, and "the reported case now works" is not the same claim as "the fix is complete."
- **Zero subagent background-wait stalls this run — 3rd time this specific dispatch-prompt phrasing has produced a fully clean run** (joins 2026-08-15 and 2026-08-19): all 5 area subagents (including the one reporting clean) delivered complete, self-verified results on their first background-completion notification, no `SendMessage` correction needed for any of them. Continues to support keeping this exact phrasing ("there is no notification mechanism available to you... 600000ms timeout... do not use `run_in_background`/`Monitor`") in future dispatch prompts over the shorter/more abstract versions that produced worse rates on other nights (2026-08-05: 3/5 stalled; 2026-08-19: 5/5 stalled with otherwise-similar wording) — sample size remains too small across non-identical prompt wordings to call this fully solved, but the concrete-and-specific phrasing keeps outperforming vaguer phrasing whenever tried.
- **A rules-tightening pass that adds a new denial condition must be checked against every fire-and-forget client write to that collection, not just against the rules-test suite**: #2512's `pollVotes` shape-validation fix is logically correct and empirically verified against the emulator, but `hasOnly([...])` applies to the MERGED RESULT of an `update`, not just the write payload — so any pre-existing document that already carries a field outside the new allowlist (from before the rule existed, or a manual edit) has every future write against it silently denied. The client's own write (`PollWidget.vote()`) was `void setDoc(...)` with no `.catch` — a rejected write became an unhandled promise rejection while the UI had already optimistically shown the action as succeeded. Caught only by automated review, not by the orchestrator's own emulator-based verification (which only exercises the rule in isolation, not the client's error-handling path around it). Fixed by adding error logging to the write (not by reverting the optimistic UI update, which is a larger UX change) and a test pinning the documented edge case. **Standing checklist item for any future `firestore.rules` write-rule tightening**: grep the write's client call site for `.catch`/error handling before considering the fix complete — a rule that's newly strict needs its client caller to newly handle rejection, and "the rules test suite is green" does not prove that.
- **The standing "branch from an unmerged sibling PR's head" recommendation (recorded repeatedly since 2026-08-15, followed inconsistently since) was followed cleanly tonight for the first time across all 4 areas with an open PR**: run 45's 4 area PRs (#2509-#2512) and its log PR (#2513) were all still open/unmerged at tonight's dispatch, with `dev-paul` unchanged since run 45 branched (`c0eeb0a`). Rather than re-dispatching from `dev-paul` and risking the same-base-commit duplicate-bug collision documented for 2026-08-14 through 2026-08-18, each area's worktree branched from its own run-45 PR head (State & Data — clean last night, no PR — branched from `dev-paul` directly), and this log branch was based on `nightly/debugger-log-2026-08-20` rather than `dev-paul`. Zero duplicate findings this run: all 5 areas found genuinely new, distinct bugs from what run 45 had already fixed. This is the cleanest evidence yet that the recommendation, when actually followed, eliminates the collision class entirely rather than just reducing its odds — the residual risk it does NOT cover (a truly concurrent same-day dispatch from a different session, per 2026-08-18) is a different failure mode with no full fix short of same-day merging.
- **Orchestrator-driven comment-length trimming is now a routine, expected part of Phase 2, not an occasional catch**: of the 5 shipped fixes tonight, 4 (Widgets was the only exception) needed at least one multi-line comment block trimmed to one line before shipping — `DialogContainer.tsx`/`DialogContext.tsx`/`DialogContextValue.ts` (State & Data, 3 separate blocks), `ConnectionsTab.test.tsx` (Admin & Config, 1 block), `AnnotationOverlay.test.tsx` (Dashboard & Layout, 3 blocks — with one pre-existing multi-line comment on a neighboring, untouched test correctly left alone since it predated this run), and `recount-org-members.js`/`.test.ts`/`organizationMemberCounters.test.ts` (Build & Tooling, 4 blocks across 3 files). Extends the 2026-08-19 lesson ("2 of 4 areas" that night) — tonight's 4-of-5 rate confirms this is now the norm, not the exception, and reinforces that Phase 2 must scan every new/changed file's comments in every shipped PR, not just the one that happens to catch the eye first.
- **Background-wait stall recurred at 5-for-5 tonight, but the single-`SendMessage`-correction recovery rate was also 5-for-5 — the highest reliability yet observed for that recovery path**: every one of tonight's 5 area subagents ended at least one turn waiting on a backgrounded `pnpm run validate`/`test` notification it could never receive (the same standing failure mode tracked since 2026-07-05), despite the dispatch prompt using the same concrete "there is no notification mechanism available to you... 600000ms timeout... do not use `run_in_background`/`Monitor`" phrasing credited for prior clean runs. Unlike some previous nights where a first correction sometimes needed a second follow-up, tonight every single agent fully self-recovered (checked the real PID/output file, continued synchronously, delivered a complete, independently-verifiable result) after exactly one `SendMessage` telling it (1) there is no notification mechanism for a subagent, (2) to poll the real process/output file directly instead of waiting, (3) to run all further validate/build/lint/test calls as synchronous foreground commands. Treat "every subagent will stall at least once, and one correction is very likely sufficient" as the standing operational baseline — budget for exactly one nudge per agent, not zero and not a takeover.
- **Comment-length trimming was needed in 4 of 4 shipped fixes tonight (100%), continuing the trend from "2 of 4" (2026-08-19) → "4 of 5" (2026-08-21) → "4 of 4" tonight**: every single shipped fix had at least one multi-line comment block that needed trimming to one line during Phase 2 review, including a from-scratch 16-line JSDoc header on a brand-new test file (`ShortLinkQuickCreate.escapePropagation.test.tsx`) that had no pre-existing convention to justify it. This is no longer worth tracking as a trend — treat "scan every new/changed file's comments in every shipped PR during Phase 2, expect at least one trim" as a fixed, permanent step of the review checklist, not a data point to keep collecting.
- **A file's own pre-existing test-body comment convention (not just a whole-file header convention) is a valid reason to leave a multi-line comment untrimmed**: `useNutrislice.test.tsx` already had several pre-existing 3-5 line comments explaining test rationale inside `it()` bodies (e.g. the legacy-shape migration test, the P1 stale-closure regression guard) before tonight's run touched it. Tonight's 2 new tests each added a similarly-sized comment — left untouched during review, since trimming them would have made the new tests inconsistent with their own file's established norm while the rest of the file's older comments stayed multi-line. This is a refinement of the existing "match the file's own convention" exception (previously applied only to whole-file header styles like `firestore.rules`/`tests/rules/*.test.ts`) — it applies at the level of individual pre-existing comments within a file, not just a file-wide header pattern.
- **OOM kills (exit 137) from concurrent nightly `eslint`/`vitest` processes are a shared-cgroup memory limit, not a system-wide RAM exhaustion**: confirmed 2026-08-22 by a subagent inspecting `/sys/fs/cgroup/memory/.../claude-code-bash` directly — all concurrent worktrees' Bash tool calls share one ~14.3GB cgroup cap, and 3-4 simultaneous `eslint`/`tsc` processes from sibling worktrees saturated it even though total system RAM (`free -h`) had headroom. This refines the standing "cap concurrency, check `free -h`" guidance from 2026-07-04/2026-08-18: also check `cat /sys/fs/cgroup/memory/*/memory.usage_in_bytes` (or equivalent) against its limit when `free -h` looks fine but a validate/lint step is still getting killed — the constraint can be narrower than the whole-machine view suggests.
- **`NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=8192` unblocked a standalone `eslint . --max-warnings 0` that was twice OOM-killed under sibling-worktree contention**: a subagent this run hit exit 137 on plain `pnpm run lint` twice, then succeeded by prefixing the same command with `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=8192`. Worth trying before assuming a lint failure needs code investigation, when the kill happens under known concurrent-worktree load (per the two OOM notes above) rather than in isolation.
- **A div-level `onKeyDown` Escape handler only fires if focus is already inside that div — opening an overlay does not move focus into it by default**: `ImportWizard.tsx`'s `AiAssistOverlay` (#2529) had its own `onKeyDown` calling `onCancel()` on Escape, correctly scoped and correctly `stopPropagation()`'d, but it never fired on the actual first-press path: nothing in the overlay (no `autoFocus`, no focus effect) moves focus into it on open, so focus stays on the trigger button that opened it — which is not a descendant of the overlay `div`, so React's synthetic `onKeyDown` (delegated at the root, but still keyed to the element that has focus) never reaches the overlay's handler at all. The bug reproduced on the MOST COMMON path (press Escape immediately after opening) despite passing a test that clicked into the textarea first. Caught only by automated review's own probe test, not by the orchestrator's independent re-verification (which reused the subagent's own textarea-focused test scenario).
