Two fixes both close it: (a) focus management — give the overlay `tabIndex={-1}` + focus it on mount, which also closes a real a11y gap (a `role="dialog"` that never receives focus is invisible to screen readers); or (b) a `document`/`window`-level listener scoped to the overlay's mount lifetime (matching `ShortLinkQuickCreate`'s pattern, #2531) — focus-independent by construction, the fix applied here. **Standing lesson**: any Escape/keyboard handler attached via a JSX element's own `onKeyDown` (rather than a `document`/`window` listener) is a bug waiting to happen unless something in the same component explicitly moves focus into that element on mount — check for `autoFocus`/a focus effect before trusting an element-level keyboard handler to fire at all, and prefer the document/window-listener idiom for any newly-opened overlay that doesn't already manage focus.

Same-day merging before the next dispatch is the only complete fix.

All 5 confirmed accurate — no fabricated or overstated fail-before claims this run.
- **Automated PR review (the `claude[bot]`-identity reviewer that runs on push) caught a real, orchestrator-missed regression in tonight's own Widgets fix (#2478)**: the Stations id-keying change's `setAssignment` unconditionally deleted `next[legacyName]` for cleanup, but in custom-list mode `id === name`, so it deleted the very key the same call had just written two lines above — silently breaking assignment persistence for the entire custom-roster mode. Neither the dispatching subagent's own tests nor the orchestrator's independent fail-before/pass-after re-verification exercised custom-list mode at all (only class-roster and legacy-name-key paths were tested), so this shipped in the initial push and was only caught by the async review pass after the PR was opened. Fixed same-run (`legacyName !== studentId` guard + a regression test for the custom-mode case) before merge. Same review pass also flagged a real-but-non-blocking correctness nuance in #2482 (see gcPlcOrphans Backlog row — now moot, superseded by #2490's approach) — comment corrected same-run. **Takeaway: subagents' own regression tests reliably cover the bug they were dispatched to fix, but not necessarily every code path touched by their fix — the automated post-push review is catching real gaps the orchestrator's spot-check re-verification (which re-runs the subagent's own tests, not fresh ones) structurally cannot.** Consider having subagents explicitly enumerate all modes/branches touched by a fix and add a test per mode, not just per bug.

- **A dispatch prompt's explicit "never `git stash`" reminder needs to be in the prompt itself, not assumed from institutional memory**: despite this doc's standing rule (documented since 2026-07-11/07-31, and followed correctly by the orchestrator's own re-verification every night since), tonight's Admin & Config subagent independently reached for `git stash` to test fail-before/pass-after — the dispatch prompt covered the commit-signing worktree workaround explicitly but not the stash rule. Caught and corrected via `SendMessage` (bundled with an unrelated background-wait-stall correction to the same agent); `git stash list` confirmed empty before continuing, no actual collision occurred this time. **Lesson**: fold the "never `git stash` — use `cp`/`git show HEAD:<path>` to back up instead" instruction directly into every area's dispatch prompt alongside the signing-workaround note, the same way the background-wait-stall warning already is, rather than relying on a subagent independently knowing or rediscovering it.
- **Refinement to the "branch from unmerged sibling PR head" technique, when the new fix touches disjoint files**: 2026-08-23 (run 48) branched Widgets/State & Data/Admin & Config from their run-47 sibling PR heads as usual (to avoid rediscovering the same bugs during the hunt), but since each area's fresh fix that night landed in files entirely disjoint from its sibling PR's changes, the orchestrator additionally rebased each finished, fully-verified fix commit onto latest `dev-paul` (`git rebase --onto dev-paul <sibling-PR-tip> <branch>`) before pushing/opening its PR. All 3 rebases completed with zero conflicts (confirming the disjoint-files assumption), and a full validate+build re-run post-rebase passed unchanged on all 3. Result: each PR's diff contains only that night's own fix, not entangled with the still-unmerged sibling PR's changes — cleaner for human review, and the PR no longer silently depends on the sibling PR merging first. **When to use this**: only after independently confirming (via the rebase itself — a conflict means real overlap) that the new fix doesn't touch the same files as the sibling PR; if a conflict occurs, fall back to the established practice of opening the PR from the sibling-branched commit as-is (accepting the diff includes both) rather than manually resolving a rebase conflict on unreviewed work.
- **`pnpm run test:rules -- <path>` does not scope to one file** — the script wraps the vitest invocation inside `firebase emulators:exec "... && node scripts/checkTestCounts.mjs rules"`, and passing extra positional args after `--` gets forwarded to `emulators:exec` itself, which rejects them ("Too many arguments. Run firebase help emulators:exec for usage instructions"). When re-verifying a rules-only fail-before/pass-after, run the full unscoped `pnpm run test:rules` (single-file rules suites are fast enough — under 90s for 49 files/1175 tests on this repo) rather than trying to target one file.
- **Background-wait stall rate this run: 2 of 5 (Widgets, State & Data), both recovered with exactly one `SendMessage` correction each** — Admin & Config also stalled once (bundled with the git-stash correction above), so 3 of 5 total needed at least one correction; Dashboard & Layout (clean) and Build & Tooling delivered complete results on their first completion notification with no correction needed. Consistent with the established ~50-60% baseline stall rate for this dispatch-prompt phrasing on nights that don't hit the "zero stalls" outlier — continues to support budgeting at least one correction round-trip per subagent as standard, not an exception.
- **Two of four run-48 PRs needed a post-push repair, both caught by automated review hours after the run's own Phase 2/3 closed** — same standing lesson as prior nights (2026-07-05's #2478, 2026-08-20's #2511, etc.), reconfirmed: a subagent's/orchestrator's own re-verification reliably covers the reported bug's specific repro, not every actor/branch/sibling-file instance the fix touches. On #2537, review found the CSV-export dedup fix's identical bug (plus a DISTINCT second defect — uncounted-duplicate double-counting, not just the same last-wins issue) in `PublishedGLReview`, a file outside every area's glob; fixed as a one-time out-of-glob exception per the #2213/#2432 precedent. On #2539, review found the new `update` rule's format check only guarded the `isDomainAdmin` branch, not the standalone `isSuperAdmin()` `||` term — a super admin could bypass the very invariant the PR set out to guarantee; fixed by restructuring to match `create`'s already-correct hoisted-condition shape. Both repairs: verified the reviewer's claim directly (reproduced fail-before against the pre-repair code) before fixing, added targeted regression tests, ran full validate/build(+test:rules) before pushing, then replied to and resolved the specific review thread. **Standing takeaway, reinforced**: for a PR touching either (a) a shared bug-class pattern with siblings outside the diff's own files, or (b) an authorization rule with multiple actor branches, explicitly enumerate every sibling instance / every actor branch as a checklist before considering Phase 2 review complete — not just the one instance/actor the dispatched fix's own test exercises.

- **Run 49 (2026-08-24): 4 bugs shipped + 1 clean, zero same-base-commit collision, zero subagent background-wait stall.** All 5 area subagents delivered complete, self-verified results on their first completion notification with no `SendMessage` correction needed (dispatch prompt kept the concrete "there is no notification mechanism available to you... 600000ms timeout... do not use `run_in_background`/`Monitor`" phrasing). Phase-0 branched Widgets/State & Data/Admin & Config from their run-48 PR heads, Dashboard & Layout (clean in run 48) from run-47's head, Build & Tooling from `dev-paul`; all 4 fix commits rebased `--onto dev-paul <sibling-tip>` conflict-free (confirming disjoint files) for clean minimal PR diffs. The log branch was based on `nightly/debugger-log-2026-08-23` and appended cleanly (no reconciliation needed — no concurrent same-day debugger run). Two subagents (Dashboard & Layout, State & Data) could NOT complete the full `pnpm run validate` in their own worktrees (each worktree's `functions/` had no `node_modules`, and full-repo eslint OOM'd in the constrained subagent env) — the orchestrator installed `functions/` deps per worktree (`pnpm -C functions install`) and ran the full validate+build itself for all 4 fixes, plus independent fail-before/pass-after by reverting each fixed file to its pre-fix version in-place (`git show <sibling-tip>:<path> > <path>` / `cp` restore, never `git stash`). Widgets and Admin & Config subagents installed their own `functions/` deps and ran full validate themselves. Dispatch prompts this run explicitly folded in BOTH the background-wait-stall warning AND (new) the "never `git stash`" instruction with the `cp`/`git show` alternative — zero stash incidents this run (contrast run 48, where the stash rule was missing from the prompt).
- **Automated review caught one real, orchestrator-missed correctness gap in run 49 (#2544 Widgets), matching the standing "review catches sibling/branch instances the fix's own test doesn't" lesson** — the HotspotImage Escape fix added a `document`-level keydown handler that called `stopPropagation()` on EVERY Escape while the popover was open, WITHOUT the `isEscapeFromWidgetInput` guard that every other popover in the codebase uses (and that the subagent's own report cited `CatalystSetPickerPopover` as the reference for — but the fix omitted the guard). Reachable: popover left open in the background + user hits Escape to blur an unrelated widget text input → handler swallows the event before `DashboardView`'s `isTypingFieldActive()` check, so the input fails to blur and the popover silently closes instead. Fixed same-run (`if (isEscapeFromWidgetInput(e)) return;` + import), added a 3rd regression test (Escape from an INPUT inside `[data-draggable-window]` must NOT close the popover and must NOT be stopped); orchestrator re-verified fail-before (new test fails without the guard: 1 failed/2 passed) → pass-after (3/3), plus a full validate+build re-run before pushing, then replied to and resolved the review thread. The other 3 PRs (#2545/#2546/#2547) were reviewed clean with no findings. **Reinforces**: for any fix that adds a NEW `document`/`window`-level Escape/keydown handler, the `isEscapeFromWidgetInput` guard is mandatory boilerplate — check it's present before considering the fix complete, don't rely on the fix's own popover-focused test (which never exercises Escape-from-another-widget-input).

- **A 3-night unmerged-PR backlog (runs 47-49) accumulated before run 50 even dispatched — the largest observed, and this doc's "same-day merging" mitigation has now visibly failed to keep pace for multiple consecutive nights**: at run 50's Phase-0 check, ALL of runs 47/48/49's area PRs (#2525-#2532 excluding the unifier/pr-review ones, #2536-#2540, #2544-#2548) — 12 area PRs plus 3 log PRs — were still open. `dev-paul` itself hadn't moved since run 46 (`25f6127`, 2026-08-21), meaning 4 full nightly runs' worth of verified, green-CI fixes were sitting unmerged simultaneously. The standing "branch from the latest unmerged sibling PR head" technique still worked mechanically (zero same-base-commit collisions this run — every area's fresh find was genuinely new, not a rediscovery), but this is a symptom-management workaround, not a fix: the backlog itself keeps growing every night nobody merges, and each additional unmerged night compounds the review burden for whoever eventually triages it. **This is a human-process gap this routine cannot self-correct** (per the explicit "never merge, never force-push to dev-paul" guardrail) — flagging prominently in the run summary is the only lever available; a maintainer needs to either merge the backlog down or explicitly pause nightly dispatch until it clears.
- **A subagent killed mid-response by a transient API error recovers identically to a self-inflicted background-wait stall — treat both the same way**: run 50's Dashboard & Layout agent was disconnected mid-tool-call ("Connection lost mid-response"), not by anything it did. One `SendMessage` telling it to check its worktree's `git status`/`git diff` for already-in-progress work and continue synchronously from there recovered it cleanly on the first attempt — it had already confirmed the target bug before the disconnect and picked up exactly where it left off. Same recovery shape as the 2026-08-18 API-kill precedent; worth remembering this failure mode is not rare (2 confirmed instances now) and the fix is always the same: assume uncommitted worktree progress survives, don't restart the investigation from scratch.
- **A genuine security bug (unnecessary `allow-same-origin` on a `srcDoc` iframe rendering untrusted AI-generated content) surfaced from an unprompted Widgets sweep, not a backlog lead** — none of the dispatch prompt's suggested leads pointed at `MiniApp/Widget.tsx`; the subagent found it independently while exploring the area cold. Reinforces that backlog leads are a floor, not a ceiling, for what a dispatched subagent should look at — a fresh, broad read of an area can still surface something no prior run's audit trail anticipated, including security-severity findings in years-mature, frequently-touched files.
- **Two subagents this run wrote a fresh multi-line comment block in production code despite the dispatch prompt's explicit one-line rule — orchestrator Phase-2 review is still catching this every run, not converging toward zero**: `organizationInvites.ts` (a 9-line block re-explaining the whitespace-trim rationale already partially covered by an adjacent pre-existing comment) and the brand-new file `actorBuildingScope.ts` (an 8-line JSDoc block with no established file convention to justify it, since the file didn't exist before this fix). Both trimmed to one line each during Phase 2, independently re-verified (lint/format clean, fail-before/pass-after re-run) before committing the trim. Consistent with the 2026-08-20 "4 of 5 areas" and 2026-08-19 "2 of 4 areas" data points — this remains a near-universal subagent habit; continue treating full-diff comment-length scanning as a mandatory Phase 2 checklist item, not a spot-check.
- **A div-level `onKeyDown` handler is unreachable for a keypress if nothing moved focus into that element — check for `autoFocus`/a focus effect before trusting a hand-picked-node test**: run 50's Dashboard & Layout agent independently rediscovered the `AiAssistOverlay` Escape-propagation bug already fixed on run 47's still-unmerged #2529, but implemented a DIFFERENT fix — `stopPropagation()` on the overlay `<div>`'s own `onKeyDown` — that doesn't work for real usage. The overlay opens via a trigger button; nothing moves focus into the overlay afterward (no `autoFocus`, no focus effect), so a real Escape keypress fires with focus still on the trigger button, outside the div's subtree, and DOM bubbling only propagates target→ancestors — the handler never runs. The shipped regression test passed anyway because it called `fireEvent.keyDown(textarea, ...)` — dispatching directly on a hand-picked descendant node instead of `document.activeElement`, synthesizing a target no real user interaction produces. #2529's actual fix (a `document`-level listener registered on mount, focus-independent by construction) was correct from the start. Caught only by automated review with a reproduced probe, not by either the subagent's or the orchestrator's own fail-before/pass-after re-verification (both re-ran the same hand-picked-node test). **Lesson, generalizing beyond this one bug**: any regression test for a keyboard handler that fires `fireEvent.keyDown` on a specific node (rather than `document.activeElement`) is only valid if something in the component actually moves focus to that node — verify the focus path exists (grep for `autoFocus`/`.focus()`/a mount effect) before trusting such a test's pass-after result as proof the fix works for real usage.
- **When a same-base-commit collision produces two DIFFERENT fixes for the same bug (not just a duplicate), empirically cross-test both against a focus-independent/mechanism-accurate probe before picking a side — "which PR is newer/more thorough" is not sufficient**: unlike prior collisions (#2452 vs #2459, #2486 vs #2478) where both sides implemented the same correct approach and the question was just which shipped first, this run's #2554 and #2529 diverged on actual mechanism (div-level vs. document-level listener) and only one worked. Resolution: reproduced the reported bug's real trigger-to-Escape flow as a standalone probe test, ran it against both branches, and let the empirical result decide — #2529 passed, #2554 failed, so #2554 was closed rather than "the newer one wins" or "keep both open for a human." Confirmed 2026-08-25: #2554 (this run's PR).

- **A backlog item's "likely unreachable" assessment can be flatly disproven by tracing one more hop through a sibling system's own documented behavior**: `useBuildingSelection.ts`'s non-empty→empty reset gap (flagged 2026-08-22 as "no confirmed live path found despite investigating hard") turned out to be directly reachable — `context/AuthContext.tsx` has an explicit, self-documented `permission-denied` branch that clears `orgId`/`roleId`/`buildingIds` on a revoked org membership mid-session ("Do NOT preserve access for a removed user"), which is exactly the trigger that drives `useAdminBuildings()` (this hook's sole real consumer path) to `[]`. The original investigation apparently stopped at "is `orgId` monotonic in normal operation" without checking whether `AuthContext` has an explicit non-monotonic escape hatch for exactly this scenario. Lesson, extending the 2026-08-18 "re-derive a stale claim against current source" pattern: a "likely unreachable" verdict on a hook fed by an upstream context should include an explicit grep of that context for every place it can reset/null the value in question, not just a plausibility argument about normal-path behavior.
- **A backlog row spanning multiple distinct findings from the same investigation night needs to be split when only some of those findings get fixed later, not treated as all-or-nothing**: the 2026-08-24 Dashboard & Layout row bundled `FolderPickerPopover.tsx`'s dead-orphan-subtree gap with `SegmentedControl.tsx`'s missing a11y keyboard contract as one row. Fixing `SegmentedControl.tsx` tonight (#2564) required splitting the row so the still-open `FolderPickerPopover`/`FolderTree` item doesn't read as fixed and the `SegmentedControl` item doesn't read as still-open. When a future run partially resolves a multi-finding row, split it rather than leaving one combined status that's half-wrong either way.
- **The Admin & Config building-ID-drift lead, tracked narratively across 3+ prior nights' Run Log entries rather than as its own Backlog row, is now confirmed exhausted**: tonight's agent traced every raw `buildingIds`/`building.id` consumer across `components/admin/**` (9 files) and found each one already routes through `canonicalBuildingId`/`canonicalizeBuildingIds` or is internally self-consistent — last night's `d2b9ca0` mopped up the final 3 raw comparisons. No Backlog row existed to strike through (the lead lived only in Run Log prose across #2547/#2555's entries), which made it easy to keep re-chasing; a lead that recurs across 2+ nights' fixes is worth promoting to its own trackable Backlog row even while still "in progress," so a future run can see at a glance whether it's still open without re-reading old Run Log entries.

- **`pnpm install:all` can silently under-install a real dependency in a fresh container even though the lockfile is correct**: run 52's very first `pnpm run validate` was RED (`tsc`: `Cannot find module 'pdfjs-dist'`) despite `pdfjs-dist@6.2.108` being correctly present in both `package.json` and `pnpm-lock.yaml` — the SessionStart hook's install reported "Lockfile is up to date, resolution step is skipped" and exited 0, but `pdfjs-dist` (added by the same-day `084b4a5` quiz-stimuli feature) was simply absent from `node_modules`. A plain `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` re-run immediately fixed it (added 3 files). **When a baseline `pnpm run validate` is red on a fresh container with a module-not-found error, re-run install before treating it as a real `dev-paul` bug** — check `pnpm ls <package>` returns empty despite the package being in `package.json`/the lockfile as the confirming signal, same class of issue as the documented worktree-signing/ESLint-`/tmp` environment quirks above.

- **Run 53 (2026-08-28): 5 bugs shipped, zero clean reports, zero same-base-commit collision.** All 5 areas found and shipped a genuine bug on the first pass, including a real Firestore security-rules gap in Build & Tooling (`sessions/{userId}/students` create+update, unvalidated since 2026-08-24 per `firestore-rules.md`) and a real cross-account data-leak in State & Data (unseeded, uncollision-checked live-session join codes). Two of tonight's fixes directly closed backlog rows that had been sitting open from run 52 the night before (`useLiveSession.ts` join-code collision, spotted-not-fixed 2026-08-27; and the Stations/LunchCount name-keyed-assignments row, which turned out to already be fixed as of #2486/2026-08-18 and reconfirmed clean twice since — a 3rd cold-read reconfirmation of an already-resolved lead, this time caught and struck through instead of left stale a 4th time). One background-wait stall (Dashboard & Layout), recovered with one `SendMessage` correction — consistent with the established ~20-60%-of-agents-per-run baseline.
- **Orchestrator's own Phase 2/3 re-verification hit the documented OOM pattern at 5-way concurrency, cleanly resolved by falling back to sequential retries**: running all 5 areas' independent `pnpm run validate` re-verification passes concurrently (right after 5 subagents had just finished, residual memory pressure stacking with the verify runs themselves — the exact 2026-07-19/2026-08-18 pattern) OOM-killed 3 of 5 (`admin-config`, `widgets`, `dashboard-layout`, exit 137 mid-`eslint`) while 2 (`state-data`, `build-tooling`) completed cleanly. All 3 killed runs passed cleanly on a sequential retry once the other jobs finished and `free -h` showed headroom — no code issue, purely contention. Reconfirms: default to sequential (or cap at 2) for the Phase 2/3 re-verify step even when Phase 1 dispatch itself didn't need throttling.
- **A backlog row can go stale from a WITHIN-BACKLOG cross-reference, not just a Run-Log-to-Backlog gap**: the Stations/LunchCount row (open since 2026-07-07) had already been reconfirmed fixed by a *later Backlog row itself* (added 2026-08-25, "confirmed fixed 2026-08-25 (stale row)") — but the original 2026-07-07 row was never struck through when that reconfirmation row was added, so it still read as "open, needs widget-level fix" and was investigated cold a 3rd time (tonight, run 53) before being caught. All the prior "stale row" incidents documented in this file (2026-07-18, 2026-08-18, 2026-08-25, 2026-08-27) were Run-Log-fixed-but-Backlog-row-never-struck-through; this is the first confirmed instance where the SAME table had two rows about the same claim and only the newer one got updated. When adding a reconfirmation/fixed note as a *new* backlog row rather than editing the original in place, always go back and strike through the original row in the same edit — a new row alone doesn't stop the original from being re-investigated.
- **"Traced every real client write path" must include a write that CAN target a pre-existing document, not just calls literally named `update`**: on #2625, the subagent (and the orchestrator's own re-verification) enumerated every `updateDoc()` call site in `hooks/useLiveSession.ts` and correctly restricted all of them to `status`-only — but missed that `joinSession`'s `setDoc(ref, fullShape)` ALSO evaluates against the `update` rule (not `create`) whenever the target doc already exists, which happens on any rejoin with persisted anonymous auth (a page refresh, the single most common real-world path for this feature). Firestore rules classify an operation as create/update based on whether the document existed before the write, not by which client SDK method was called — a `setDoc()` with no `{merge: true}` is a full overwrite from the app's perspective but is still an `update` at the rules layer if the doc pre-exists. Caught only by automated review, not by either the dispatching subagent's fail-before/pass-after (which only tested fresh `updateDoc({status})` calls) or the orchestrator's independent re-verification (which re-ran the same test scenarios). **Lesson, generalizing beyond this one bug**: when auditing a Firestore rules `update` block against "every real client write," explicitly check every `setDoc()` call in the audited hook/utility too, not just calls to `updateDoc()` — any `setDoc()` targeting a path that can already have a document (an upsert/rejoin/re-save pattern) is an update-rule write in disguise, and its full write shape (not just its "intended" delta) is what the rule must accept.
- **A one-shot `useState`/`useEffect` timer fix can leave a live-toggle path stale even when mount is correct**: on #2621, `ClockWidget`'s `time` state initializes correctly at mount (`useState(new Date())` runs fresh), but the seconds-hidden effect branch only scheduled a boundary-aligned `setTimeout`/`setInterval` — it never resynced `time` when the effect itself (re)started. Toggling `showSeconds` off via the live Settings panel shortly after a minute rollover left the previous minute on screen for up to 60s. The bug was invisible to the original fail-before/pass-after (which tested the steady-state tick rate, not the moment of transition) and to the orchestrator's own re-verification (which re-ran the same tests). **Lesson**: for any effect keyed on a config value that schedules a *deferred* first action (a `setTimeout`, a debounce, a boundary-aligned tick), test the instant the effect (re)starts — not just its steady-state behavior — especially when the same effect handles both "fresh mount" (where `useState`'s initializer already got it right) and "value changed after mount" (where nothing did).
- **`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rejects the "obvious" literal fix for exactly the class of bug above — reach for the adjust-state-while-rendering pattern instead**: the reviewer's own suggested fix (a synchronous `setTime(new Date())` at the top of the effect body) is real, correct-behaving code that this repo's lint config specifically rejects, because it causes a cascading extra render. The correct fix is CLAUDE.md's documented pattern for prop-driven state resets: track the previous value of the dependency in a `useRef`, compare during render, and call the setter immediately if it changed (not inside the effect). Doing so re-triggers the now-familiar `react-hooks/refs` v7 false positive on the render-body ref read AND write, both needing the standing `// eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/refs -- intentional render-body ref sync (CLAUDE.md pattern)` comment — this is the same disable-comment pattern documented since 2026-06-02, now confirmed to also apply to a ref used purely for previous-value comparison, not just the state-adjustment ref itself.
- **A concurrent automated session can apply a fix to a finding the orchestrator already investigated and declined** — not just re-fix an already-fixed bug (the previously-documented collision pattern) or duplicate a comment already handled (the already-documented "safe to skip" case). On #2621, a separate PR-babysitting session pushed a harmless no-op cleanup (dropping an unused import eslint was already tolerating via `jsx-uses-react`) minutes after the orchestrator posted a reply explaining why no change was needed. Both were factually correct — the import truly was unused and removing it is harmless, but was also not required for CI to pass. No conflict occurred and no reconciliation was needed since the change was strictly no-op, but it's a new variant of the concurrent-process family worth naming: two independent, correct analyses of the same finding can still reach different (but both defensible) action decisions.
- **Orchestrator's own `nohup cmd > log &` premature-notification quirk (documented 2026-07-13) recurred repeatedly this run, for BOTH the orchestrator's `pnpm run validate` calls AND `firebase emulators:exec` invocations**: every single one of tonight's 6+ backgrounded verification commands (baseline validate, 5 per-worktree validates, 3 emulator rules runs) fired a "command completed" notification while the real work (`node .../pnpm run validate`, or `node .../firebase.js emulators:exec`) was still running underneath, sometimes for many more minutes. The fix is always the same and now fully proven for `firebase emulators:exec` too, not just `pnpm run validate`: after backgrounding, `pgrep -af` for the actual long-running interpreter process (not the shell wrapper), confirm its `cwd` matches the intended worktree if multiple are running, then poll `kill -0 <real pid>` in a loop — never trust the tool's own "completed" notification timing for these two command shapes specifically.
- **Concurrent per-worktree `pnpm run validate` runs can be OOM-killed (`exit 137`/`Killed`), not just slowed or timed out**: distinct from the previously-documented CPU-contention timeout flakiness, tonight two orchestrator-launched `pnpm run validate` runs died with `Killed`/exit 137 during the `eslint` stage while 2-3 sibling worktrees' own validate/lint processes were running simultaneously (`free -h` showed ~8.7GB/15GB used at the time, `load average` 11-18). Simply retrying the identical command once `free -h`/`uptime` showed memory recovered (12GB+ free, load back under ~10) succeeded cleanly both times with no code changes. Lesson: an OOM kill during a concurrent multi-worktree validate run is not a signal to investigate the code — check `free -h` at the time of the kill, and if memory was tight, just retry once contention clears rather than treating it as a real failure needing root-causing.
- **Firestore-rules-emulator test flakiness reproduces even in near-isolation (2-file runs, low system load) against completely unmodified `dev-paul` rules — this is stronger evidence than the previously-documented "5+ concurrent worktrees" contention theory alone**: tonight's full 52-file `test:rules` run (build-tooling area, load already low, no other worktree emulators running) showed 2 failures in `firestore-rules-external-user.test.ts`/`firestore-rules-organizations.test.ts` — files completely unrelated to that run's `starterPacks` fix. Re-running just those 2 files in isolation reproduced 3 DIFFERENT specific test failures; re-running the same 2 files against literally unmodified `dev-paul` rules (file-swapped back, no fix applied at all) reproduced the same class of failure with yet another different specific test failing. This rules out "too many concurrent worktrees" as the sole cause — the emulator itself appears to have some baseline flakiness in this sandbox independent of contention level. Treat any `firestore-rules-organizations.test.ts`/`firestore-rules-external-user.test.ts` failure as a suspect-flake-first case (confirm by re-running against unmodified `dev-paul` rules before concluding a real regression), but this does NOT extend to other rules test files without their own re-confirmation — only these two have now been empirically shown flaky in isolation.
- **A nightly run's own "run 54" Run Log entries went missing from this doc, discovered only by cross-referencing Backlog/Notes mentions against the Run Log table** — run 54 (2026-08-29) is referenced by name in at least 2 Backlog rows (the AudioContext-consolidation re-confirmation, the `projectSessionStimuli` dedupe re-confirmation) and its log PR (#2645) shows as merged in `dev-paul`'s git history, but this file's Run Log table jumps directly from run 53's rows (2026-08-28) to run 55's (this run, 2026-08-30) with no run-54 rows in between. The most likely cause: run 54's log PR (#2645) and run 53's log PR (#2626) were created around the same time but merged out of order (#2645 first, then #2626), and whoever/whatever resolved the resulting table-conflict on the second merge kept only one side's new rows instead of both — the same failure class as the sibling `pr-review` routine's own documented incident (PR #2647, "restore run 2026-08-29 log entry lost in the #2627 merge"). Not reconstructed here — no source file preserves run 54's actual per-area findings/PR links, only secondhand mentions. **Flagging for a maintainer**: if run 54's original PRs (dated 2026-08-29, likely numbered before #2645/#2646) are still identifiable via `git log`/closed-PR search, their bodies could be used to manually reconstruct the missing Run Log rows. **Standing lesson for future runs**: when two nightly runs' log PRs are both open simultaneously (check Phase-0's own open-PR listing for multiple `nightly/*-log-*` or `nightly/debugger-log-*` branches), merge/rebase-check the second one against the first's already-merged content before finalizing, rather than assuming a plain merge preserves both sides' table insertions.
- **Fully clean dispatch phase: zero same-base-commit collision, zero subagent background-wait stall, zero comment-length trims needed, across all 5 areas — but 1 of 5 still needed a post-push repair**: run 56 dispatched cleanly from `dev-paul` directly (no sibling PRs to branch from), `dev-paul` stayed static for the entire run so no rebasing was needed before any of the 5 PRs, and every subagent delivered a complete, self-verified, root-cause fix with fail-before/pass-after evidence on its first completion notification — no `SendMessage` correction needed for any of them. All 5 new comment blocks either were already single-line or matched an established multi-line convention already present in the specific file touched (continuing the trend first noted 2026-08-27). But automated review still caught a real, both-the-subagent-and-orchestrator-missed correctness gap on #2660 within minutes of push (see that PR's Run Log row) — reinforcing the now-standing lesson that a subagent's own fail-before/pass-after test, and the orchestrator's re-run of that same test, only prove the SPECIFIC reported scenario is fixed, not every edge case the fix's own new logic can reach. A clean dispatch phase is not the same claim as a clean ship — keep watching PR activity after pushing, not just after the subagent's original commit. Small sample (one run) — don't read any of this as the failure modes being solved, just a clean night with one real review catch.
- **A well-specified, concrete backlog lead (not a vague "audit this area" note) reliably pays off**: Admin & Config's dispatch prompt named one specific, actionable lead — a broader semantic grep sweep for the `canonicalBuildingId()` bug class beyond the two literal strings a prior run's narrower grep had checked — and the subagent found a second real, live-reachable instance (`GraphicOrganizerConfigurationModal.tsx`, #2662) on the first attempt, plus surfaced that ~35 more files share the same gap (new Backlog row, recommending a shared helper rather than continuing file-by-file). Contrast with backlog leads phrased as "worth a look" or "re-confirm this is still clean," which mostly yield re-confirmations, not new fixes. When a lead has a concrete, greppable signature (a literal pattern, not just a vague area), name it explicitly in the dispatch prompt.
- **A Firestore rules `update` whitelist correctly anticipated the `setDoc`-on-existing-doc-evaluates-as-update lesson (#2625) proactively, without rediscovering it the hard way**: Build & Tooling's `activity_wall_sessions/submissions` `update` rule's `hasOnly()` whitelist included the Admin-SDK-only archive fields (`driveFileId`/`archiveError`/`archivedAt`/`archiveStartedAt`) specifically because a teacher's later client update evaluates `request.resource.data` as the doc AFTER merge, so previously-set fields the update didn't touch must still pass. This is the first confirmed case of a subagent applying that standing lesson from the dispatch prompt correctly on the first pass, rather than shipping a narrower whitelist and having review catch the gap (the pattern in #2625/#2653's own history). Worth keeping this lesson worded exactly as-is in future Build & Tooling dispatch prompts.
